**PART I**

**Chapter 1**

***8:00 AM 14th February 2010, Girbin, Scotland***

The streetlights in a faraway old Scottish town were making way for the early morning sunshine, as the sunbeam was passing through the morning haar. The dawn sun was hiding in the cold brume as if this was the game it had chosen to play. The birds roused from their repose, tweeting with verve, and the gentle breeze made the chirping fade into some melodious hum. No matter how serene it appeared, it had that eerie uneasiness that was giving signs of an unwelcome morning. This was confirmed by the people who preferred their shelters to the road.  Amidst nature’s caper, a Range Rover was slowly rolling down the road. It was black with dark tinted glass. The car came to a full stop a few feet away from the town square. The town square was the only attraction this little hamlet had. Besides the farms, where people gathered for merrymaking, everything that the town needed was around the town square – the shops, the church, the wooden benches. The car remained parked with utmost silence, blending with the surroundings and almost appearing like an old landmark.

The morning coldness couldn’t keep the small-town dwellers from their routine. The shopkeepers opened their stores, the ringer pulled the rope of the church bell, and the streets soon got crowded with the Sunday mass-goers. The town was slowly waking up. All this while, the Range Rover held to its spot as if it had some long-standing monumental significance.

For Daniel Roberti, it was the special day of the week when he spent time with his family. His family was everything to him, and he made sure every minute of the Sunday was special for them. Every Sunday began with him running to the local bakery to get his daughter’s favourite freshly made muffins. He would cook the specials, brewing black coffee with select beans crushed at home, and making scrambled eggs, peanut butter sandwiches, and freshly squeezed orange juice. He put the spread on the table before waking everyone. His daughter always woke along with him but pretended to be asleep, as she loved being roused by her father. Her father hardly ever disappointed her, and never missed any chance to treat her like a princess. This tradition had continued ever since she could remember, from her childhood days to this day, at sixteen. After breakfast, the family visited the local church. Weather permitting, they usually planned some outdoor activities as well. As a family, they cheered for each other, celebrated every little joy, and lived contented. He had a tough job as a deliveryman, often working long hours, and Sunday was his way to thank his family for being there for him.

This Sunday was no different. Besides the cold weather, everything else was aligned with his expectations. Bright sun, resting family, and the dewiness in the air, a perfect Sunday as he wanted it to be. Following his routine, he stepped out of the house and headed to the bakery. His daughter crawled out of bed and dashed to the window. The droplets of coldness on the windowsill had turned half ice, half water, battling the difference in temperature between the heated room and the coldness outside. She grabbed her sleeve, stretched the cloth further out and used it as a perfect tool for cleaning the haze. As the window gave way to the outside view, she saw her father sprinting out humming his favourite song. No matter how many times she had seen it, the sight never failed to excite her. She stood there, like always, waiting for her father to dissolve in the morning mist. Soon he disappeared, and she dashed back to her bed pretending to be asleep again. Both father and daughter played this little game, but they pretended to be unaware of each other’s ruses for reasons best known to them.

While the Roberti family was expecting this to be yet another Sunday of love and bonding, little did they know that this Sunday would change their lives forever. Change for the worse.

**Chapter 2**

***7:00 PM 14th April 2014, Helsingør, Denmark***

The streets were bustling with people, a little more than usual for this small, quiet Danish town. It was a cold spring evening, and the burg was engulfed in layers of mist. A sudden gust of cold air surged past, as a fast-approaching limousine swung around the cobbled pavement to reach the entrance of the hotel at the corner of the street. It was an elegant palace built in the 16th century, now remodelled as a grand luxury hotel. It had some historical significance and was the best the town offered to the elite class.

The concierge sprang into action. A valet reached for the door, and another one approached the driver to get the car keys. From behind the dark tinted glass of the car appeared a man. He was over six feet tall and sharply dressed in a cobalt blue Versace suit. His eyes were hidden behind the dark shades and his chiselled face refused to give any expression away. Two well-built men surrounded him, and they briskly walked towards the hotel lobby. The entourage turned left from the entrance towards the executive bank of elevators.  Soon they vanished behind the lift doors.

Diagonally opposite the elevators was a coffee shop where a petite girl was sipping her rich Danish coffee. She was about twenty and looked a little out of place in her gorgeous black evening gown. The gown left her back bare and fitted well across her slim waist, with the top slightly covered by her turquoise blue scarf.  Her long brown hair was tied in a knot, and her radiant face could make anyone go weak at the knees. From time to time she craned her neck towards the lobby, her eyes in constant search for someone’s arrival. She was anxious but wouldn’t show it, hiding her apprehensions behind the gentle sips of her evening coffee. Her wait was soon curtailed.  The man she was waiting for swiftly went past her to enter the elevator bank. The day she had waited for so long had finally arrived. Everything was planned except for one small glitch – she didn’t have a well-thought-out strategy to get introduced to him.

At this time every year, he visited this place to take part in the annual poker tournament. It was the only place where she could hope to initiate a conversation with him. Preoccupied with her quandaries, she headed towards the casino. She took a deep breath, shook her anxieties away, and approached the poker room. This side of the casino had a distinct air of richness. Large chandeliers were dangling in the foyer, and their dim golden light gave a spark to the exquisite 16th-century paintings on the walls. Two tall, sturdy sentinels guarded the entrance to the secluded area. With confidence in her stride, she walked towards the entrance. “Hej!” greeted one of the guards in the local Danish way. She glanced at him with smiling eyes and continued walking. One of the guards raised his hand to stop her and asked, “May I see your membership card, please?”

She still carried her infectious smile and stated she was new to the town and was visiting the casino for the first time. “I have the money,” were her concluding words. The guard politely refused and declared the place to be a members-only club. From the corner of her eye, she saw the man she was waiting for walking towards her, and she purposely raised her voice to get his attention. “I want to see the manager. Is this how you treat a lady?” Before the guards could react, she launched her next assault in an even louder voice, “Where is it written that only members are allowed?”

By now he was next to her. He was sharply garbed in a tux aptly fitting his torso. The guards gave way for him to enter the casino. As he was passing through the tall doors, he felt a gentle tap on his shoulder. He turned around and saw a girl greeting him with a radiant smile. She looked straight into his eyes and continued, “You seem to be popular. Can you put these miserable souls in their place? They are rude and discriminating.”

He looked at her with interest. Her eyes were inviting, and her innocent face revealed frustration and despair. There was something in her that was noble, which urged him to know more about her. And in his most casual way, he uttered, “She is with me,” and led her to the elite poker room.

The room was circular. There was one large mahogany table in the centre with seating for five players. The dealer occupied one side of the table facing the dark velvet wall. Three seats were right across from him, and one on each side of the table. The room was dimly lit, and the walls were humming Mozart’s “The Marriage of Figaro”. On the far-right side of the table was the bar with an elaborate collection of wine, single malts, and some of the most exotic liquors from around the world, arranged in an exquisitely designed temperature-controlled wooden cellar. The elite guests were arriving at leisure and taking their designated seats around the table.

“I deeply appreciate your kind gesture.” She took the initiative, extended her hand and continued, “My name is Rita.”

He gave her hand a gentle grip and reciprocated by introducing himself in a single word, “Benedikt.” Like any gentleman would, he continued with a distinct deep voice, “The seats are limited to members only. Please go ahead and take mine for tonight’s game.”

She had been waiting for a moment like this. In a split second she turned towards him, and with a pair of inviting eyes she stretched to reach his ear and whispered, “I appreciate the gesture. For tonight, let me be your lady luck.”

Benedikt’s curiosity had increased manifold. He was enamoured of her charm but wouldn’t let anything show. With slightly parted lips his voice trailed, “The pleasure is mine.” He waved his hand to the keeper and instructed him to arrange the seating for the lady.  The keeper got an additional armchair and placed it next to Benedikt.

The high-stakes poker game had some rules. Each of the five players was required to bring ten million pounds to the table, and no one could leave until one of them was declared the winner. Benedikt had a decent record. He had won five out of the last seven outings and was clearly the favourite. He was known for bold moves and for making his opponents shudder with his signature all-in stakes. During play, he never gave anything away and wore a poker face regardless of wins or losses. The E. N. Welch clock chimed, welcoming the evening’s ninth hour. It was the official time to commence the annual poker game.

The dealer showed two fresh card decks sealed in cellophane wrap, which he unwrapped in front of the captive audience. The first pack was shuffled, and Robinson, who was sitting to the left of the dealer, did the honour of cutting the deck. The players created the initial pot by each putting in a couple of white chips as the ante. Right after that, the dealer distributed five cards to each player.

The first round of blind betting was initiated. Robinson started with a bet of a hundred thousand, which was matched by the next two players. When Benedikt’s turn came, he raised the bet to two hundred thousand. Sitting next to Benedikt was Holger. The increased bet didn’t sit well with him, which was quite evident in his fidgety manner. However, he avoided calling it quits so early in the game, and though hesitant, he went ahead and matched the bet. The dealer waved over the pot, indicating that the players could look at their cards.  The girl, who had introduced herself as Rita, was observing the players with great interest, hoping to read their minds as they peered into their share of cards. None of them gave anything away. No smile, no widening of eyes or a trickle of sweat, not a shade of any slipping hint. Sitting in a live high-stakes poker game for the first time, she understood impassiveness was the unwritten protocol of the game. However, she kept alive her hope of finding some cue she could use to impress Benedikt.

The second round of betting started. Robinson put forward two hundred thousand to stay in the game. Sitting to the left of Robinson was Ingrid, who decided to fold after seeing her cards. Alberto was next, and he matched the stake. It was Benedikt’s turn; he pushed forward chips worth a million pounds, raising the stake by eight hundred thousand pounds! Like before, he was inscrutable, and it was hard to tell whether he had good cards or was simply bluffing. Holger was next. Like Ingrid, he decided to avoid the agony of losing and folded.

Rita noted Alberto’s stance. He sat with his legs crossed and was rotating a chip between his fingers. The waitress was making rounds with the drinks. It seemed she knew her audience well. She brought vodka with two olives on the side for Benedikt. The waitress looked at Rita with interest.

“What would you like to have?”

The waitress’s eyes carried an exquisite smile, and before Rita could respond, Benedikt looked at her and with a possessive voice queried, “May I?”

And without waiting for her affirmation he ordered, “Krupnik with vodka for the lady,” his gaze still piercing her.

“Coming right away,” declared the waitress.

She was certainly impressed, but she reminded herself that she was there on a mission, not to get carried away by some infatuated foolishness. “I am sure this must be the best, and I trust your choice.” Her eyes flirted as she glanced at him with a mysterious smile.

“It is a Lithuanian delight, and I am sure it will suit you well,” Benedikt stated, his gaze not falling. She knew that the executive floor wasn’t too far from her reach now.

With the pot past two million, it was now Robinson’s turn to fold. There were two players left, Alberto and Benedikt. Alberto raised the stake to one million two hundred thousand pounds. Rita was keenly observing Alberto as he continued to rotate the chip between his fingers. She was a little startled when the waitress broke her concentration. “Your drink, madam.”

She couldn’t resist anymore. She bent a little closer to Benedikt’s ear and whispered, “I think he is bluffing.” Benedikt hardly showed any reaction. And after a brief pause, he increased the stake by two million pounds. The game was getting intense, and Alberto did not disappoint the audience. He matched the stake, and it was time for the final showdown. Alberto opened his first two cards, the seven of spades and the ten of spades. The dealer put the cards in order, unfolding the suspense for the inquisitive audience. Now it was Benedikt’s turn to open his two cards. “Two aces,” announced the dealer as he laid them out facing the players. Benedikt’s remaining three cards, an eight of clubs, a nine of diamonds and a two of hearts, added nothing to his aces. Alberto opened the rest of his three cards, the six, eight and nine of spades, and took the game away with a straight flush. Alberto finally grinned, showing his yellow-tinted teeth, and collected the pot.

Rita was embarrassed by her puerile guessing. Though uncomfortable, she mustered some courage. “I am truly sorry for my infantile behaviour, and I am sorry for your loss.”

Benedikt, for the first time that evening, had a thin smile on his lips. He looked at her and gently said, “Don’t be sorry. It wasn’t your fault.” He continued with a frivolous tone, “And never cease giving your views. I like it when someone puts their mind to work, as I do.”

Rita was puzzled. She couldn’t figure out whether it was a compliment or a sarcastic remark. She reciprocated with an edgy smile, and all the confidence she had gained that evening seemed to fade away, leaving her in a nervous and dazed state.

The dealer announced a fifteen-minute break before resuming the next session. “I have to make an important call. I will be back in ten minutes,” Benedikt said, and left her in the room alone.

Seeing Benedikt fade away, Alberto made his advances. “Never seen Benedikt with a lady before. Guess he never needed one.” He was self-amused and chuckled.

With a smile on her face, she responded, “The game is still on, and who would know better than a pro like you that it takes one good hand to change the tide?” She stretched out her hand and declared, “Name is Rita.”

Alberto, with some awkwardness, took her hand, introduced himself and swiftly moved away.

As promised, Benedikt came back in less than ten minutes. The players exchanged some pleasantries and settled down for the next round. All players put forward an initial ante. The dealer shuffled a new pair of decks. This time Benedikt cut the deck, and the cards were dealt. The dealer announced the first blind call. Alberto started with a blind of five hundred thousand pounds, which everyone matched. He called for a second blind and raised the stake by one million pounds. One could see slight shades of hesitation on some of the players’ faces. Regardless, everyone obliged, and the pot stood at seven and a half million pounds. It was time for everyone to look at the cards. Alberto, after seeing his cards, raised the stake to two million.

“Looks like it’s Alberto’s day today.” That was how Rita felt, observing the proceedings. With dwindling hope, she kept watching Alberto. Unlike last time, he hadn’t crossed his legs, but he still took a chip and started rotating it between his fingers. Her mind was racing. It must be a cue, but she couldn’t figure it out. It was Benedikt’s turn, and he surprised everyone by raising the stake to two and a half million pounds. Holger, already shaking his head, called it quits. Robinson followed suit. Ingrid picked up her cards for the second time, and after pondering for a while, she raised the stake by three million pounds. Rita knew Ingrid had good cards. However, it hardly deterred Alberto from raising the stake by three and a half million. The game was becoming fierce, and it was almost certain that most of them would be out of the tournament soon.

Alberto shifted his posture, and Rita got the cue she was looking for. She observed two things that were different from before. First, he crossed his legs only after seeing his cards, unlike last time, when he had crossed them from the beginning. Also, this time he crossed his left leg over his right. She couldn’t resist and succumbed to her intuition. Besides, she was sure Benedikt wanted her to speak her mind.

“I am sure it is a half bluff. He has the cards, but not high in the order.” Benedikt refrained from uttering a word, but his action brought her both a sense of achievement and a surge of nervousness. He went with an all-in stake. She couldn’t believe her eyes. High-stakes money was rolling in. Ingrid didn’t dare to go any further and saved her remaining chips for the next game. The battle was again between Alberto and Benedikt. Alberto decided to go with an all-in, forcing Benedikt to show his cards. Everyone held their breath as the pile was over a whopping twenty million pounds.

Benedikt opened his first two cards, the five of hearts and the seven of clubs. Alberto smiled and put forward his two kings. Now it was Benedikt’s turn. All eyes were on him. He revealed his next three cards as slowly as he could. He had a six of clubs, an eight of diamonds and a nine of hearts. It was a straight! All attention shifted to Alberto. Could he pull the game? He appeared pale and his visage flinched. All he had was one more king, a queen and a four. Three kings were not enough to pull the game away from Benedikt.

With the other players significantly down and Alberto badly weakened, Benedikt was heading towards a clear win. Like always, he gave nothing away. The dealer called the game off for the night, to be reconvened the next day. Benedikt left his seat, passed a faint smile to his fellow contestants and dashed out of the room with his prized possession, Rita.

“It has been a long night. May I offer you a drink at my place?” There was a clear hint of curiosity in his demeanour as he put forth his suggestion.

Rita couldn’t believe her ears. It was like pieces of a puzzle coming together. Keeping her excitement under control, she responded with a distinct smile, “I would love to.”

As soon as they left the room, the two escorts magically appeared and guided them to the executive elevator bank. They took the elevator to the 15th floor. The high-speed elevator was moving with extreme velocity, and so were her emotions. After four years of meticulous planning and a painful, arduous wait, she was about to take the first step. Her emotions were getting the best of her. She was disconcerted and was trying hard to conceal it from the prying eyes of Benedikt.

**Chapter 3**

***11:00 PM 14th April 2014, Helsingør, Denmark***

The elevator opened to a grand hallway. The corridor was laced with cameras covering every inch of the hallway. There were men in dark suits guarding the passage. The aura of the place was overwhelming, making Rita visibly nervous. A trickle of sweat appeared on her temple and she felt the thumping of her heart against her chest. Benedikt gave her a cursory glance and observed her visage turning pale. He gently squeezed her hand and with a hushed voice gave his assurance, “Don’t get intimidated. They are for our safety.” She smiled sheepishly and appeared perturbed. The bewilderment worked to her advantage, as Benedikt hardly considered her a threat.

The hallway led to a large living room. The place was grand, with tall ceilings and pointed arches. The room was separated into two sections by raised stairs. The seating area was vast, with spread-out sofas, decorative sculptures, and paintings, all meticulously arranged. From the door, she could also see the bedroom, which extended from the living room to the furthest corner of the floor. Benedikt called one of his accomplices and whispered something barely audible to her. The black-suited man sprang into action, and soon all the guards vanished behind the closed doors.

There she was in Benedikt’s room, alone with him. For the past two years, she had visualised being here many times. All her waiting was finally taking a stride forward. Her brooding was broken by Benedikt’s grating voice. “Do you have a preference, or shall I get you one of my favourites?”

She smiled and responded with equal vigour, “Surprise me.”

He went to the bar and made the drink with amazing skill.  “Here is your between-the-sheets drink. Hope you like it.” The name itself had sensuousness written all over it. Gently he settled down with his drink. They were intensely looking at each other, and before she could comprehend, he slowly leaned forward and kissed her. The alluring kiss grew passionate, and soon their tongues were exploring each other’s mouths. There was hunger, passion, and desire. He lifted her off her feet and carried her to the bedroom. She was taken by surprise. She knew something like this would happen, but not at this pace. She had a plan and she couldn’t afford to ruin it in some silly lovemaking episode. With a lot of reluctance, she pushed him away, first gently, then with some force. His emotions were a mix of dejection and surprise, but like a gentleman, he let her go. His hair was ruffled, and his state dishevelled.

She said with a smirk on her face, “I am sorry. I didn’t mean to be rude, but I need to use the bathroom and, if possible, change.”

Benedikt’s despair soon turned into a harmless chuckle, and he led her to the bathroom. He winked as he pointed to the wardrobe and declared, “Here is my robe. The best I can offer.”

She locked the bathroom door and carefully took off her gown. The dress had her escape material stitched inside its long sleeves. She wanted to secure her belongings before heading back to the room. With extreme caution, she stashed her gown in the cabinet below the sink, slipped into the robe and came back to him in a jiffy. He was still working on his vodka and was looking away towards the window.  He was taken by surprise as she locked her arm around his neck. His strong arms swung her around and made her fall into his lap. The robe and the clothes disappeared, and they turned to making passionate love. She hadn’t experienced anything like this before. Her trepidations slowly melted away. They made love again and again like there was no tomorrow.

It was a little past 1:00 AM when she roused from her transient repose. Benedikt was sound asleep. The sedative lotion she had applied to him had worked. She moved discreetly, ensuring she made no noise, and tiptoed to the bathroom. Hidden in the gown were a body-hugging suit and a pair of gloves. She slipped into the suit, put on the gloves to avoid leaving behind her fingerprints and picked up the briefcase lying by the bedside. With the help of a hairpin, she tried to open it. It didn’t open. She tried again, but nothing happened. Her time was running out. She had to get out of the place before the break of dawn. She gave it one final push and, with a gentle click, the briefcase sprang open.

There it was, the small telescopic cylinder. She opened it and found a piece of paper carefully wrapped inside it. She took her phone out of the purse, ensured the silent mode was on and took multiple pictures of the paper. She gave a quick glance at the pictures. Satisfied, she carefully inserted the paper into the cylinder and placed it back in the briefcase. There was one more job left before she could escape the room. She searched the room for Benedikt’s wallet. After some combing, she found it in the bedside drawer. She took all the cash, hoping the missing cash would misguide any future investigation, gave one final look at Benedikt and dashed towards the bathroom.

Her gown was specially designed for her escape. Besides the suit, gloves and the sedative lotion, she also had a glasscutter as part of her accessories. She tore open the fringes of the gown and scooped out the tiny battery-operated cutter. She made sure all the doors were closed to muffle any noise and diligently started her job. In about fifteen minutes the glass was off the window. She had earlier checked into a room two floors below Benedikt’s. She had paid a premium for the corner suite as it had a downtown-facing view. She wrapped up the rest of her things and put them in the drawer underneath the faucets, and it was time to escape.

She crawled out of the cut-out window. It was 2:20 AM and pitch dark outside. Not a soul was visible. She put her foot on the frame block of the window. Earlier in the day she had put a rope with an anchor a floor above her room. The anchor was hidden behind the wall alongside the drainpipe, which ran from the roof to the ground. She had to climb down one floor to reach it. The hotel had a castle-like appearance with gothic structures, which worked to her advantage. Using the hinges of the building frame, she descended with careful steps. Once she was close to the drainpipe, she moved sideways, resting her foot with extreme caution on the pipe’s ring. With utmost care, she took the excruciating journey down, one step at a time.

“Creak, creak!” A loud cacophony broke the otherwise silent night. She lost her left foothold and slipped, only to get hold of the hinge of a bulging shell. Her heart leaped into her mouth. She had never expected in her wildest dreams to hear a creepy noise at such an ungodly hour. For a few seconds, she had no clue what had hit her. And then she heard the flutter of birds moving away. Unknowingly she had invaded their privacy. She was hanging on the hinge and was in desperate need of a foothold. The guest in the room right below her switched on the light and peered outside, and to her advantage, the flutter of the birds erased any apprehension he may have had and made him go back to sleep. As the light was seeping out of the window, she saw a frill edge connecting the drainpipe to the brick wall towards the right of her dangling foot. She knew that if she couldn’t get onto the edge, the hinge would soon give way. She swung her feet and managed to get a grip on the drainpipe. With caution, she shifted her body weight to the newfound shelter. Her journey was one floor down from there. She found the rope that she had tied earlier in the day and slipped down the cord to her room’s window. Once inside, she pulled the rope in and closed the window behind her.

“The Don’t Disturb sign worked like a charm,” she mumbled after finding her room untouched by the housekeepers. She was perspiring from head to toe. Though she would have loved to take a shower, she refrained. She was running out of time, as the sedative on Benedikt would soon start to fade away.

She changed into a pair of jeans and a hooded pullover. She picked up her bag, which she had packed the night before, and dashed out of the room. The lobby was almost without a soul.  To her benefit, the receptionist was stealing an early morning nap in the adjacent office room. She found a lone bellman in the lobby, offered him a hundred kroner and asked him to get her a cab. She didn’t have to wait long. Once inside the taxi, she instructed the driver to take her to the Sofitel hotel. From Sofitel, she took another taxi to the main market, and from there rode the bus to the railway station.

“When is the next train to Copenhagen?” she asked the man behind the glass wall, who was half asleep. He looked at his watch and with a groggy voice announced, “In twenty minutes from platform 2.”

She took a business class ticket and boarded the train. The train took about forty minutes to reach Copenhagen. At Copenhagen, she bought a ticket to Basel. The train to Basel was leaving in an hour, and she had enough time to make a phone call.

She approached the public telephone booth at the station platform and dialed a number. After a few rings, a man with a gruff voice snapped, “Who is it?” She introduced herself and continued in a low tone, “I have the map. Can you call Adriana and confirm my arrival next week? I am calling from a pay booth. I will call you back in fifteen minutes.”

After fifteen minutes, she called back. The man responded, “She will be waiting for you. Good luck.” She thanked him and heaved a sigh of relief. After an hour-long wait, the train to Basel approached the platform. It was the first train out in the morning. She took a corner seat and, with the bag on her lap and her arms around it, drifted off to sleep.

**Chapter 4**

***12:00 AM 15th April 2014, Helsingør, Denmark***

A man dressed in a black tracksuit and a windcheater was bravely fighting the biting cold in the open backyard of the hotel. His dark getup blended well with the night’s darkness, and he was almost invisible unless someone came within a few yards of him. The temperature had dropped to single digits, and he had no respite from the gelid air but his clothes. Though he was dressed for the season, he couldn’t withstand the frigid brutality without the occasional shivers.

He was waiting for a girl to appear from the executive floor’s badested ridge. He knew the layout of the floor well and was anticipating her arrival any moment from the windowsill. While he was certain of her breakout route, what he was unsure of was when it would happen. There was also a slim chance of her being caught. In such a situation, he would have to execute his alternate plan. But for now, it was a long, agonising wait.

A soft tinkling sound brought his senses to alert. It was a weird thrum, hard to notice unless someone was expecting it. He wasn’t sure of its origin but was almost certain who had caused it.

Then he saw her shadow dangling by the hinge. He was puzzled, not sure what he should do. Before he could conclude anything meaningful, he saw her find the much-needed support. Soon she was on her way into her room through the open shaft of the French window. He was relieved to see her escape the mishap unscathed. The moment she disappeared behind the window, he scurried to the lobby area. He stood outside, a little further away from the entrance. Taking the darkness as a shield, he waited for her to appear. His wait wasn’t long. He saw her sprinting from the front lobby to the concierge area. He witnessed her handing the bellman some kroner, requesting him to get a taxi. Soon the taxi arrived, and she slipped into the ghostly night with no trace left behind. The man jumped into the taxi he had kept for the night and instructed the driver to follow.

He traced her meandering route to the train station and followed her all the way to Copenhagen. He trailed her like a shadow and boarded the same train she took to Basel. He settled himself in the booth next to the door, and for the first time that night, he closed his eyes to get some much-needed rest.

**Chapter 5**

***4:00 AM 16th April 2014, Zurich, Switzerland***

The train came to a sudden stop, jolting away her transient sleep. She woke from her repose, struggling to break away from the ordeal she was going through. No matter how much she wanted to avoid the painful nightmares, they kept reappearing with renewed vengeance. The train was moving slowly through the early morning darkness. The lone bulb in the coach was fighting hard to cast dim yellow shadows in the ghostly darkness. She rubbed her eyes to get a clearer vision and tried hard to read the dial of her wristwatch. It was twenty minutes past four in the morning. Her destination was just a few minutes away. The train rocked, blew a long whistle and came to a full stop with a shrill cranking noise. She cursed herself for sleeping so long. Had it not been for the train’s obnoxious thrust, she would have missed the station.

Grabbing her bag, she slipped out of the train and headed towards the north exit. She pulled her cellphone out of her jacket and called a number. “I will be out in five minutes,” she whispered, and ended the call. As she walked along the platform, she looked around for any suspicious character but found none. There were a few people walking towards the exit, but they all appeared to be regular commuters, nothing for her to worry about, and she continued her walk.

Despite her alertness, she failed to notice the shadow that had followed her from Denmark. He blended well with the commuters at large and followed her with extreme caution.

There was a BMW 5 Series sedan waiting for her at the exit with its engine running, ready to leap. Without wasting any time, she disappeared behind the front passenger door. The shadow called for a cab and discreetly followed the BMW.

She smiled and looked at the person behind the wheel and announced, “Mission successfully accomplished.”

The driver did not appear that pleased. With a concerned look, he said, “I have a hunch we are being followed.”

She looked back and noticed a grey Peugeot SUV steadily following them at a well-maintained distance. Though it appeared harmless, there was something about the car that made her uneasy. Her mind raced hard to make sense of the situation, but her brain froze, and she failed to comprehend anything meaningful.

**Chapter 6**

***5:00 AM 15th April 2014, Helsingør, Denmark***

Benedikt woke from his dishevelled state with a dreadful headache. The room was draped in darkness, and it took him a while to adjust to the gloomy surroundings. He stretched out his hands, looking for Rita, but instead encountered crumpled bedsheets. His nerves were pulsating with such vigour that he thought his head would explode. Searching for the bedside lamp switch, he moved sideways and, after some fumbling, managed to turn the light on. The room was filled with brightness, making him shield his eyes from the glare. With reluctant steps, he headed to the bathroom. The splash of cold water pierced like shrapnel but provided him the much-needed relief from his irritable self. He opened the cabinet to get the aspirin bottle, took two pills and gulped them down with the faucet water. With the aspirin in his blood, he felt a lot better.  As he turned towards the towel rack, he saw the cut-out window pane neatly placed by the Jacuzzi. There were way too many things for him to comprehend. The thought of the briefcase suddenly crossed his mind. He dashed to the bedroom and, to his relief, found the briefcase exactly where he had left it the night before. He entered the number combination and the briefcase opened. Everything appeared untouched. He opened the cylindrical case and pulled the paper out. The paper appeared original, without any trace of tampering.  Without wasting any further time, he called his head of security.

The security head appeared perplexed and stood at attention, waiting for Benedikt’s instructions.

“Have you seen the girl?”

The man looked bewildered. “Not that I am aware of. Let me check.” He pulled out his transmitter and started talking to someone.

Benedikt was furious. How could a girl make such a mockery of him? Despite the security, the restricted access, and all the measures, she wandered into his bedroom, accomplished what she intended to, and vanished without a trace. His migraine was creeping back, adding fuel to his anger.

“How could she disappear with dozens of you around here? What do I pay you all for? I want her whereabouts in the next thirty minutes. Damian, I want you to trace her and bring her to me. If you fail, don’t bother to come back. Am I clear?”

Damian was sweating profusely. He nodded and dashed out of the room.

Benedikt was puzzled by the girl’s motive. He looked around the room, checked the briefcase, the living room and the closet. Nothing was missing. Besides the wallet, he had checked everything else he could think of. He pulled it out of the drawer and found all the cash missing. The amount was meager, about 500 kroner and a few hundred US dollars. He was baffled and flustered. “Which sane girl would risk her life for some insignificant sum of money, jump out of the 15th floor, and take on the challenge of invading a decent security web?”

It made no sense to him unless she took pleasure in stealing from affluent people. The more his mind tried reasoning, the more frustrated he became. The only respite was the aspirin. Rattled by the chaos, he tried calming his mind but failed. He called Damian and instructed him to get a doctor. He wanted to find out whether he had been drugged. Soon Damian came along with the doctor. The doctor took a blood sample for the test, checked his vitals, and advised him to repeat the aspirin every four hours.  After the doctor left, Benedikt asked Damian what he had found so far. The only person who had seen the girl since she disappeared was the bellman. According to him, the girl had left around 2:30 AM in a taxi. He told Benedikt his men were on the job to find the driver.

Benedikt was a tad calmer than before. With a gravelly voice, he said, “My money is missing from the wallet. I have a hunch she didn’t come for the money. She had a bigger motive. This girl is smart. Let us not underestimate her intent.”

He sat in the reclining chair with his fingers moving over his forehead, making a futile attempt to get some temporary relief from the nagging pain, and continued his monologue, “This is a disguise, a trap to make us believe she was here for the money, but I am not fool enough to be taken in by her deception. We need to be pragmatic in our approach if we are serious about nabbing her. I am still not sure of her motive, and the only way we can find out anything about her plan is by finding her. We need to be quick. The longer we take, the harder it will be to get our hands on her.”

He took a few sips of water and continued, “Here is what you need to do. First, find out everything about her. When she checked in, when she was last here, anyone she met during her stay, the room she stayed in. I want every minute detail that concerns her. Is that clear?”

“Yes,” was all Damian managed to say.

Benedikt continued, “Next, I want to talk to the taxi driver. I don’t care where he is. Go and find him. I also want all the hallway, lobby and poker room camera recordings. Get a picture from the recording and do a scan to find any information about her. Report back in twenty minutes. Will you?”

“Lars is on the job. I have tasked him to collect the tapes. I will ask him to run a scan on the girl as well,” Damian said and rushed out of the room to work on the remaining tasks.

**Chapter 7**

***6:00 AM 15th April 2014, Helsingør, Denmark***

Damian called his second in command, Russ, and tasked him to get Rita’s registration details from the hotel manager. He also instructed him to ask Lars to get all camera recordings for the last 72 hours. After putting his team on the job, he rushed to the concierge area to trace the cab driver.

The hotel security team was active and vigilant, covering every exit. Benedikt was an important customer and an influential person. They knew he possessed enough power to ruin the hotel’s reputation. And if he decided to do so, it would take a long time to mend the damage.  Seeing the head of Benedikt’s security, the manager on duty dashed towards him and assured him of all cooperation. He wasn’t sure what the girl had done to upset Benedikt, and despite his curiosity, he couldn’t muster enough courage to ask. Damian instructed the hotel manager to get the details of the taxi that took Rita out of the hotel. It was an easy task, as at that time of the morning there would be hardly any other guest looking for a ride. Besides, as part of the guest security protocol, the details of all taxis transporting guests were noted by the bellman.

The name of the cab driver was Cruz. Damian scanned the record book and, without looking away from the register, asked, “Where can I find him?”

“He usually drives at night and must be sleeping now. I tried his number, but he isn’t answering.”

“Do you know where he lives?” Damian’s gravelly tone showed clear frustration.

“I don’t, but the other drivers may know. Let me ask them.” He left and soon returned with a person who knew where Cruz lived. Without wasting any time, Damian commanded one of his men to get him.

In twenty minutes, Damian was back in Benedikt’s living room. Benedikt was on the phone, pacing across the room. He gestured to Damian to set up the camera recordings and continued with the call. Damian pulled out the CD, put it in the player, changed the television to AV mode, and waited for Benedikt to finish his call.

“So, what is the status?”

Damian almost jumped out of his skin. He hadn’t expected Benedikt to finish his call that soon. He recovered his composure and continued.

“Rita checked into the hotel three days back. The address she left in the records was not real, as we couldn’t find any street in Copenhagen by the name she provided. She was always seen alone, no friends, no meetings with anyone. What baffled me the most was her request for a room two floors below this executive floor. According to the manager on duty, the request was made on the pretext of having the downtown view. Also, she came to this place twice before in the past few months, and she always stayed in the same room.”

Benedikt was listening to every minute detail with great interest. The slight benefit of the doubt he had given Rita had now vanished. He was certain it was a well-thought-out intrusion, planned with extreme care and executed with brilliance. What he was unable to fathom was her motive. Except for the money, not a single object from his suite was out of place. The fingerprint report was also negative. While Benedikt was still deep in his brooding, Damian continued, “We should also have the cab driver here any moment. Would you like to see the recordings in the meantime?”

Benedikt agreed, and Damian played the video. It was over three days of recording covering the hallway, lobby, and the poker room area. Benedikt was keen to see the poker room recording first, around the time when he had stepped out for a call. He fast-forwarded the video to the poker room portion. There was nothing out of the ordinary. He saw how Alberto approached her and the way she rebuffed him. He carried a wry smile and admired the lady for her witty response. Though he was irritated with the chaos around him, he couldn’t refrain from praising her for the courage she had shown. He commanded Damian to change the video to the lobby area. Before Damian could change the CD, a gentle tap on the door made him leap to open it. He saw Lars standing with Cruz, the cab driver.

The cab driver was in his pyjamas and looked like he had been dragged out of his sleep. He appeared scared, and his eyes were searching for answers unknown.

Benedikt launched his questions, “Did you give a ride to a lady around 2:30 AM this morning?”

“Yes,” came the response. His voice had a shade of anxiousness.

“Where did you drop her?” Benedikt continued with his barrage of questions.

“I dropped her at the Sofitel hotel on Kruns Street.”

“Was she with someone? Did anyone join her on the way?”

By now Cruz was convinced that his life wasn’t in danger anymore. “No one. All along the ride, she was quiet. I tried striking up a conversation, but her response was cold, so I left her alone.”

Knowing there was nothing more he could get from the driver, he let him go. Damian informed him that they had found nothing in the Sofitel hotel register, and no guest had stepped out of the hotel around that time.

The smirk on Benedikt’s face confused Damian. “She is a genius, several shades higher in intelligence than all of you put together. Check all nearby hotel lobbies. At that hour, hotel lobbies are the only places to get cabs. Sofitel wasn’t her destination. I am sure she took another cab from there. Find that cab and find her next destination.”

Damian had never taken such an embarrassing jolt before. Without further delay, he disappeared behind the closing doors.

Benedikt continued with the lobby-recording playback. He was fast-forwarding, rewinding at times and zooming in, but he noticed nothing of significance. As he was sweeping through the tapes, he saw himself moving to the executive bank of elevators, and then he saw her. Her picture was grainy, as the video captured her on the extreme end of the frame. He paused the video and zoomed in. Though the image was blurred, he could see Rita in a black gown, appearing out of place in that small coffee shop.

There was a knock at the door.  With a raised voice he enquired, “Who is it?”

“This is Russ from the security team.”

He was let in. Russ informed him the doctor’s report had come, and he had indeed been drugged. “Looks like the substance was applied to your lips.”

Benedikt wasn’t surprised, as a piercing headache and the bad taste in his mouth were evidence enough of her doings. He gave the report a cursory glance and continued watching the tape.

This time he played the video in slow motion to observe her reaction when he went past her. He saw her attention going towards the elevators. He could tell she had been waiting for him all this time while pretending to have her coffee. He played the video forward and saw her approaching the elevators to the first floor, where the poker tournament was held. The more he was learning about her, the more his intuitions were taking a definitive shape. It was evident she was there for a purpose. She was there for him.

He couldn’t believe how he had fallen into her trap, in such a foolish and naïve manner. It was unsettling, and his mind was crowded with conflicting thoughts. The girl who had put his entire morning in disarray was not frustrating him for her misdeed, but for her disappearance. Deep inside, he knew he wanted to find her, more for himself than for anything else.

Benedikt glanced through the rest of the tape with dwindling hope. Besides confirming that she was in the hotel for him, he couldn’t find anything meaningful. He was about thirty minutes into it when his phone rang. He saw it was Damian calling and picked up the phone in a jiffy. “So, what did you find?”

“You were right.  She went across the street to the Carson Hotel and got a taxi from there. The concierge identified her from the picture I showed him. She had to wait for a good fifteen minutes before she got her next ride. We also located the other taxi driver. According to him, he dropped her on Market Street. Like on the previous ride, she was alone, didn’t speak and kept to herself. Market Street is a business area, and no businesses were open at that time of the morning. I am certain Market Street was again a transient stop for her. Right where she got down, there was a bus stop. I am quite certain she took the bus to the train station.”

Benedikt’s lips carried a vicious smile, as if he was enjoying the cat-and-mouse game. It was like a puzzle with pieces scattered all over the town, and he was tasked to put them together to solve the mystery called Rita.

“Head to the station and find out where she went. At that time of the morning, I am sure there were very few travellers,” Benedikt commanded.

“I have sent Lars. I am expecting a call from him any moment.” This time his voice had a spark, a fervour that was hard to conceal.

“I am impressed. Now find that girl for me.” Benedikt couldn’t resist a cynical smirk as he concluded the conversation.

Damian, for the first time since morning, appeared relieved. He knew it would be a hard day, but he was enthused with renewed hope.

Benedikt knew sooner or later he had to inform the higher authorities. He wanted to ensure he knew enough before calling them. It was never going to be an easy conversation explaining how a girl had landed in his room, vanished in an unceremonious manner, and worse, that he had no clue of her motives. No one would buy the money-stealing story either. He was never a philanderer, and once when he tried, he had put himself in trouble. And now, after the entire hullabaloo, he wasn’t sure if he wanted to catch her for her misdemeanours or for a purpose he was avoiding finding answers for.

In about thirty minutes, Damian was back. His glowing face suggested that he had got hold of something important. Before Benedikt could put forward any question, he blurted, “Her name isn’t Rita. At least her photo ID says Sanchez. She boarded the bus to the train station and from there took a train to Copenhagen. There was another person with the name Trevor who took the same train. Not sure if they are related by any means. From Copenhagen, she took another train to Basel. Trevor also took the same train. Couldn’t find any more information on Trevor. He didn’t stay in this hotel or in any of the nearby hotels.”

Benedikt had found three crucial pieces of information, the real name of the girl, the place where she had gone, and another person with the name Trevor. Basel wasn’t a big city. If she stayed there for a day, he could find her. Time wasn’t his best friend, and he knew he had to move quickly. He instructed Damian to put the Basel circuit on the job.

Keeping the news away from his higher-ups was never a good idea. Now that he had some details of her whereabouts, he decided to call his seniors. With some hesitation, he dialed a number and was in the conversation for a good fifteen minutes. At the end of the talk, though he appeared worn out, a sense of relief consumed his countenance. Despite all the pain and the nightmarish ordeal, he was in complete admiration of the girl. It was evident he was getting drawn to her, which was not good for him by any measure, but for now, he carried the discomfort. The eagerness to meet her had increased many times over. All he wanted now was a good sight of her.

While he assumed she was in Basel, little did he know about the detour she had taken at Zurich.

**Chapter 8**

***4:30 AM 16th April 2014, Zurich, Switzerland***

While Sanchez was trying hard to comprehend where she had gone wrong, her companion drove the car along random paths to lose the Peugeot. Part of his plan was to confirm whether the Peugeot was really behind them or whether it was just a false alarm. After a few arbitrary turns, it was evident that the Peugeot was indeed following them. It never disappeared, no matter which way they went.

The roads were a little busier than usual. This was the fortnight before the Sechseläuten festival, Zurich’s traditional spring holiday. Though the Peugeot was dissolving at times on the busy streets of Zurich, it kept resurfacing, confirming their apprehensions of being trailed. He kept a close eye on the rear-view mirror as he maneuvered his car with great caution, thinking of ways to bolt away.

Sanchez was warily observing everything that was happening around her. Their plans for the day were in complete disarray. Belted in the car, all she could do was sit through the commotion. She decided not to intervene and left it to Stephen to rescue them from the situation. Though she was silent, her mind was muddled with several competing thoughts. Could this be one of Benedikt’s horde? Or was it someone that she wasn’t aware of? If this was Benedikt’s doing, it was beyond her comprehension, for he was too powerful to chase them in such a discreet manner. His men could stop them anytime and do what they needed to. She knew even the local politicians, the police, and the government officials would all support Benedikt without question.

She replayed in her mind everything that had happened during her final dash from the hotel. She distinctly remembered that no one was in sight, neither in the lobby nor at the concierge desk, the only exceptions being the bellman and the cab driver. On her way to the station, she had checked multiple times to see if anyone was following her and couldn’t recall seeing any suspicious character. No one except Stephen knew about the plan. But despite the prudent planning, there were people chasing them who were aware of their whereabouts. She was clueless. She was jolted out of her brooding when Stephen applied the brakes with urgency. She looked at him with inquisitive eyes, questioning the sudden stop. And then the car made a sharp turn into an apartment parking lot.  She realised it was an attempt to evade the followers.

Sure enough, he succeeded, as he saw through the rear window the Peugeot passing by the complex without stopping. The parking lot was quiet; given the early hour, people were still in repose. Stephen wanted to be certain about his conjecture. He waited for a good twenty minutes before stepping out of the car. He told Sanchez to stay in the car and went to check the streets for the rogues.

The cold wind of the early spring welcomed him, forcing him to button his coat and push his hands into the warmth of his pockets. He tried his best to operate in stealth mode. He kept to the side of the parking lot and walked swiftly towards the street. Once at the edge of the apartment entrance, he put his back to the building wall, peeped out and, without exposing his torso, glanced at both sides of the street. Besides the moving traffic and the occasional pedestrian passing by, nothing caught his attention. He looked for the Peugeot and couldn’t trace it either. Realising his ruse had worked, he was relieved.

Once he was sure no one was watching him, he stepped out onto the street and walked to his left. He stopped at the intersection to check for anything out of the ordinary. Convinced there was nothing unusual, he turned around and approached the other end of the street. He couldn’t find anything concerning there either. Satisfied with his perusal, he headed back to his car.

Sanchez’s perturbations were testing her patience. From time to time she craned her neck sideways to check on Stephen. After about ten minutes of angst, she saw him pacing towards the car. Stephen opened the door and settled down with urgency.

“Looks like our little trick worked. Seems the Peugeot went past us.” He rubbed his hands to get some warmth before starting the onward journey.

“Good riddance.” She dropped her shoulders as if she had heaved away a long-carried burden, passed a frail smile to Stephen, and closed her eyes.

“We need to leave Zurich soon. Let us get back to our apartment, rest and leave first thing tomorrow morning. Sooner or later we have to reach Portugal, and nothing like having a few extra days to our advantage.” His voice was edgy and his mind still clouded with thoughts of the Peugeot.

Sanchez wanted to get a good rest. After the last four long, restless nights, to say she was tired was an understatement. She knew Stephen was anticipating a detailed report of her expedition, as they hadn’t spoken for the last four days. They had set a protocol not to call while she was on her mission, no matter how dire the situation might be. Now was the time for them to catch up on everything. But she was sleep-deprived, and she had no vigour left to put her brother’s perturbation to rest.

Stephen could read her mind and told her to get some sleep while he drove them home. Before Stephen could finish his statement, Sanchez was already in deep slumber. He looked at her and saw a sense of tranquility covering her face. He smiled and got back to business. He pulled his car out of the parking lot with extreme care, looked for any warnings with vigilant eyes, and put the vehicle in motion. He knew once he left the city it would take a good couple of hours to reach the apartment, and he wanted to finish it in the shortest possible time without attracting any more trouble.

As Stephen drove through the busy streets of Zurich, his mind went over everything that had happened since Sanchez’s arrival. He knew what Sanchez had accomplished was the first small step towards their goal. He was also aware that no matter how strenuous the journey might seem, they were ready to face it without worrying about the consequences. It wasn’t easy for him to let her go into the devil’s world, but they had no choice. While he was glad to see her back from the dangerous liaison she had undertaken, he was aware of the peril that loomed ahead.

The unexplained chase by the Peugeot was still hovering in his mind. There were many unanswered questions, but for now, he was glad to put the immediate danger behind him. With the car-chasing nuisance out of sight, his mind turned to the mountainous task that stood in front of them. He knew their path was rough, with many uncertainties, but that didn’t deter his enthusiasm. The glitter in his eyes was shining bright, even though his mind was crowded with unsolved quandaries.

**Chapter 9**

***11:00 PM 16th April 2014, Helsingør, Denmark***

Benedikt was flabbergasted. A few hours back he thought he had cornered her, but now that belief was rapidly diminishing. Despite his stronghold in the region, his team had no clue about her whereabouts. The detailed reports had come from his Basel team and they were disappointing, to say the least. There was no scintilla of her existence. His infamous poise was dishevelled, and his hair tousled. No matter how much he tried, he couldn’t keep his agitation curbed. His team had checked the closed-circuit cameras of the train station, interviewed taxi drivers and gone through town and council reports, but Sanchez was, simply put, missing. When he first started the search, his assumption was the hunt wouldn’t exceed a few hours. It was two days now, and the progress made was a mere collection of a few scattered clues. He knew the next few hours would be decisive, or his worst fear of a long-haul chase would come true. The threat of intervention from his higher-ups was looming over his head as well. They were equally concerned and demanded that he control the situation with urgency. With every passing moment, his fear of becoming a sheer mockery amongst his peers was taking a clearer shape.

He pulled out the report on her that his team had put together, glanced through all the checklist items and found nothing missing. The only thing that had worked for him so far was his connection with the local passport office. He was now aware that before coming to Denmark she had spent considerable time in Ayr, a port town in Scotland. His team carried out a complete search of her apartment in Ayr. Besides the fact that she had been living there for the past two years, nothing concrete was found. The apartment owner rarely saw her. She paid the rent on time and kept the apartment clean, with no note of any untoward incidents. She lived an isolated life. No neighbours knew her, and she had no friend or acquaintance for them to question.

Benedikt’s mind was spinning. The way Sanchez’s character had evolved was a shade higher than a professional’s. The discreet life she lived, the meticulously designed plan to get to him, the immaculate execution of the same and her vanishing from the scene were all disturbing signs. He was outclassed. If his accomplices could exhibit even a dash of what she had demonstrated, he would be at a different level within his clan. It was absurd, but there was also a sense of bereavement that bothered him, as if he had lost a loved one. He was scared to admit it, but deep in his heart, he knew he was fatally attracted to her. He still remembered the passionate indulgence they had shared, and he couldn’t forget her eyes. It was as if they were narrating a story untold.

He shook his head, attempted to clear the untimely reverie and mumbled, “It has to be that map.”

It was a cryptic map depicting the pathways of a dangerous dealing and was classified information. At first, he was indifferent, as he assumed that even if she had access to the map, she could hardly do anything with it, but now that belief was slowly giving way to deep trepidation. He was aware that it would be a mammoth undertaking for anyone to defeat the plan. Too many affluent people were involved and too much was at stake. “No one can corrupt it. No one can do anything about it,” he said to himself, and no matter how firm he wanted to sound, his voice was frail and unsure.

This time he diverted his attention to Trevor. He had a vague notion that both Sanchez and Trevor had something to do with him. Trevor was another mystery. Unlike in Sanchez’s case, his team could find nothing about Trevor. As disturbing as it sounded, it led him to the conclusion that Trevor wasn’t his real name, and he was successful in hiding his identity. What was more puzzling to him was why, if Trevor and Sanchez were together, only Sanchez’s identity had been exposed. It was possible they weren’t together, or that her identity had been intentionally exposed to misguide him.  And then he thought about the other possibility, the probability of Trevor discreetly following Sanchez. However, he couldn’t find any reason strong enough to support the hypothesis.

As his mind was going through several such quandaries, one thing he was certain about was her presence on that train to Basel. He knew she had bought tickets for Basel but chose not to get off the train. As the facts unfolded, he was convinced that he was up against a seasoned professional. She was intercepting his moves, which was dangerous by any measure, and it required him to treat her like an expert, not like the naïve girl he had considered her before. His mind had become a mud puddle, and the more he stirred, the muddier it became. Deep in his heart, he knew that to get to her he had to think like her.

He went back to the drawing board and replayed the events that had unfolded so far. He tried distilling facts from assumptions. He was certain that she must have disembarked at one of the ten stations between Copenhagen and Zurich. He pulled out a sheet, plotted all ten stations and started reasoning about the place where she might have chosen to disembark. He bit the end of the pencil and started mumbling, almost lost in his contemplations.

“She will not take a chance to get off the train too quickly, as that would put her in imminent danger of getting spotted.”

He put an X on the first four stations as they were within four hours of Copenhagen. He also crossed out Basel. That left him with three places, Hamburg, Freiburg, and Zurich.

He pulled out his phone, tapped Damian’s speed dial number and instructed him to come to his room. Damian appeared in a flash and stood in an attentive position, waiting for instructions.

Benedikt was pacing the living room with his hands crossed behind his back and eyes on the floor. He was aware of Damian’s presence but didn’t bother to pay any attention. With no verbal exchange, the place turned ghostly and eerie, making Damian visibly uncomfortable. It was a long two minutes before Benedikt spoke, giving the perspiring Damian some respite.

“It is clear from the findings so far that she isn’t in Basel. I strongly believe...” He paused and continued, “in fact, I am positive that she got down somewhere in between. I have given it a lot of consideration. The train stops at ten places between Copenhagen and Basel. We don’t have enough resources to deploy at all ten places. I had to pick a few, and I have picked these three.”

He passed the paper he had scribbled on to Damian. Damian looked at it with great interest. He saw the names of the stations and a lot of pencil marks across them. Before he could draw any conclusions, Benedikt continued in his monotonous tone, “Hamburg, Freiburg and Zurich, she is in one of these three places. We need to find her in the next four hours. Once the morning dawns, I have a hunch she will change her location again. Put all your resources behind it, choose the best three men to lead each place, and I need the teams to report back every thirty minutes. As soon as you learn anything about her, inform me. I would like to be in that place personally to oversee matters. Also, for the remaining stations, get all the closed-circuit camera recordings. In case the odds are against us, we should know quickly. Any questions?”

“No, I am good for now.” Damian swiftly left the room.

After Damian departed, Benedikt had one final job left for the night. He grabbed his phone and called someone. The person picked up the phone on the first ring. Benedikt said in a low tone, “Listen, I am calling you because I trust you. I have a job for you. But you need to make sure you operate in an absolutely discreet manner. I don’t want anyone to know.”

The person confirmed with a firm yes.

Benedikt continued, “I am going to send you details of a girl named Sanchez and a man called Trevor, although I doubt the latter is his real name. You need to find out whether they have any association with TransPacific in any form or shape. Once you find anything, call me on my personal number.”

The person agreed. Benedikt tried getting some rest. He lay on the sofa, but his mind was restless, struggling to keep pace with several competing deliberations.

**Chapter 10**

***3:40 AM 17th April 2014, Zurich, Switzerland***

The alarm clock ruffled the quietness of the otherwise serene morning. The night had still not given way to the dawn, as the grey clouds held the darkness with all their might.  Stephen’s hands searched for the clock and after a few failed attempts found the snooze button, which reclaimed the calmness instantly. On a regular day, Stephen would have extended his sleep to the last possible minute, but not today. He had wanted to bail out of Zurich the night before but decided otherwise, as he wanted Sanchez to recuperate from the last four days’ ordeal before they embarked on their next mission. While she slept like a log, he struggled to get any rest. His mind was too clouded with doubts and apprehensions. He tried hard, but as it always happens, the things you run away from haunt you more fiercely. The previous morning’s events kept revisiting him until the wee hours. He was mentally exhausted, and when he finally managed to sleep, he was unceremoniously stirred by the dreaded alarm.

After an initial struggle, he dragged himself out of bed. He decided to use the bathroom first, giving Sanchez a little extra time to rest. In twenty minutes, he was all done. Wrapped in a towel, he sauntered towards the only bedroom the apartment had. Sanchez was still sleeping. The wall clock showed 4:00 AM, and it was time for Sanchez to wake up. He gave her shoulders a gentle nudge and whispered, “Wake up, San! We need to catch the 7:00 AM flight.”

Sanchez looked at him with blurry eyes, agape and lost.  She quickly regained her composure, stretched her arms, and pushed her back against the wall, adjusting a pillow behind her, and said with a gravelly voice, “Good morning.”

“Good morning. Sorry for being hard on you. We need to hurry. I want to be on the road in thirty minutes. Why don’t you get dressed? In the meantime, I will fix breakfast.”

Sanchez responded to his request with some reluctance and traipsed off to find the bathroom. Meanwhile, Stephen got to his business hurriedly. It wasn’t long before Sanchez was back, all ready to conquer the world. She was dressed in petite dark blue jeans and a pullover with a hood, and her waist-long natural brown hair was left without any confinement, giving her an alluring appearance. She was giving herself a good look in the mirror when Stephen announced that breakfast was ready. There was coffee, scrambled eggs and some toast. They sat at the kitchen table and devoured it all.

By 5:00 AM they were ready to hit the road. They had two carry-ons with them. Stephen wanted to keep the luggage light to acquire more agility. They put their bags in the car, locked the apartment door and headed north to St. Gallen-Altenrhein Airport.

Stephen knew Zurich was the most popular airport in the region and could draw undue attention from his pursuers, so he chose to go to St. Gallen Airport instead. It would take them about an hour to the airport, and he was well within time to catch the 7:00 AM flight to Lisbon.

Despite the early morning ordeal, Sanchez looked well rested. The drive to the airport was uneventful.  Soon they were in the boarding area ready to leave Zurich.

The flight departed on time and it was sparsely filled. There was nothing alarming so far and slowly they were convinced of their success in dodging the miscreants from the previous morning. It was a long flight, enough time for them to rest and rethink their plan.

**PART II**

**Chapter 11**

***15th March 2012, Ayr, Scotland***

Tracing John Patterson in a large city like Ayr was like trying to find a needle in a haystack. Sanchez had been in the city for over four months now, still clueless about where to start or whom to approach. Besides the name and a vague assumption that he was in Ayr, she had nothing concrete.

Four months back, her life had been confined to a small town where she knew everything that surrounded her. The known faces, the choir group, she was familiar with all of them. But now she was in a city, much bigger than her past abode, exposed and vulnerable. Besides Aunt Nina she knew no one in Ayr. The college, the city, and her home, everything appeared new, everything was poignantly altered. She came to Ayr with boatloads of hope, but with the passing time, the reality showed its ugly side, morphing her into a disgruntled person. She tried every possible way to escape the misery, but every attempt ended up defeating her.

The only person who could bring that occasional frail smile to her face, despite the hullabaloo in her life, was Aunt Nina. She gave her the much-needed space and never questioned her disconcerted behaviour. It was not that Sanchez didn’t try to contain her emotions, but the past was too harsh, and her melancholy state often surged without any forewarning.

She didn’t want to trouble her aunt anymore, and moving out to her own place was the only answer she could think of. She managed to get some odd jobs after college hours and earned enough to rent a place. This was her escape from the guilt of showing a glum demeanour to her aunt.

She had several unanswered questions, many unresolved mysteries, and a mind crowded with anxieties, all screaming loud, making a perfect cacophony, with no visible escape path.

**Chapter 12**

***10:00 AM 16th March 2012, Ayr, Scotland***

The apartment was a sixteen-by-twelve-foot studio with an attached bathroom and a small kitchen area. The room had just enough space to fit a small mattress. All her belongings were scattered around the cramped space, unkempt, and no effort was spent to put the place in order. It was no more than a refuge for her, a temporary home until she could devise a well-thought-out plan to find John Patterson.

It was a beautiful Friday morning. The summer was around the corner, and the warm weather consumed the surroundings. People were out on the streets enjoying the climate, taking a break from the dreadful winter they had witnessed not too long ago. She had a day off from college. With nothing to do, she pulled out a worn-out notebook from the closet and started flipping through the pages. It was the same diary that had John’s name scribbled in it, the writings that had given her the idea to leave her hometown and come looking for him in Ayr. It was not that she had not gone through the journal before, but she kept revisiting it with dwindling hope of finding one clue that could help her find John.

And then she was on the page that had the map of Ayr. It was a busy print with odd scrawls made in pencil in a haphazard pattern. Over time it had discoloured, giving it a faded greyish look. She had seen it many times before, and every time it appeared more puzzling than before. The TP scribbled within parentheses next to John’s name was always a mystery to her. She had tried decrypting it many times but never succeeded. Today was no different. She kept staring at the page for a while and when she couldn’t conclude anything meaningful, frustrated, she put the notebook away.

The time was a little over 11:00 in the morning. She opened the window and put her head out to feel the gentle breeze. There was something in the air, an unexpected freshness that convinced her to leave the confinement of her room. Seaport Park had always been her favourite destination, a place where in the past she had succeeded in breaking away from the shackles of her gloominess.

She boarded the bus, took a window seat and put her earpiece on with Adele’s “Set Fire to the Rain” playing at a high decibel. She was in a trance, floating in her own world when the signage on a passing building almost made her jump from her seat. The tall structure had TransPacific written on it, but what caught her attention was the letters TP carved like a logo shining distinctly in the morning sun. She hurriedly got off at the next stop and traced her way back to the building. It was a wild guess, and her instinct suggested some connection between the TP in the notebook and TransPacific.

The building was a tall glass structure and the architecture suggested a modern design. With some hesitation, she stepped into the lobby, and the place blew her away. It was nothing less than what she had seen in movies. The place was bigger than life. It had a gigantic water fountain positioned in the centre, rising to an almost 100-foot ceiling. An array of modern paintings graciously covered the walls. At a distance she could see an elevator guarded by biometric access and an elegantly designed reception desk. The place also had sturdy, serious-looking security guards, discreetly monitoring the area with prying eyes. The whole setup was intimidating, making her nervous and fidgety.

She mustered some courage and approached the receptionist. “How can I help you?” the receptionist queried with a warm smile.

In a low voice, she enquired if she could meet John Patterson.

“Do you know which department he works for?” the receptionist asked.

She had no clue how to respond and shook her head. She was perspiring. Her face looked pale as if she had been caught pilfering.

The receptionist smiled, mostly to comfort her, and continued, “Let me see,” and got busy working on her computer. After a few minutes, she looked back at her, still carrying the same smile, and declared that there were four John Pattersons, and unless she had some more details she wouldn’t be able to help.

Sanchez had mixed reactions. While the company had four Johns, it hardly meant anything. John Patterson was a common name. Besides, even if all four of them were called out, there was no way she could have recognised the one she was looking for. Going on a quizzing spree with them was never an option anyway. She politely thanked the receptionist and promised to come next time with more specifics.

**Chapter 13**

***11:45 AM 16th March 2012, Ayr, Scotland***

Despondent, she traced her way towards the exit. Her mind suggested ways to converse with all four Johns, but none of the alternatives held any water. She had almost exited the building when a large kiosk by the guest waiting area caught her attention. A few visitors were playing with it. While unsure what the kiosk was about, out of curiosity she walked towards it. There was nothing on her mind besides a faint hope of finding answers. She spotted an empty space and tapped on the large interactive display.  The screen came to life. It had various zones providing information about the company, its business and credo.  She was randomly playing with the infographics when a section called “New Employment Opportunities” caught her attention. She tapped the area with interest. The screen displayed information about TransPacific’s recruiting programme for summer interns. There was an email address at the bottom of the display and instructions on how to apply for the job. She took a quick note of it before leaving the building.

On her way back home, she convinced herself to do whatever it took to get the job. She had a strong hunch that the person she was looking for was at TransPacific. And her hope was to reach him through this job. After reaching home, she soaked herself in the bathtub. The deluge of hot water helped her immensely to collect her thoughts. She felt fresh and rejuvenated. For the first time since she had come to Ayr, she had something to cheer about, a purpose to chase, and she wanted to give it her best.

Her first strategy was to write an email to them. She knew if she failed to get their attention the first time, it would be her last, as she presumed there would be some software to filter and accept only one mail per candidate. She wanted the letter to be crisp and to the point, yet interesting. She turned on her computer, and after a lot of writing and rewriting, she crafted her final script, which she believed was one of the best pieces she had ever written. It took her the rest of the evening to bring it to a closure.

She was extremely good at language arts. During her school days, she used to write stories and poetry. This email was the toughest letter she had ever written. Writing poetry was never a do-or-die situation, but finding words to secure a job she badly wanted was overwhelming.  Once satisfied with her work, she read it aloud to make sure it sounded right.

“Dear Madam/Sir,

As a child, my most exciting moments were always at Christmas time, when Santa magically delivered my wish list toys. I used to thank the reindeer that carried Santa to my home despite the dreadful winter.  I also grew up thanking the postman who gave me the joy of receiving my pen friends’ letters from distant places, or surprise gifts from my grandparents. It always brought a smile to my face.

Soon I was introduced to reality and learned there are no magic reindeer. Nor does the postman have a magic wand that could bring surprises on a whim. And I learned that all the complex crisscrossing of parcels across the globe is immaculately handled and accurately delivered by logistics companies such as yours. It is because of enterprises like yours that this otherwise vast world is truly within our reach.
