Sanchez had fallen a few metres away from the car. Stephen opened the door and was about to leave the car when Sanchez shouted, “Don’t get out of the car. Go. At least one of us will be safe, and with you around I will have a chance to escape.”

Stephen was perplexed. He was worried and nervous and had no clue how to respond. He finally said, with clear resistance, “I can’t.”

The man was fast approaching. He fired another shot, which hit the car trunk with a loud ear-splitting BANG.

“Don’t be a fool. Can’t you hear the footsteps?” she screamed.

In that moment, Stephen saw the sense in his sister’s words. He knew another minute of delay would jeopardise any chance of his escape. He put his foot on the accelerator and sped away. The screeching sound of the tyres was dreadful, as if providing its final resistance to leaving Sanchez behind. He was frantic. He screamed with frustration, and tears rolled down his cheeks. He kept looking in the rear mirror till the house faded away in the distance. Adriana was too shell-shocked to react to anything. She kept staring at Sanchez without uttering a word. Soon Sanchez was out of her sight.

**Chapter 40**

***5:35 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal***

With a dizzy head, Tiago slowly opened his eyes. His vision was blurry. A nerve-wracking pain shot down his spine as he reluctantly raised his head. He tried to get on his feet, but his legs refused to support him. With brute force, he managed to stand using his hand for support. He almost screamed when a sharp pain ran through his spine, as if he had been pricked with shrapnel. For a few seconds, he struggled to wrap his head around his whereabouts, and gradually the events came back to him.  He looked around and all he saw was miles of open fields. He pushed himself hard and with some effort managed to walk, but with a bad stagger.

As he hobbled towards the house, he found his friends scattered around, unconscious. He tried waking them, pushing them with his feet and speaking loudly, but failed. As he dragged his dishevelled self away from them, he saw a gun lying a few metres away. It took all his might to bend down for the gun. After a few painful attempts, he managed to pick it up and headed towards the house. He was throbbing with pain, but the thought of Sanchez escaping and Benedikt’s rage made his torment look trivial. As he walked past the backyard, he heard a car engine coming to life. He turned the corner and saw Sanchez sprinting towards the front of the car. Though he was within striking distance of the car, he couldn’t do much besides watch her go. He knew his options were running out and he had one final chance to rescue the situation. And against his superior’s orders, he made the decision. Shaking off all inhibitions, he aimed at Sanchez’s leg and fired. There was a loud bang followed by a scream. He knew he had got her this time. Next, he aimed the gun at the car’s tyre but missed by a few inches. He used all his might to reach the car in the shortest possible time. As he moved forward, the car zoomed away, leaving Sanchez behind. He wasn’t concerned, for he wanted the girl and she was within arm’s length of him, sitting in a pool of blood.

He knew what the girl could do, so he was extra careful. He held the gun with both hands, pointed it at her temple and walked towards her. He growled, “You trick me this time, I will kill you. Do you hear me?”

Sanchez gave back a blank stare. Besides the pain on her face, she gave nothing away. There was no fear, no apprehension. Tiago got the jitters seeing her intrepid reaction. He had pointed guns at people on several occasions before, and every time he had seen fear, horror, and dreadfulness in the other person’s eyes. Never once had he encountered anyone with such grit and fearlessness.

He tried again, “Listen, I am serious, so you better behave. I want you to take my support and walk towards the car.”

He stretched out his left hand in anticipation, but Sanchez didn’t budge. He knew his threats wouldn’t do any good, so he decided to take things into his own hands. With the gun in one hand, he tried to make her stand by pulling her arm with the other. Despite a great deal of effort, he only succeeded in moving her an inch. The car was a few strides away, but at this pace, he realised it would take him the entire evening.

It wasn’t working. As he contemplated his next move, he heard approaching footsteps. He turned sharply and found two of his friends, bedraggled, walking towards him.

He now found a reason to smile. After briefing them about the situation, he sought their help to get her to the car. The men sprang into action. While Tiago got the car, his friends dragged her into the backseat. Sanchez exhibited great resistance, but with a weak limb, mounting pain and two men holding her arms, she gave in. She was in the middle seat, surrounded by the two goons.

Tiago decided to take the opposite road, away from the path Stephen had taken a few minutes back. It was a longer route, but he knew this way he could avoid any untoward skirmish. The bruises from Sanchez’s blows were still hurting him, and he knew none of his friends had the strength for any more fighting.

One of the men tore his shirt and wrapped it tightly around Sanchez’s leg.  It eased the bleeding. The bullet had gone through the calf muscle without hurting the bone. She looked exhausted, and her struggle to stay awake was fading fast with each passing moment. The humming of the motor on the deserted road of the laid-back coastal town soon made her drift off to sleep.

Ten minutes into the drive, Tiago decided to call Benedikt. He fumbled around to find the phone and finally located it in his back pocket. He saw numerous missed calls from Benedikt flashing all over the phone screen. He hesitated a bit, but somehow mustered some courage and dialed the number.

Benedikt picked up the phone at the first ring. A sharp voice pierced his ear. “What took you so long?”

Tiago was petrified. He hadn’t dealt with Benedikt before. He pulled himself together and said, “This is Tiago. We have the girl. She is in the car with us and we are on our way to the place.”

Benedikt’s restlessness was instantly pacified. He asked, “How far are you?”

“We should be there in about an hour and a half.”

“Ok. I will see you at the place.” Benedikt’s tone was much calmer this time. For once that afternoon he looked to be in control of the proceedings.

Tiago’s face was pale, as if he had weathered a storm. He couldn’t gather enough courage to mention Sanchez being hurt. He remembered Benedikt’s clear instruction, “Do not hurt the girl, no matter what the situation is.”

“She didn’t leave me with any options,” he mumbled, trying to reason with his troubled soul. He knew it was a false hope, the best he could do to distract his mind from the confrontation he would face in a short time.

**Chapter 41**

***5:40 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal***

Stephen was driving in an absolutely reckless manner. Tears blurred his vision and his mind was clouded with fretting apprehensions. He was unable to comprehend how he had left Sanchez alone in such imminent danger. And with a confused mind, he kept driving on the desolate roads of Nazaré, unsure where he was headed.

Despite all the proceedings, Adriana demonstrated no reaction. She kept staring outside with no display of emotion. The evening sun was casting long shadows of trees on the deserted road, making everything appear ghostly and eerie. Besides his own wheezing, all Stephen could hear was the constant humming of the motor engine. In that sinister setting, a car went past him with great urgency. There was a sense of fortitude the passing car exuded, which jolted Stephen out of his helpless shell.

“How can I leave her alone?” he mumbled.

He regretted his decision to run away from the situation, and between his trepidations and confusion, he knew he had lost crucial time. With that sudden urge of wisdom, he swung his car around, but failed to negotiate the turn and crashed into the paddy field. With some struggle, he managed to get the car back on the road. The tyres made a screeching noise as he pressed the accelerator with all force, but he instantly had to apply the brakes to avoid banging into an oncoming car. Adriana maintained her silence despite all the commotion happening around her. He was about to speed away when he saw a young man rushing towards him, raising his hand high and signaling him to stop.

There was genuine concern on the man’s face, compelling Stephen to stop much against his wish. He rolled the window down and gave him a curious stare. The man dashed to the car and his incisive eyes became glum when he couldn’t find what he was looking for.

“Where is Sanchez?” His voice reflected deep anxiety.

Stephen was confused, and all he could manage was to keep staring blankly at the stranger.

“Why aren’t you speaking?” the man demanded.

“She got shot. Those men…” His voice trailed, laced with fear and helplessness.

“Move over!” the man demanded. Stephen followed the command without any resistance.

The man drove with purpose and determination. There was speed and aggression as he covered the distance with finesse.

“How could you? God damn! You left her alone to save your life!” he shouted.

Stephen was too ashamed to respond. He hid his face in his palms to avoid any further confrontation. He had no curiosity about the man who knew everything about him, Sanchez and the situation.

**Chapter 42**

***7:15 PM 18th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal***

This time the men committed no mistakes. Everything worked according to plan, and they reached their destination without any more untoward incident.

The place appeared isolated, a few miles south of the Lisbon dockyard. The building was a long flat concrete structure with a dark greyish exterior. The warehouse appeared abandoned, borne out by the long wild vines and unruly shrubs covering the time-stained walls. It was a storage place of some kind from a faraway time.

A tall rusted wrought iron gate valiantly guarded the place. It was open halfway, not wide enough for the car to go through. Tiago got out of the car and pushed the gate. The structure made a squealing noise, resisting the push, and slowly made way.  He drove the car into the open area and parked it right in front of the entrance.

The men got out of the car with urgency. She was not in a state to walk, so they decided to carry her. One of them held her by the arms and the other two wrapped their hands around her legs.  The flow of blood had slowed, but a good amount had been lost, evident from the pale look on her face.

The door to the building was ajar. The warehouse interiors complemented the exteriors. There were no windows to let any light trickle in, and a few dim lights were fighting hard to keep the darkness away. The structure was one long hall with no rooms or partitions. There were steel rods and pipes running throughout the roof, and it appeared that during its prime days the pipes were used to provide cooling and heating to the vast interiors. The whole place was covered with moss, and the floor had caved in at regular intervals, exposing the earth and allowing wild flora to grow in abundance.

The men, carrying the girl, walked towards the centre of the hall. It was evident they knew the place well. Once at the centre, they turned left and continued walking until they reached the wall. There was a small flap on the wall hidden behind a few vines, blending with the wall colour. Tiago tapped the flap, exposing a few small buttons. He pressed the buttons in a pattern, and soon one of the floor tiles slid open, exposing a staircase. The men climbed down the spiral stairs with utmost care. There was another switch right at the bottom of the steps. Tiago pushed the button, and the tile slid back to its original position.

The place appeared to be in a different world. It was a spacious place with a modern look. The ceiling of the basement was almost twelve feet high. There were plenty of lights dropping from the ceiling in S-shaped structures, illuminating the place in pale yellow brilliance. The wide entrance was neatly covered with brown leather sofas, and a coffee table graced the centre with a wide collection of journals and magazines. The walls were coloured a light yellow, sporting some modern-day paintings. To the left of the staircase was a transparent glass door, which guarded a few closed rooms. And on the right, there was an oval-shaped conference room with opaque glass walls. Tiago tapped a few buttons on a keypad located right outside the conference room, and the door opened with a click. All of them entered the room. Francisco and Damian were standing in the corner, and Benedikt was pacing across the room.

Benedikt’s face lit up for a moment, but soon it showed deep concern. His heart was pulsating with such vigour that he could hear it. A thousand emotions crossed his face. He was torn between the excitement of seeing her and his wrath at finding her in such a dishevelled state.

“What happened to her?” he screamed.

Tiago, without mincing any words, said, “I shot her in the leg. I didn’t have any option. Otherwise, she would have run away.”

Benedikt’s rage had no boundaries. His face turned red and his eyes fumed with anger. “I told you not to hurt the girl at any cost. Not at any cost!” He took his gun out and almost pulled the trigger but managed to refrain. The best the others could do was to keep any conversation to a bare minimum.

Benedikt knew Sanchez needed medical attention. He dashed with all his might to be near her. He had spent the last few days in unbearable longing. He had spent hours brooding, playing in his mind the things he would do when he met her. But fate had brought him close to her in the most infelicitous manner he could have ever imagined.

He gently held her unconscious body in his arms. The spiral locks of her disarrayed tresses were all over her face. With one hand still holding her, he put the hair aside to make her face visible. The touch of her soft cold skin sent shivers through his spine. Her face hardly showed the signs of the ordeal she had been through. The serenity she was exuding made him nervous. He craved to heal her with his touch, wanted her to regain her senses, and hoped to tell her how much he was yearning for her.

Instead, all he could manage was to keep looking at her in silence.

**Chapter 43**

***6:05 PM 18th April 2014, Nazaré, Portugal***

Maxwell squeezed the car into the narrow driveway of Adriana’s house. He flung open the door and rushed towards the main entrance. The house looked deserted with no sign of any soul.  The surroundings complemented the derelict house, welcoming him in a most undesirable manner.

Stephen shouted from the back, “This way!”

Maxwell turned around and traced his way back to the sidewalk. There was blood all over the place and a long stretch of dark gore across the driveway. He could make out that Sanchez had been dragged. The smear of blotchy brownish spots indicated her resistance as she was hauled. He knew he had to act fast, but he had no clue what to do. He held Stephen by his shoulders and demanded, “Now listen to me carefully. Tell me everything. What happened here? Every second is precious, so be brief and exact.”

Stephen rubbed his eyes to get a clearer vision and told the entire episode in an embarrassed tone. He concluded with a shaking voice, “The man who shot her was standing there.” He pointed towards the narrow pathway, which opened to the paddy fields. He regained a spark in his trailing voice, as if remembering something important, and continued, “There were four of them, but when I fled, I could only see three in the rear-view mirror. One of them must still be there in the fields!” Without wasting any time, Stephen led the way towards the open area where he and Sanchez had confronted the goons not too long ago. Maxwell followed him. As he went past the backyard, he saw his police officer friend lying in a pool of blood.

Against his wish, Maxwell ignored his friend, for he wanted to catch hold of the goon first, if he was still around. Nuno, the last man whom Sanchez had knocked out, was still there, lying flat and unconscious.

“Guard him,” demanded Maxwell, and he sprinted back to check on his officer friend. He had been shot twice in the chest. He checked for a pulse, but it was too late. He had been dead for a while. He rushed back to check on the goon. The man was lying motionless on the vast open field. Staring at the unconscious man, he ordered Stephen, “Go and get some water from the house.”

Maxwell’s only hope was this man. The transmitter he had put in Benedikt’s van had stopped responding a while back. He knew the car carrying Sanchez had taken a detour, for he hadn’t noticed any other car passing by. He regretted his decision not to stay close to Sanchez.

Stephen came back with a water pail. He sprinkled some water on the man’s face. After a few tries, the man came to his senses but struggled to open his eyes. He became aware that he had been captured. He made a faint attempt to escape but was too exhausted to execute his vague plan.

Maxwell took his gun out. “I neither have the patience nor the time. Tell me where you were planning to take the girl.”

Nuno was lost. He wasn’t sure where his friends were. He couldn’t spot the girl anywhere close by. From the conversation, he concluded that his friends had got the girl and this person was trying to find her. He was brought out of his brooding by the cold touch of the gunmetal against his chin.

Maxwell continued, “Look, I don’t have time. If you don’t speak, I will make you bleed to death.”

The man remained quiet without uttering a word.

Maxwell meant every word. He fired a shot right into his leg. A loud painful scream came out of Nuno, and he held his leg in disbelief.

“I am asking for one last time. The next bullet will hit the hand that is holding your leg.”

Nuno screamed for mercy, and in broken English he blurted out the place where his friends were planning to go after capturing Sanchez. With the help of Stephen, Maxwell lifted him and put him in the car. He looked at him with fierce eyes. “The sooner you take me to them, the better your chance of survival.”

Nuno agreed despairingly as he squirmed in pain.

Maxwell instructed Stephen to stay back with Adriana and told him that he would call him soon. Stephen resisted. But he had no option but to follow orders.

While driving, Maxwell called the Lisbon police and told them about the unfortunate incident. He was tempted to seek the help of the local police to trace Benedikt, but he knew that would need a lot of explanation. Moreover, he wasn’t sure whom to trust in the system.

He spotted the warehouse from a distance, deserted and in a dilapidated condition. He dragged Nuno inside the hallway. Nuno had no verve left to fight back, and he cooperated in showing him the secret pathway. Spots of blood in the area confirmed Sanchez’s presence in the building. He touched the blood. The fluid was still warm with no clot formation, suggesting that the episode had happened not long ago.  He left Nuno lying on the floor as he took out his gun and advanced with watchful steps. There was no sign of any living soul.  He climbed down the staircase and came to an open hall. Maxwell saw a large conference room towards his right. The doors were open and he barged in but found no one. For the next few minutes, he searched every corner of the place without any success.

“They must have taken her to a hospital,” he concluded, though he was not sure what incentive Benedikt might have in saving her life.

He leaped out of the underground hideout to the place where he had left Nuno. He found him lying there, almost inert and bleeding profusely. He showed no mercy whatsoever as he bent and lifted him by his collar. Nuno was half dizzy, on the verge of passing out.

“Your luck is riding high. I had no intention of offering you any medical attention, but it looks like you are going to get it. How far is the nearest hospital?” Maxwell queried.

Nuno looked dazed and managed to say in a thin voice, “About twenty minutes away.”

Maxwell dragged him back to the car, dumped him in the front seat and started driving with Nuno acting as his navigator.

In about twenty minutes he was in the lobby of the city hospital. The emergency area looked more crowded than he had expected. He put Nuno on a waiting chair, approached the lady at the counter, flashed his ID and stated in a sharp, low tone, “The man has been shot. Treat him, if you wish. Call the cops and don’t let him go till I come back.”

The lady was startled, and she hurried to the doctor’s room to get some assistance.  There was another lady in the reception area who witnessed the conversation. She too was in a state of disbelief and carried a frightened expression on her face. Maxwell turned his attention towards her, flashed Sanchez’s picture and enquired, “Did someone bring this girl here in the last hour or so? She was shot in her leg.”

The lady looked puzzled and denied seeing her. Maxwell had no time to waste and decided to take things into his own hands. He rushed towards the emergency room, swung open the door and entered. He went room by room to check but couldn’t find Sanchez. He checked with the resident doctors, but everything turned out to be negative.

His heart sank. Deep inside he knew every passing moment was making his task of finding her more arduous. He started his journey towards the old dilapidated house where Benedikt had been a few hours ago. He was running out of options and falling short of ideas.

**Chapter 44**

***7:30 PM 18th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal***

Benedikt was devastated, as if a bolt had struck and shattered him to pieces. He was a man of a thousand shades, oscillating between hues of love and shadows of pain. He had waited for this moment for a lifetime, and when the moment presented itself he was unable to fathom it. It was surreal, and with every passing minute it was becoming harder for him to comprehend. He wanted to embrace her, hold her forever and never let her disappear again.

For the first time, he was convinced of his yearning for her. And now that he was certain, he was determined to do anything to get her out of the misery. He wrestled hard not to let his innocuous admiration appear as ogling to others. And before he could free himself from the quagmire, Sanchez opened her eyes, coming out of her delirious state. Seeing her quivering with pain, he felt the practicality of the situation sink in. He lifted her in his arms and rushed towards the staircase. He wanted to get her the best medical assistance at the earliest possible time. As he climbed the stairs, he announced, “Call the best doctor in town, and bring him to the nearest hospital.”

Francisco led the way to the van. He was talking in a hurried manner, instructing some doctor to come to the nearest hospital. He opened the back door of the van and helped Benedikt lay her straight on the seat. Benedikt climbed into the van, carefully lifted her head and put it on his lap as he made space to sit next to her.

Francisco took the driver’s seat and Damian sat next to him. Francisco realised that in the commotion he had misplaced his gun somewhere. With a faint hope, he opened the glove compartment and fumbled around for the gun. And then his hand struck a metal piece. He peeped inside and found a tiny instrument taped in the lowermost corner of the compartment. It appeared damaged. He pulled it out and showed it to Damian as he continued to study it.

“It is a bug!” announced Damian. For the first time that evening Benedikt paid attention to his accomplices’ conversation.

“It makes complete sense. That police officer at Nazaré wasn’t an accident. Destroy it right away. Tell our men to vacate all places. I want to leave this place as soon as we can,” said Benedikt.

“This piece is already damaged. I believe this happened when I threw the gun in the compartment. It must have hit it squarely.”

Francisco was still amazed by his accidental discovery. He finally got something right even if it was by accident.

The engine came to life with a big roar. Benedikt’s mind was racing, contemplating who could be behind him. He wanted to leave Lisbon as fast as he could. Now that he had Sanchez, he wanted to avoid all unnecessary confrontations. The evening sun was fading fast, spreading a scattered tinge across the horizon. It resembled his state – his trepidations were receding, and a sense of calmness was slowly engulfing his soul.

**Chapter 45**

***8:45 PM 18th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal***

The streetlights were appearing in a sporadic fashion on the busy roads of Lisbon. A dull wind was casually sauntering with a hint of laziness written over it. People were gracing the streets, walking aimlessly with no set destination, but not Maxwell. He had a purpose. His face exuded anxiety and his mind was clouded with a sense of urgency. He parked the car a street away from where Benedikt had stayed a few hours ago. The porch was empty, and the place was in complete darkness. He tried opening the front door, but it didn’t open. Frustrated, he pulled out his gun, pointed it at the doorknob and shot it. The silencer on the gun made a blip sound and the knob gave up its resistance.  He flung the door open and the worst of his fears came true.

No one was in sight. He searched the house with haste but found nothing. He felt asphyxiated. His intuition suggested he had lost track of Sanchez. Time wasn’t on his side, for every passing minute was taking him further away from her.

He sprinted back to his car and sped through the busy evening traffic of Lisbon. This time his destination was Montijo airport. His last dwindling hope was Benedikt’s jet, Benedikt’s escape path from Lisbon. He hoped to reach the airport before Benedikt. Throughout the drive, he tried hard to avoid all the spiteful deliberations that invariably crowded his mind but failed miserably.

The drive to the airport was strenuous. The traffic didn’t help his cause either, and it took him over an hour. On the way, he got a call from the Lisbon police, inquiring about the death of the police officer. Maxwell tried to explain Benedikt’s transgressions and sought their help in finding the van. But the Lisbon police weren’t interested in chasing a random van without any concrete evidence. He realised he was wasting time convincing them and promptly concluded the conversation.

The airport was an unusually quiet place. He spotted a few planes resting in the open hangars but wasn’t sure which one belonged to Benedikt. He parked his car by the entrance and rushed into the small waiting area through the revolving door. He saw a reception desk at the end of the hallway towards the left. It was near the back door, which opened onto the airfield. There was a young girl guarding the place and she was busy talking on her phone.

Maxwell took a few long strides to the desk. The young girl took her attention away from the phone, looked at him and asked, “Good evening. How may I help you, sir?”

He forced a smile and asked, “Is Benedikt’s jet still here?” His heart was racing as he anxiously waited to hear what she had to say.

The girl continued to carry her infectious smile and enquired, “Do you have his jet’s tail number?”

He knew it was a valid request. “Sorry, miss, I don’t have it with me. Can you please find it by name?”

Maxwell’s demeanour strongly influenced the girl and she agreed to his request.

“Did you say Benedikt? They left about twenty minutes back.”

Maxwell knew he had lost the chase. With a dejected tone, he asked, “Was there a girl with him?”

“I wouldn’t know. My shift started only a few minutes back.”

He was exasperated. It seemed everything was working against him. “Where did the jet go?”

He tried not to sound riled as he put his query with some reluctance.

The girl knew she was giving away information against the protocol, but she proceeded anyway, “They are off to Tallinn.”

Maxwell scribbled his number on her notepad and said, “Thank you. Do me another favour. Can you call the other attendant and find out if there was a girl in her early twenties with him?”

“I will. I have a day off tomorrow. If your schedule permits, we can meet. Maybe over dinner?” The girl smiled as she put forward her request.

He carried a mischievous smile and responded, “Maybe.” He couldn’t take the chance of upsetting the girl. Besides, she was extremely helpful.

**Chapter 46**

***7:45 PM 18th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal***

The nursing home was a mile off the highway exit. It was a small two-storied building, dwarfed by the crowded concrete neighborhood. The van took a loop around the curve to reach the nursing home’s lobby. Two men came rushing with a stretcher to carry the injured girl inside.

Sanchez was struggling to keep herself awake. She appeared anemic and had no strength to discern the situation or resist the proceedings. The voices seemed to come from a distance, and her body wanted to spiral down to rest.

She was put on an operating table. The doctor checked her vitals, found her pulse running low and decided to put her on a drip.   Benedikt kept watching her with a fretful expression as she went through the required surgical procedures. About thirty minutes into the treatment, the doctor announced, “The bullet went past her calf muscle. It is a flesh wound. She should be fine in a few weeks.”

Benedikt thanked the doctor. “We have to leave now. I have a private jet and I can easily accommodate a bed inside. Do you see any issue with that?”

The doctor frowned. “I wouldn’t recommend it. What if she needs a doctor’s attention midair?”

“Well, in that case, you are coming with us,” Benedikt announced and stormed out without waiting for his response.

An ambulance pulled up at the entrance and soon Sanchez, the doctor, and Benedikt along with his team left for the airport. Benedikt was sitting beside Sanchez. Her eyes were closed, and her face exuded a faint radiance.  It was a renewed hope, an anticipation of the arrival of good times. He was relieved and waited for her to regain her senses.

The doctor appeared upset. He had no option but to follow the course. But for Benedikt, everything was falling into place. He could now be in Tallinn for the big meeting, and even better, he had Sanchez with him.

It took them less than twenty minutes to be on the plane. The jet slowly inched towards the runway. All this while Benedikt’s entire attention was on Sanchez. She was in a deep slumber. He was glad she was getting the much-needed time to recuperate from the sufferings she had undergone.

“How will she react once she regains her senses? Will she get to know my state of mind? Will she accept my love?” His mind was racing with competing thoughts.

**Chapter 47**

***11:30 PM 18th April 2014, in the air, en route to Tallinn***

There was no sound besides the roaring of the jet. Everyone assumed silence, except for Benedikt. He appeared restless. It seemed he couldn’t get enough of her and kept looking at her.  All his annoyance and trepidations seemed to have faded away after he was assured of her good health. However, shades of despair occasionally clouded his elated soul. Deep within he knew it was his fear of losing her that was causing him to quail. He had many questions for her, but he was unsure whether he would ever have the courage to speak his mind.

They were two hours into their journey when Sanchez came out of her deep slumber. She looked around with confused eyes. After a few minutes of struggle, she tried getting on her feet, but failed. Benedikt held her and said in a calm voice, “You need to rest. Let me call the doctor.”

For some reason best known to her, she listened to Benedikt and remained still. Benedikt called the doctor, who appeared half asleep as he took Sanchez’s hand to check her vitals.

“How do you feel now?”

“Where am I?” she enquired, confused and startled.

He had no answer for her. He himself was in captivity and unsure where he was headed.

“Well, I would suggest you have some soup. I will administer an injection. It will help you to fight the fatigue.” He succeeded in avoiding her question.

“She is fine now. All she needs to do is eat and rest. She should be back on her feet in a week.”  He turned his attention to Benedikt to avoid any further questions from her.

While Sanchez remained disconcerted, Benedikt was at peace. He asked the attendant to get Sanchez some soup and requested the doctor to join Damian at the rear of the plane. He wanted some time alone with her.

Sanchez’s memory of the evening started returning in patches. She had a vague remembrance of being dragged into a car. Besides that, everything else was a series of hallucinations. And now she was on a plane, unsure of where she was headed. One thing she knew for sure was that the hope of finding her father was eroding at a rapid pace. She flinched and assumed a glum look. Benedikt saw how her face turned pale and how a sign of uneasiness engulfed her.  He felt terrible seeing her in such a helpless state.

“Let me help you sit up so that you can have your soup.”

Sanchez resisted taking any help from him. She shook her arms free of his hold and looked away. Benedikt tried again, but she held the bed rails with firmness and blurted in agitation, “Leave me alone. Will you?”

Benedikt was composed and answered in a soft tone, “No one wants to harm you. You weren’t supposed to be shot. It was a mistake. Now if you don’t get well, how will you pursue your undertakings?”

Sanchez was surprised to see the care displayed by Benedikt. She had eluded him for the past several days, and in all seriousness, she should be a corpse by now. But instead of questioning her, he was concerned.  She knew there had to be a motive behind his dubious behaviour but failed to decipher it.

Benedikt’s strong arms overcame her feeble resistance, and though indisposed, she gave in to his request. He put a cushion behind her back and placed the soup tray in front of her. She drank her soup. She was famished, and the soup tasted like heaven.

“How is the pain?” Benedikt enquired.

His constant gazing was making her uncomfortable. “Where are you taking me?” Her voice had a clear shade of agitation.

“We are going to Tallinn. I guess you stole the map to be there. So, in a way, I am helping you.” He didn’t mean to portray it as sarcasm, but it came out that way.

She never wanted to show her melancholy self to Benedikt, so she kept staring into space without giving anything away.

“I know it is hard for you to believe, but let me reassure you no one is here to harm you. I know a lot about you, but not your motive. Tell me what is on your mind. I promise, if I can help, I will do everything to help you, and in case I cannot, I will let you know right away.”  Benedikt went ahead with his monologue.

Sanchez was still staring at nothingness and refused to speak.  Benedikt was a tad frustrated. By now he knew stubbornness came to her innately.   Exasperated, he decided to leave her alone. He retraced his steps to his sofa, loosened a couple of his shirt buttons and closed his eyes in a desperate attempt to take his attention away from her.

Sanchez’s mind was in overdrive, thinking about ways to escape. One thing was evident – Benedikt had a soft corner for her. She knew he would risk a lot for her. The more she weighed the thought, the more she was convinced that Benedikt was her key to escape. She wanted to reciprocate his endearment, but in a deceptive manner. She wanted to be prudent to avoid making it look obvious. Luckily, she wasn’t badly hurt, but she knew it would take a few days for her to recuperate. She decided to use her healing time to come close to Benedikt.

She decided to maintain her silence for the remainder of the flight to make her charade appear more real.  From the corner of her eye, she glanced at Benedikt and saw him sleeping in peace. The flight to Tallinn was long, and the best way to kill time was to get some respite from the daylong ordeal she had gone through. With a crowded mind and a hopeful heart, she closed her eyes.

**Chapter 48**

***10:00 PM 18th April 2014, Lisbon, Portugal***

Maxwell used his charm to his advantage, and the receptionist confirmed the presence of a girl on the plane to Tallinn. He also learned she had been brought to the plane on a stretcher.

“I have to be in Tallinn as soon as possible. I am about twenty minutes away from the Lisbon Airport. Can you put me on the next available flight?” There were no greetings, no introductions. All his voice carried was urgency.

Maxwell had a strong hunch he would not get any flight to Tallinn so late in the night. There was a moment of silence before the lady on the other side responded, “I am afraid there are no flights to Tallinn tonight. The first flight out is tomorrow morning at 6:00 AM. You can take a connecting flight tonight, but that will take you closer to Tallinn. You will still be in Tallinn only by tomorrow morning.”

Waiting until morning wasn’t an option, so he decided to explore getting a private jet. He knew it wouldn’t be easy. It meant talking to his superiors and convincing them of such an exorbitant expense. To the extent possible, he avoided any interactions with his seniors. However, the situation required him to break the mores, so he continued with a reluctant voice, “Is Drake in office?”

The lady was surprised to hear Drake’s name. She knew Maxwell well, so in a teasing tone she said, “Well, I hope you aren’t resigning.”

Maxwell heard the jovial sarcasm in her voice. He had neither the mood nor the time to flirt, so he insisted, “I am running against time. Put me through to him. Will you?”

Without wasting any more time, she transferred him to Drake. She knew Maxwell meant business today.

Drake had a surreal attitude towards everything. Maxwell usually restricted his conversations with him to a bare minimum and put them off till they became inevitable. Though Drake was aware of Maxwell’s intractable behaviour, he tolerated him because Maxwell was the best he had. It was an unstated protocol, and they both managed to stay out of conflict by limiting their discussions to the extent possible.

Maxwell heard the deep-throated hello on the other end of the line. He knew the voice well and continued, “I am close to busting the TransPacific racket. I need your urgent help in getting a private jet arranged at Montijo airport near Lisbon. It will be a long flight, and I would appreciate it if you could get me a good throttle engine.”

The callous attitude shown by Maxwell made Drake furious. There was no explanation whatsoever for such an atrocious demand. He tried controlling his anger and asked, “Would you care to explain why you cannot take a regular flight out of Lisbon? Where the hell are you headed? How many hours would you save anyway?” There was a clear refusal in his tone.

Maxwell was agitated. At some other time, he would have fought back, but not today. He wrestled down his rage and responded with a flat voice, “One of the main accomplices is on the run to Estonia. I am afraid he will find his way to Russia the moment he lands there. Every moment is precious, sir!” He stretched the word sir to underline the importance of his demand.

It was late in the evening, and Drake hardly had the energy left to argue. Besides, Maxwell always had the best arguments to convince him. After a long pause, he announced, “In thirty minutes you should have the jet,” and abruptly concluded the conversation.

Maxwell was relieved to be out of the situation. Next, he had to talk to Stephen. He called Adriana’s house number, and the line was picked up before he could hear the phone ring.

Maxwell said in a hurried tone, “Take the car and drive as fast as you can to Montijo airport. Do you have a GPS with you?”

Stephen responded, “I called Jules. He will drive me.”

“Good.”

Maxwell knew he had lost some crucial time, but he was still hoping to get Sanchez free from Benedikt. He had no clue how. However, now that he knew where she was, he was roaring with confidence and beaming with newfound zeal.

**Chapter 49**

***4:30 AM 19th April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

The jet touched down in Tallinn right before the break of dawn.  Damian sprang to action and got busy on his phone, ensuring everything was in order. The jet came to a complete halt, and the flight stairs were rolled down. Damian was the first to get off the plane. There was a large black van waiting for them. He had a quick word with the team on the ground, and soon all of them were on the plane.

Benedikt glanced casually towards Sanchez and found her wide-awake. He cleared his throat and tried to put his point forward, sounding as genuine as possible. “I request you not to resist the move. Like I have told you before, I don’t mean any harm.”

To his surprise, he found her in profound quietness, as if she had surrendered to the situation. And like before, she hardly bothered to respond. When asked, she lay on the mobile bed without any resistance. Soon the three men lifted the stretcher and carried her off the plane. It was still dark in Tallinn. The neon light hanging from the hangar was illuminating the open canvas in a bright white shade. It was quite cold for a spring morning. There was a light wind blowing, adding to the coldness. Benedikt tucked his hand into his trouser pocket and paced down the flight stairs, unsure what was causing him to shiver more, the weather or the uncanny girl.

He hopped onto the front seat next to the van driver, while Damian, along with another person, sat alongside Sanchez. There were no greetings and no pleasantries exchanged. An unusual quietness filled the van. The roads were empty, and they drove without any delay to their destination.

The grand Hotel Palace was one of the most elegant hotels in Tallinn, located next to the town’s Freedom Square. They had two presidential suites reserved. Sanchez was on her own in one of the suites. This time Benedikt wanted to take no chances. Her room was well guarded to prevent any escape. Benedikt decided to take some rest and went straight to his suite without any conversation with her.

It was ten in the morning when Benedikt woke up. After a long time, he had rested well. His mind could finally repose in peace after the last few days’ ordeals. After taking a quick shower, he went to check on Sanchez’s wellbeing. The guard, upon seeing Benedikt, knocked on the door and waited for a while before opening it. Benedikt found Sanchez sitting on the bed and the doctor examining her.

“Good morning!” He smiled as he entered the room. The dining area had a nice spread of breakfast ready to be devoured.

The doctor responded in a pleasant tone, “Good morning.”

Sanchez remained quiet and conveniently ignored Benedikt’s presence.

“How do you feel now?”

Sanchez looked at him intensely, and after a long pause responded, “I want to talk to you in private.”

A sudden surge of excitement engulfed him.

“Would you like to have breakfast as we speak?”

She tried getting out of bed but failed. Benedikt leaped forward to give her a helping arm. She hesitated at first, but when she failed to balance without support, she gave up her resistance and held his stretched hand to balance herself.

Benedikt was jolted by an unexplainable sensation at her touch. A sense of exhilaration consumed him, and he slowly drifted to a euphoric state. The moments he had spent with her in the past came rushing back, making him thrilled, nervous, dizzy, all at once. He controlled his surge of emotions with great difficulty and helped her sit down at the breakfast table. His mind was racing. He wanted to speak but feared stating something uncalled for. And before he could decide the best course forward, Sanchez’s soft voice broke the uncomfortable quietness. “Thank you.”

“You are thanking me for what?” Though he was thrilled to hear those words, he eschewed showing any emotion.

Sanchez ignored his question and continued, “How did you come to know about my location?”

Any conversation was good for Benedikt. “I will not hide anything. Your mysterious disappearance from the hotel made me conclude that the only reason for you to come close to me must be the map. The map was linked to TransPacific. After many unsuccessful attempts to nab you, my mind started racing, and I was curious to find out if there was anything common between you and TransPacific.” He took a pause to observe her. She was attentive, her eyes glued to him.

He continued, “Soon I came to know that your real name is Sanchez, which made it easy to trace you. I found that you were an employee of TransPacific. I checked everything you did at the company. I looked through the security logs, emails and everything electronically available about you. After laboriously skimming a plethora of information, I zeroed in on a few names in TransPacific that were linked to you. John Patterson was one such name.”

He poured a glass of orange juice for her and continued, “I must tell you, John didn’t budge at all. We tried all measures but failed to get anything from him. We then decided to use other means to find something meaningful. Checking John’s phone records was one such means, which led us to a big breakthrough. We found that he received a call from Denmark, and immediately after that he made a call to Lisbon. We already knew from the airport records that you were in Lisbon. We put the two together and figured out where you were. We knew John called Adriana, and I put her house on watch. The rest you know.”

He paused to observe her reaction, but Sanchez gave nothing away. After a frustrating wait, Benedikt queried, “Do you have anything to say?”

She was playing with the coffee cup and had no intention of responding. Benedikt continued, “You invaded my privacy, came close to me and made me behave like a human again. I know we were together for only a few hours, but those moments were enough for me to discern. And against all my perceived absurdity, I fell for you. The more you eluded me, the stronger that craving became. I know it may sound foolish and insignificant to you, but the truth is I long for you. When you are around, there is calmness. There is serenity.”

Benedikt couldn’t believe he had spoken those words. He left the table and slowly sauntered his way to the large glass wall. He stood by the window, staring into space with his back to her.

Sanchez knew Benedikt was telling the truth. She knew if she could manage it well, she might be able to use his help. A ray of hope, distinct and clear in the midst of all the madness. She wanted to make it look real, for she knew if she missed the mark, her plan would be in complete jeopardy.

Benedikt was still looking at the void when Sanchez responded in a low tone, “You don’t want to know what I am after?”

Benedikt rushed back to her. “Try me once. I won’t disappoint you.” There was excitement in his voice and his eyes were showering eagerness, reflecting the innocence of a young boy.

“Once you hear me, you will forget your promise. If you truly mean your words, why don’t you let me go? I promise you, I will not hold anything against you.” Sanchez looked at him squarely as she uttered those words.

And at that very moment, Benedikt’s energetic bearing was transformed into despair. His candour was miserably failing to convince her to trust him. He made another attempt, “Try trusting me once. If I am unable to help you, I will do exactly what you want me to do. I will let you go.” His voice was soft and reflected a deep yearning for her.

He was looking straight into her eyes, patiently waiting for her response. Sanchez couldn’t manage to hold his gaze for long. She saw a different shade of Benedikt, someone who meant his words. The last thing she wanted was to get swayed. She took her eyes off him. “I need time.”

Benedikt found some hope. “Sure. I will leave you alone for now. I will meet you for lunch at noon. Hopefully, it will give you enough time to decide.” He appeared relieved. His voice had a subtle tone that failed to hide his excitement. He smiled at her and scurried away.

Sanchez kept looking at him until he disappeared behind the closing door. She saw in him a child, a restless soul, and an honest man. Suddenly a wave of sadness covered her soul. She hated the thought of messing with his emotions. Her heart ached for him, but she had bigger problems to solve and until she found the answers, falling in love didn’t fit into any of her schemes. She knew she would be encouraging Benedikt, and at times she might give him vibes she would never mean. A wave of guilt swept over her, leaving her hapless. She tried hard to reason with her mind, but her heart wasn’t aligned with her conceived rationale. To the best of her ability she made an earnest effort to divert her attention, but the more she tried, the more strongly it came rushing back.

Benedikt came to her suite sharp at noon and found Sanchez reposing at the breakfast table. He couldn’t take his eyes off her. Not a shade of the ordeal she had gone through was shadowing her graceful visage. Her hair was unkempt and scattered all over her face. Her long eyelashes were touching the edges of her cheeks, giving her a fairy mien. Even in her sleep, she carried a subdued smile.

The raging sound of Benedikt’s phone stirred the calmness of the room. He cursed himself as he fumbled to switch it off. Sanchez was awake by now and looked at him through her sleepy eyes with a shade of happiness.

Benedikt was mesmerised as she slowly moved her lips and asked, “Can we go out for lunch? I am tired of resting.”

Benedikt wasn’t sure what to say, and he blurted, “Let me check with the doctor,” and without waiting for her response, he rushed out to call the doctor. The doctor checked her and cleared her to go out. A pair of walking sticks was ordered, and soon they were on their way out.

It was a mild afternoon, and the sky was spotless. The slow-moving spring breeze was mellowing the effect of the brightly shining sun. The hotel doors opened to the turquoise coloured multi haze occult, which was standing tall in the vastness of the town’s Freedom Square. The atmosphere was gorgeous and lively. People from all spheres were crowding the web of cobbled streets, some with a purpose and some to admire the gothic look of the continental town. Sunny days were short-lived in this part of the world, and people flocked out at the slightest hint of warmer weather.

They took a left turn from the hotel. Despite Benedikt’s repeated insistence, Sanchez chose to walk instead of taking the car. She wanted to explore the city and break away from her confinement. Benedikt tried his best to match the slow pace of his limping lady. She was doing well with her walking sticks, and they walked aimlessly away from the hotel.

Freedom Square was the town’s centre of attraction. The streets offered plenty of options for visitors to explore. There were restaurants with open sitting areas, street food venues, and vendors selling souvenirs. The slight chilliness in the air was hardly a hindrance to people sitting in the open to enjoy their lunch. After about five minutes of casual promenading, Benedikt sheepishly enquired, “Do you have a place in mind?”

Sanchez responded with a teasing tone, “You don’t have patience. Do you?”

He tried hard to sound as normal as he could. “You have a wounded leg. I am not sure if walking for long is good for you.”

“I am looking for an Estonian eat-out place, but it looks like this place is influenced by tourists.” There was a hint of tease in her faint smile as she put forward her observation.

This was in his comfort zone, and he responded with excitement, “You should have told me before. There is a small place right around the corner that serves by far the most astonishing local delight. It isn’t a fancy place, more like a hole in the wall, but the food is unbeatable. What do you think?”

Sanchez was enamoured of his charm. He appeared like a teenage boy who was excited to show some hidden treasure to the love of his life. There was a spark in his eyes and a warmth of togetherness. She couldn’t resist smiling and announced, “I am ready to explore.”

They took a corner seat by the large window with a refreshing view of the streets. As Sanchez was resting her legs to find a comfortable position, Benedikt took the conversation forward, “Have you been to Tallinn before?”

Sanchez smiled and answered in a word, “Never.”

“Allow me to order the drink. May I?”

Sanchez smiled with affirmation and discreetly watched him from the corner of her eye, as he got engrossed in the menu. The waiter appeared with a broad smile on his face, and Benedikt asked him, “May I get a bottle of Kali?”

Sanchez was getting dangerously attracted to him. His suave manner, his complete control of the situation and the authoritative way he took possession of her were getting increasingly addictive. She sipped her drink and enjoyed its smooth texture. Soon she was brought back from her thoughts by Benedikt’s question, “So tell me your story.”

For some reason, Sanchez did not want to keep anything from him. She took another sip of her drink and reeled her mind back to that fateful Sunday morning.

**Chapter 50**

***12:30 PM 19th April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

“I can never forget that dreadful Sunday. I saw him for the last time from the windowsill of my room, slowly drifting away into the morning mist, never to return.”

Sanchez was gazing into the void, lost in her own world. Benedikt didn’t want to invade her secluded world with his probing. She took a long pause, looked dead straight into his eyes and continued.

“Our family was all we had. Mum, Dad, brother and me, we were so happy together. We played and laughed together and were so content in each other’s company. It was such a blissful time, until one day our father disappeared. He vanished in such an implausible way that I am still not able to comprehend the events of that fateful morning.

“He never worked on a Sunday, but we kept our hopes alive, assuming he had gone to work, tending to some urgent request from the office. But the day went by without any news or sign of him. We combed every corner of our small town, contacted all his friends, and called every person he had any connection with, but he was nowhere to be found. When all our options ran out, we called the police. The police officer traced the path to the bakery where my father used to go every Sunday morning, and after some inquiries, he found out about a suspicious-looking vehicle parked right opposite the shop around the time my father disappeared. While the bakery attendant didn’t see anyone, the choir group from the Kirk right next to the bakery saw a person entering the car. Their description matched my dad’s appearance, and all our dwindling hopes came crashing down. We tried everything to find out about the car but failed miserably. As time advanced, the police assumed nothing could be done, and their effort to find him gradually faded away.”

The waiter interrupted their conversation as he spread out the lunch on the table. Benedikt was keenly listening to her and refrained from voicing any views. He offered her the basket of bread and waited for her to continue.

This was the first time Sanchez had narrated the entire proceedings to anyone. The scenes from the past had played in her nightmares a thousand times, but recounting her experience to someone was strangely relieving. It was as if she was heaving away a long-carried burden. And in that moment, all her guards were slowly but surely dissolving. Unsure of herself, she gave Benedikt a deceptively nonchalant look and continued.

“Six months went by without any news. Our only hope was Mr. MacCallum, the police officer. In the beginning, he did everything he could to find Dad, but soon he withdrew from the case. He stopped answering our calls, and when we confronted him, he appeared callous, not giving any convincing answers. After many unsuccessful face-offs, our hope of getting any meaningful insights from him diminished.

“When the police officer turned cold, I knew the only way I could get Dad back was to take things into my own hands. I was determined but had no clue what to do. And one Sunday morning I decided to put together a plan.”

She took a quick break to chew her bread, gulped some Kali, and took her narration forward.

“I took an old notebook and started making a list of things that I thought were important to initiate my investigation. And after a lot of deliberation, I could pin it down to three.

Talk to Dad’s friend from work

Look through Dad’s office bag

Find out more about Dad’s workplace

“I decided to start with Dad’s friend. My father was discreet about anything that faintly came close to office work. The only co-worker’s name he had ever mentioned, in all likelihood by accident, was John Patterson. I wanted to meet John, but besides the name, I had no clue where to find him. I tried rekindling old memories of my long walks with Dad, but nothing came to mind that could relate back to John.

“As I was going through my options, I remembered how curious I always was about the places he used to travel to. He was a contract deliveryman and worked for multiple logistics companies on short assignments. Whenever I enquired about his travel, he used to say, ‘San, one day I will take you to all those places. I want you to see it for yourself.’ Those words from Dad led me to an incident that had happened about a year before he disappeared.

“I was getting my geography project done. The project was about tracing the migratory birds’ travel path in and out of Scotland. It was a Saturday morning and Dad was at home. I approached him for help. While plotting the map, I enquired about his travel paths and the places he visited. He took the pencil from my hand and drew some lines on the map. I wasn’t sure if they were real or randomly drawn lines, but that was my only concrete lead. I decided to find the notebook and study it closely.”

In the middle of her narration, she concluded she was giving away far more detail than was required. Something in her was making her pour out everything she had held in her heart all this time. She sheepishly gazed at him and found him looking at her with interest.

“Sorry for taking you through all the unwanted details. Let me come to the point...” Unsure how to react, she fumbled as she tried to shorten her narration.

Benedikt interrupted her before she could proceed. He stretched his hand to hold hers, squeezed it gently and said, “I like details.”

To state that the words were reassuring was an understatement. She couldn’t take her gaze away from him. She slowly continued.

“It was a lazy Saturday afternoon. Mum was at the church and Stephen was outside dribbling a football. There was no one to disturb me. I sprinted to the basement, turned on the light and closed the door behind me. There were racks full of old toys, books, and bags. I went straight to the bookrack. There was a huge pile of books filling the space haphazardly. I pulled a stool close to the tall rack, stood on it and from the top shelf pulled out a heap of books. After a long search, I finally located the geography notebook. I pulled it out hurriedly, flipped through it and found the page that Dad had marked with a pencil.

“I latched my room door. I peeped through the window and saw Stephen still dribbling the ball in the front yard. I pulled the window curtain and finally settled on the bed with the notebook. I turned to the map page. There were several lines Dad had drawn showing the places he visited. Between the crisscross of lines was a place marked with a star. The place was Ayr, a port town in the southern part of Scotland.

“Dad was a delivery person, so there was a high possibility he would go to Ayr. I vaguely assumed John would be in Ayr too. Before I settled on Ayr, I wanted to do one last validation. I decided to check Dad’s office bag to see if there was anything related to Ayr. It was in a large closet in our parents’ bedroom.

“I rushed to the closet, opened it and found all Dad’s belongings neatly arranged. His clothes, shoes and the office bag, everything was there. It was overwhelming to see his belongings, and the fact that he wasn’t there loomed in my mind.  I forced myself out of my brooding and took the bag out of the closet. There were official receipts, some pens, and a notebook. I looked through the receipts and found all of them with an Ayr address. I quickly flipped through the notebook, which mostly had addresses from various places in Scotland and England. I took the receipts and the notebook and left the bag in the closet.

“All my findings so far were pointing to Ayr, and Ayr became my default choice to initiate my investigation. Ayr was about three hours away from our village, and to go there for a while, I had to find a good reason. I secured the notebook and the receipts in my school bag. All I had to do was to work on a plan to convince Mum.

“It was half past six in the evening when Mum came back from the church. Stephen was reading a book in the living room and I was in the kitchen fixing dinner. She came straight to the kitchen, gave me a big hug, and I still remember what she told me, ‘You are a big girl now. Thanks for the helping hand.’

“My mind was too busy playing back the plan I had rehearsed earlier. I knew I might have only one chance to execute it right, or else it would be a long wait, for which I had neither the patience nor the time. As we sat around the dinner table, I launched into the first part of the plan. I told her that I wanted to go to Ayr on the pretext of our home being too gloomy as it kept reminding me of Dad, and it was impacting my studies immensely. Mum was reluctant, but she saw the sense in what I was saying. Seeing her soften a bit, I put the second part of my plan forward. I mentioned Aunt Nina in Ayr, with whom I could stay.

“I knew I had struck the right chord. She took her time but eventually agreed.

“I was in Ayr. Aunt Nina was welcoming and ensured I had everything I needed. I started college and before I could notice, a month had passed by. I was anxious and desperate to find answers but didn’t have any means to get to them. I started doing some odd jobs after college hours and was earning enough money to rent a place.”

Sanchez pondered for a moment and narrated in an undertone the details of her stay in Ayr. She told him how she had started working for TransPacific, her quest to find John Patterson and her interaction with Bradley. She told him how she found out about him and how she managed to get the map.

Benedikt was listening with great interest. Giving away the entire story was never her plan. She had started off intending to mention only the absolute essentials, but her heart was in complete control of her mind as she barely held back any secrets.

It was a long conversation. It took Sanchez a good twenty more minutes after lunch to conclude her part. Her eyes were moist, but inexplicably she appeared liberated and exultant.

Benedikt was still quiet. He slowly moved his hand towards her and held her hand softly. Sanchez’s heart missed many beats. She felt the same rush and fervour she had experienced with him not too long ago. She tried hard to control her longing but failed miserably. She was further rattled by the soft mesmerising voice of Benedikt, “I am sorry you had to go through such an ordeal. I will do whatever it takes to find your father.”

Sanchez heard the grit in his voice. And at that moment, she knew he meant every word he spoke. How she had wished all this while to find someone who could drive all her problems away. She desired to crash into his arms and let him sweep her away to a faraway land. This time she wanted to live all over again, but for herself. She was floating away, gently dissolving in his aura.

“I have decided. I will put you on a flight home tomorrow and I will come and meet you once I resolve your father’s mystery.” Those words from Benedikt shook her existence. She came crashing down from that euphoric state.

Sanchez slipped her hand away from him. She was flustered and couldn’t believe what she had heard. She cursed herself for getting carried away. She was incensed and bellowed, “This is my battle and I cannot let anyone else fight it for me.”

Her statement irritated Benedikt, and his tone reflected his frustration. “You know nothing about these people. I know them well. I can’t knowingly put you in any more trouble.”

Sanchez was fuming with anger and she didn’t hold back. “Who are you to decide what I should do?” She reached for her sticks, and before Benedikt could react, she was limping her way out of the restaurant.

Benedikt dashed after her, held her by the arms and shouted, “Are you out of your mind? You are nothing but a stubborn fool.”

Sanchez couldn’t believe what she had heard. “How dare you?”

Sanchez’s anger was beyond comprehension. Benedikt could see her fuming, and he regretted his conduct. He changed his stance and lowered his tone. “Alright, I am sorry. I shouldn’t have shouted at you. Can we go back to the hotel and talk?”

Sanchez’s resentment was uncontrollable, and she stormed forward without uttering a word. He saw her turning towards the hotel. A strong pang of sadness filled his mind. He knew she had gone through a lot and he had to be more considerate and empathetic, provided he got another chance.

**Chapter 51**

***3:30 PM 19th April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

Benedikt was flipping through the television channels at random without much interest. His mind was reeling back every scene from the afternoon. The more he brooded, the more agitated he became, thinking how he had ruined a perfect afternoon because of his behaviour. Deep inside he was convinced he was planning the best for her, if only he had acted in a more sensible manner. His heart urged him to mend things, but he knew the best way to deal with the situation was to give her time. The wait wasn’t easy. 5:00 PM was the time he had locked in his mind to go visit Sanchez, and since then he had stared at the clock countless times. He still had an hour and a half to kill and had already exhausted all options to pass the time. In that moment of despair, he headed towards Sanchez’s suite with renewed purpose.

With some hesitation he announced his arrival. After a few long anxious knocks, he finally heard her voice, “Come in.”

Sanchez was by the writing desk scribbling something on a notepad with great interest. She stayed focused on her work and treated his presence as non-existent. Benedikt walked towards her with quiet steps and occupied the ottoman next to the sofa. He took a moment to regain his composure and eased out one word at a time, “I am so sorry for my behaviour. I have no right to decide things on your behalf.”

Sanchez gave him a long gaze. In that moment of silence, he observed a sense of calmness in her cerulean eyes. And then her quiet voice broke his brooding, “I am sorry too. You need to understand, I can’t compromise when it concerns Dad.”

Those words were magical. All his anxieties melted, and with a sense of excitement he said, “I won’t cross the line again. That’s a promise.”

Sanchez chose not to respond, and instead gave a small smile. For a while, he kept staring at her, dazed. He was pulled out of his reverie when Sanchez, uncomfortable under his piercing eyes, took her gaze away from him and started to scribble on her notepad again.

Benedikt, embarrassed by his conduct, tried to mend the situation. “This time I want to hear you first. Tell me what is on your mind.”

“I don’t have a plan. All I know is that I want to be on that ship. I want to take that voyage and find him.”

Benedikt found her staring into space, lost in oblivion. He knew she meant every word she stated, and rightfully it was her battle. He had thought about this before, but now he was certain.

“I have a plan.”

He paused to observe her reaction. Sanchez’s eyes revealed inquisitiveness, and she brought her attention back to him. Without waiting any further, he continued, “You will join me as my assistant. If your dad is around, we will find him. But I need a promise. You need to be open and honest with me. Don’t do things without my knowledge, and I assure you, together we will find your father.”

Before he could react, Sanchez managed to stand using the table as support and hugged him. “I can’t thank you enough. I promise I will do exactly what you want me to do, and I am sorry for my outrageous behaviour in the restaurant.”

Benedikt was startled. For a moment, he stood there without reciprocating her embrace. Slowly he put his arms around her. The moment was ecstatic, beyond anything he could have ever imagined, and his mind drifted into nothingness.

Sanchez pulled away from his gentle squeeze and said, “I also have a request to make.” She paused for his reaction.

Benedikt couldn’t find his voice. And after some struggle, he responded, “Sure. Go ahead.”

“My brother must be worried. I must let him know that I am fine and I will be gone for a few weeks.”

Signs of hesitation came back to his face, and after a brief pause, he concluded, “Ok, go ahead. But make sure he isn’t aware of the trip.”

Sanchez nodded in agreement. Benedikt left her room with the promise to return after a few hours.

Sanchez was consumed by shades of mixed emotions as she tried to rationalise her comportment. She knew for certain that Benedikt had fallen for her. For a moment, she was ridden with guilt for playing with his emotions. Then she comforted herself by justifying her actions as being for the betterment of her family.

Above all the uncertainties, her mind was clouded with another apprehension, which she was battling hard to reason with. It was her fear of falling in love. She knew sooner or later she would have to confront it. But now was not the time. At least that was how she made peace with her confused self.

**Chapter 52**

***4:00 PM 19th April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

The phone made a loud noise and filled the otherwise quiet coffee shop with a shrill dissonance. Stephen picked up the phone and gave it a good stare, trying to recognise the caller. Maxwell was sitting right across from him and was watching the episode without much interest. Something suddenly crossed his mind, and before Stephen could react, he leaped forward and snatched the phone from his hand.

With a shaking voice, he asked, “Who is it?”

“Where is Stephen?” came a curt reply.

Maxwell wouldn’t have failed to recognise that voice even in his sleep. It was Sanchez, and he heard the subtle agitation in her terse response. His heart leaped with elation. He hurried to hand the phone back to Stephen, for he wanted to put Sanchez’s anxiety to rest.

By now Stephen had a fair hunch that the caller was his sister. He was perspiring, and his heart pulsated with vigour. He struggled to find his voice and said, “Hello!”

“Stephen, it is me. You don’t sound okay. Are you all right?”

Stephen’s happiness had no boundaries. He was euphoric, and it was difficult for him to conceal his exultation.

“Oh! Sanchez, I can’t tell you how happy I am to hear your voice. Where are you? How is your wound? I want to meet you.” He was on a never-ending spree of questions.

“I am fine. Who was the other person with you? His voice sounded so familiar.”

“That’s Maxwell. You knew him as Bradley. It is a long story and I will tell you more when we meet. Where are you? I want to come and get you right now.” He said everything in one breath.

“I am not in Portugal anymore. I have travelled to Estonia.” There was an unstated melancholy in her fading voice.

“Well, I know that. In fact, I am in Estonia too. Where in Estonia are you? How did you escape from Benedikt?”

His revelation astounded her.

“How did you find out about my whereabouts? I am so happy to learn you are here!” There was a spark in her voice, and suddenly all the gloominess that had persisted throughout the conversation vanished.

“It was all because of Maxwell. The rest I will tell you when we meet. Give me your address.” Stephen knew there was no end to their conversations. Both had a lot of explaining to do, and he knew the worry about Benedikt hadn’t yet passed.

“Where are you? I will come and meet you,” she said.

Stephen was puzzled, but he put his curiosity to rest and gave her the coffee shop’s address.

“I am starting now. Looks like you are in the area. Shouldn’t take long. See you soon.”

Maxwell was silently listening to every word that Stephen spoke. By now he knew Sanchez was coming to meet them.

“Did she dodge Benedikt? How come she wasn’t in a hurry? She took all her time to talk. Why didn’t she want them to come and get her?” Maxwell’s mind was spinning. It was a mystery, a conundrum he knew he could only resolve after their meeting. For now, he wanted to enjoy the anticipation of meeting the person who had ruled his soul from the time he met her two years back.

**Chapter 53**

***4:30 PM 19th April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

Maxwell’s heart was pulsating hard. This was it. After many failed attempts, he was finally going to meet her, and he was anxiously waiting for the moment.

And then he experienced an unmistakable aura of her presence consuming the atmosphere. He was desperate to catch sight of her, but she was nowhere to be seen. Was it a hallucination, or had the uncanny waft brought her from a faraway place? In that state of delusion, he craned his neck to get a clearer view. His probing eyes searched every corner but failed to trace her. However, her impressions were everywhere. Lost in his world and driven by his intuition, he turned around, and there she was, standing at the entrance of the coffee shop.

Everything stopped for him, the noise disappeared and the people faded. All he could see was her. It was the most serene emotion he had experienced in a long time, and his apprehensions vanished in her bright composure. She was wearing her little smile. Soon she spotted them and, with a limp, walked towards the table. Maxwell’s heart skipped many beats. He had been waiting for this moment all this while, and when the time presented itself his heart went numb.

Maxwell couldn’t take his eyes off her.  The floral print shirt and the knee-length black skirt graced her fragile frame. Her shoulder-length brown hair was flowing freely over her brightened face. Her large eyes held some unfathomable stories. The top two buttons of her shirt were left open, exposing her long neck. She was the prose for his poetry, the central protagonist of his stories and the hope for what the future held.

Sanchez was extremely uncomfortable seeing Bradley. He had always been a mystery to her, and his sudden reappearance puzzled her even more. She was determined to send him away before breaking into any conversation with Stephen. She knew how vulnerable her brother was, and with Bradley around she didn’t want to divulge a word about her plan.

She approached the table and demanded, “Bradley, what are you doing here?”

The sharpness in her tone penetrated straight through Maxwell. He was taken off guard by the sudden assault. He was searching for the right words, and before he could react, Sanchez jolted him further, “If you are here for the map, I am ready to give it to you, but you have to promise me that you will stay away from us after that.”

Stephen was watching the episode with extreme displeasure and decided to intervene. “Sanchez, his real name is Maxwell, and he isn’t a bad person.”

Sanchez cut him off, “You stay out of this.”

Despite Sanchez’s warning, Stephen interrupted again, “Look, you need to give the man a chance to explain.”

Sanchez had never seen her brother so determined before. His voice was firm, oozing with confidence. There was something in him that calmed her agitation.  She pulled up a chair, put her walking sticks to the side, and sat down with reluctance. “All right. I will listen to your part of the story. Bradley, Maxwell or whosoever you are, go ahead.” The sarcasm in her voice was evident.

Maxwell knew today was the day to end all charades and raise the long-standing curtain on everything he had held back all this while. “By now you know my name is Maxwell. I am an undercover agent at Scotland Yard. I have been on a secret mission for the past several years to expose TransPacific’s illegal trafficking. The police department helped me to get a job at TransPacific Ayr’s office. A few times I came quite close to exposing them, but every time I fell a tad short. You can either call it my failure or give credit to TransPacific’s prudence.”

Maxwell paused to take a sip of his coffee. He could clearly see Sanchez watching him with scepticism. He gazed away from her and continued, “Staying close to Evan helped me to get clarity on how the racket was executed. Though I succeeded in building a level of trust with Evan, it was still not enough for me to be part of his inner circle, and I was never exposed to any insider conversation.

“During my stay in Evan’s office, Benedikt’s name used to surface from time to time. A strange thing happened one day. Evan’s office corridor was cordoned off. No one could be anywhere near that area, and the place was guarded as if it was a military zone.

“After that incident, my curiosity to learn about Benedikt increased many times over. I noticed that Benedikt’s visits to Evan’s office were always unannounced, and it was almost impossible to plan anything. So finally, I decided to plant a bug in the executive conference room where they usually met.

“Though I had access to the floor, it wasn’t an easy job to execute. There was a dedicated security person for Evan’s floor, and the place was under 24-hour surveillance.

“Evan occasionally used to bring us to the executive room for official meetings. He preferred the conference room to his office for all team discussions. I knew these visits were my only chance to plant a stealth microphone, but with the cameras around, it was almost impossible to pull off something of that magnitude.”

Maxwell took a break again to observe Sanchez’s reaction. He found her guard down, and unlike before she was more involved in his narration. He gulped some more coffee and continued, “After carefully considering alternate possibilities, I formulated my game plan and waited for our next team meeting. I didn’t have to wait long. Soon we were called upon to discuss a quality improvement plan for our logistics division. There was a decorative pot on one of the side tables. Discreetly, I pushed the piece to the floor.  It was a ceramic pot and crashed on the hardwood floor, breaking into pieces. I put on a show of how sorry I was and offered to replace it with a new one. Though Evan didn’t bother, the next day I bought a vase and put it back in the conference room. The only difference was that this time the vase had a hidden microphone.

“It was a long two-month wait before Benedikt resurfaced. The microphone worked like a charm. During their meeting, they referred to a secret map, which was the pathway to all the illicit trafficking. I also learned that Benedikt was the custodian of it, and from their conversation, it appeared that the plan was revisited every quarter. I desperately wanted the map, but getting to Benedikt’s den was not an easy task. He was secretive, never stayed at one location and was hard to trace.
