“During that period, you joined TransPacific. You were the sharpest intern I had ever met. You were intelligent, always executed your job to perfection and you had such a charming manner. I found out everything about you. While my sole intention was to know more about you, in the process I also discovered that you were taking an extreme interest in Evan.

“My first impression was that you were part of some government secret service agency. I tried every source I knew to confirm my hunch, but it turned out to be wrong. Unable to explain your mysterious behaviour, I concluded it could be your sheer inquisitiveness. Then I thought I might use your help to get to Benedikt. You know how I confronted you and put you through the training. I learned that Benedikt participated in the annual poker game in Denmark, the only place I knew where Benedikt appeared in public and the only place you could have any chance to meet him.

“After I gave you the tip about Benedikt’s poker adventure, I followed you in complete secrecy. My sole motive was to jump in to your rescue if the situation became unfavorable.”

He heaved a deep sigh and took a break from his monologue. By this time, Sanchez was deeply engrossed in his narration. Her expression had mellowed, and her rage gave way to empathy. Stephen was watching a different Maxwell – one who was tender, one who could be vulnerable, and one who cared.

Maxwell continued, “I was there when you were climbing down from Benedikt’s room. I followed you on the long train ride, chased you on the streets of Zurich and onto the plane to Lisbon. I concluded you didn’t need any help. You were too good for Benedikt or any of his accomplices.

“At the Lisbon airport, you not only dodged Benedikt’s men but evaded me as well. And instead of coming after you, I decided to chase them, partly because I knew where you were headed. But that was a terrible mistake. I underestimated Benedikt’s ability and his clout.”

There was a long pause before Maxwell decided to break the uncomfortable silence between them. “I have spoken nothing but the truth, and I hope you will trust my words.”

Sanchez’s eyes were soft, and she tried hard to keep her composure. She had never expected Maxwell to give such a candid response to her implacable probe. She ignored Maxwell’s plea, and in a gruff voice stated, “I am not alarmed by your presence anymore. I must tell you guys something. I have an opportunity to be on the voyage that starts two days from now. Don’t ask me how I got access, but I want you to know that I will be safe.”

She took a pause to gauge their reactions. She had expected them to be astounded, and they were downright appalled. She continued in haste, as she knew she might be interrupted if the silence was prolonged. “Maxwell, our goals have not changed – we want to demolish TransPacific. I will get you all the evidence to bring down this proscribed swindle.  You need to trust me on this.” She looked into his eyes to decipher his thoughts.

“I can’t leave you with those wolves. They are dangerous. Losing Dad tormented our family, and if anything happens to you, both Mum and I will perish.” This time it was Stephen.  His voice trailed off, laced with anxiousness.

Sanchez leaned forward, held Stephen’s hand and said almost in a whisper, “Nothing will happen to me. You must trust me. I would never take such a decision if I were not confident of succeeding.”

Maxwell had been shaking his head all this time, and he said, “How can you trust Benedikt? He is a crook. This is a dangerous path, Sanchez. We will find some other means to get to your father.”

Sanchez knew she would never be able to convince them of her plan. While she was trying to avoid being uncouth, it appeared to be the only way out of this conundrum. She looked at Maxwell and announced in a dead voice, “I didn’t come here to take your advice. You can either support my decision or we go our own ways.”

There was complete silence. Stephen knew that if Sanchez had made up her mind, it would be impossible to alter it. His face showed frustration. With some deliberation, he said, “Give us some reason to believe that you will be safe. How can I let you go like this?”

Sanchez’s heart tore into pieces, but she did not let any emotion appear on her face. She paused and said in a firm tone, “I am not fool enough to put my life at risk. Besides, I will be in constant touch with you. Jules’s description of the map was accurate. Why don’t you guys follow me? That way you can always come to my rescue when needed.”

She saw some hope return to their faces and went ahead with the momentum she had built, “I will leave the place the moment I have my dad and enough proof to bring down this organisation.”

She waited for their agreement. There was a long pause before Maxwell asked, “Can you tell us how you will be safe?”

This was the question Sanchez had wanted to avoid. She never wanted to tell them that Benedikt was in love with her and he was doing everything out of love. “All I can tell you now is we have a business deal. I will be fine. I need to go now. You follow the map and we will be in touch.”

She sounded uncomfortable as she concluded the conversation.

Maxwell made his final comment in haste, “You need to call us daily. If you fail to call us, I will come looking for you.”

“I will,” she said with a grim face.

She left, walking with the help of her sticks. In a few moments, she disappeared behind the doors of the car waiting for her outside the coffee shop. And soon the car vanished into the busy serpentine streets of Tallinn.

**PART V**

**Chapter 54**

***4:30 AM 21st April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

TransPacific’s cargo ship was docked at Paljassaare Harbour. Unlike the other harbours in Tallinn, Paljassaare was a much quieter port. The moorage was meant only for cargo vessels, and it had the least traffic. Benedikt had explained the choice of the port to Sanchez, “This harbour was selected by design, as we didn’t want any undue attention.” Paljassaare was on a small peninsula in the northwestern part of the city. The name itself meant ‘Barren Island’, which aptly fitted the description. Besides the scenic view, the place hardly offered any excitement.

Benedikt decided to board the vessel first thing in the morning and advised Sanchez to retire early.  Though she went to bed right after her early supper, she struggled to get any sleep. Throughout the night, she oscillated between spiraling apprehensions, surging hopes, and fretful uncertainties, mostly in anticipation of what the capricious tomorrow might hold. It was a long, anxious wait, which finally came to an end when Benedikt knocked at her door, announcing it was time.

The morning welcomed her with absolute quietude. The darkness was slowly receding, giving way to the morning glow. And the subtle chilliness dissolved in the air with perfect discretion. The fresh morning brought an exhilaration that expunged all her anxieties from the night before.  She sat in the car with renewed fortitude, prepared to attempt the impossible.

It took them thirty minutes to get to the harbour. They parked the car by the docking area. A porter helped them with their luggage, and they walked from the pier to the ship’s gangway. From a distance, she could see a handful of dock workers and a few shipmates busy with their routine work. Unlike what she had thought, the area was sparsely populated.

Sanchez had never been on a ship before. She had always detested travelling on water, and the concept of a cruise ship had never excited her. Today was no different, but she was there for a purpose. The place appeared a little more intimidating than she had thought. And the morning excitement she had felt slowly morphed into a subtle anxiousness. Her mind was crowded with apprehensions. She hoped that her trepidations were transient and that things would soon turn out fine.

As she walked towards the vessel, she got lost observing the large structure standing tall in front of her. It was gigantic and almost overwhelming for her to comprehend. Unlike the larger-than-life pictures of cruise ships she had seen in advertisements, the vessel looked relatively dull. It was jet black, with ‘DAZZLE’ scribbled on the hull, which stood out from its otherwise gloomy appearance.

Benedikt was received at the end of the gangway by the captain of the ship. The captain was waiting at perfect attention along with his men.  It was hard to ignore the clout Benedikt had. She noticed that there was more fear than respect for him. His presence brought a sense of uneasiness, and a hint of discomfort engulfed the otherwise calm milieu. Benedikt instructed one of the shipmates to take Sanchez to her room. He had told her before that on the ship he would act as her boss and that she shouldn’t be surprised by his change in behaviour. As she was about to leave, he said in passing, “I will see you in my office at eight. Alex will be at your cabin door. He will help you find me.”

She followed a good-looking boy who appeared to be in his late teens. They looped around the aisle to a bank of elevators. The elevator took them to the upper deck. Her cabin was a few yards away from the elevator, towards the end of the corridor. Alex opened the door, put her suitcase on the luggage rack and politely stated, “Madam, I will wait for you right outside the door.”

The cabin was a spacious polygonal room, and its interiors were tastefully done, starkly different from the bland look of the vessel. There was a suede couch gracing the front part of the cabin. Away from the settee, on the opposite side, was a study table. The king-sized bed was behind the sofa, covered with white linens and many golden decorative pillows. The far end of the cabin was covered with tall glass doors, and the curtains were drawn halfway, allowing the morning sunlight to seep into the room.

Sanchez walked towards the glass doors. She nudged them gently, and the transparent portal opened to the balcony, giving way to a breathtaking view. She could see the sun rising beyond the edge of the sea. The orange hues were not piercing but soothing to the eyes, and the morning breeze was heavenly as it gently caressed her skin. It was a blissful experience, and she was so engrossed in the moment that she forgot everything that was making her restless. Against her wish, she retraced her steps to the cabin. Benedikt couldn’t have offered her a luxury suite, but she knew he had given her the next best option. The gorgeous view from her room was worth giving up any luxury that a suite might have offered.

**Chapter 55**

***7:30 AM 21st April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

Sanchez took a quick shower and got ready for her first day at work. Besides the fact that she was Benedikt’s assistant, she had no clue what she was required to do. Being on the ship was enthralling enough, and everything else was insignificant.

Alex was standing in an attentive position outside the cabin door. The moment Sanchez stepped out, he greeted her with a polite bow and led the way to Benedikt’s office. The place was a maze and she was glad Alex was there to guide her. Benedikt’s office was located on the upper deck right next to the captain’s cabin.  The office was gigantic, almost spanning the width of the stern. Two guards were posted at the entrance. It seemed they were aware of her arrival, as they opened the door without asking any questions.

Sanchez was surprised by the charming appearance of the office. There was a large table in the centre of the room, crowded with piles of plot papers and different mechanical and electronic instruments. The rear of the cabin tapered into an oval shape. Large French windows covered the back of the room, opening to the balcony. The light hardwood floor was elegantly polished. On the left side of the entrance, there was a spacious Roman-style curved couch, which could easily seat ten. The walls were tastefully decorated with paintings from the medieval period.

She looked around the place with great interest and soon realised that amidst all the beautiful decor, Benedikt was nowhere to be seen. And then she found a door-like structure camouflaged in the side wall. She gently tapped on it to check if it was real.

“Yes,” came the response.

She gave the door a gentle nudge and it opened into a bedroom.

“Sorry, I wasn’t expecting you so early.” He was perplexed and spoke in haste.

Sanchez avoided words and instead responded with a crinkled pair of eyes.

“How is your leg now?” Benedikt tried to take his attention away from her enticing looks.

“I am a lot better now, and I am glad I am off the sticks.” Her eyes were still smiling.

“That’s wonderful to hear. Let us go through the schedule for the next few days. Don’t forget, you have to act and behave like my assistant.” His smile was laced with vulnerability.

Like always, Benedikt looked aristocratic. He was wearing a chequered grey suit with a white shirt that was tightly hugging his well-toned body. The turquoise blue cufflinks stood out in the pale yellow light of the cabin. The charcoal tie contrasted well with the white shirt, and the dark grey boots were shined to perfection. She was lost in his demeanour and failed to grasp his words.

“Do you have something on your mind?” queried Benedikt.

Sanchez blushed and mumbled, “Sorry, I wasn’t paying attention. Did you say something?”

Benedikt was puzzled. He restated his point, only this time without the humour. “I want to make you aware of my schedule.”

“Sure, let us go through it,” she said and scurried to the centre table, trying her best to avoid his gaze.

In the past, he had always been in control of everything that happened around him. Life was simple. Give orders and see them executed. Since he had met her, his life had been in complete disorder. Most of the time he remained unsure how to deal with her. She was capricious, temperamental and at times whimsical. He had never been trained for such circumstances, and her weird behaviour was making him go even more berserk.

He cleared his throat, pushed the laptop towards her, forced a smile and stated, “From now on you will manage my calendar. Tomorrow is a big day. I am meeting key delegates at 10:00 in the morning. It is their cargo we would be carrying and off-loading at various places throughout our voyage.

“The next two days we would be loading the cargo. I have inspection meetings with the head stevedore twice a day. Also, every day I will meet the captain at 8:00 in the morning and in the evening. Before we leave, I am required to personally inspect the load. And I have an important dinner meeting tomorrow. All the arrangements are made. You can find everything in this folder. Make sure you go through the details.”

He pushed the folder towards her and took a pause, giving her time to browse through the plan.

“I see my name in tomorrow’s dinner meeting.” She announced her observation with utmost innocence.

“Can I not have dinner with you?” There was clear sincerity in his voice.

“From now on, direct all your calls to me,” she said, ignoring his question.

Unsure how to react, he curbed his excitement and stated, “There will be a desk here tomorrow. You will occupy the seat for as long as you are on the ship.”

Sanchez pushed the laptop aside, folded her arms across her chest, looked squarely at him and enquired, “So how do I go about finding my father?”

He saw her transform from a perky damsel into a girl exuding deep concern. It was hard for him to comprehend how she could switch context so effortlessly. He was convinced he would never be able to read her mind.

He pulled himself back from the debate he was silently going through and responded, “When I go on my rounds, I will take you with me. Let us see if we can find any clues on the ship first. Do you have any picture of your father with you?”

Sanchez’s eyes were full of hope. She fiddled in her purse and after a few fumbles pulled out her father’s picture. “Here, you can keep this with you.”

Benedikt took the picture from her and gave it a cursory glance. And soon his face turned white. A sudden surge of fear engulfed him, and he felt as if the world was folding in on him. He tried hard to control his emotions but failed. In haste, he looked away from her and stated, “I need to take an important call. Will be right back.”

A few minutes later he announced from his bedroom, “Let us reconvene in a couple of hours. The call is taking longer than I expected.”

**Chapter 56**

***11:00 AM 21st April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

Sanchez came back to her room confused. The frightened look on Benedikt’s face loomed in her mind, refusing to go away despite all efforts, making her restless. The more she got entwined in the labyrinth of her dark thoughts, the more she was convinced Benedikt knew something about her father. Lost in her own world, she sauntered towards the balcony and sat on the armchair, staring at nothingness. The midmorning sun was fighting hard to warm the atmosphere. The occasional sunrays seeping out of the vast spread of clouds were gently warming the cold air. But despite such pleasant weather, she struggled to come out of her gloomy self.

“Have I made a mistake trusting him? What if Maxwell was right?” She doubted every past action.

While she was fighting with her predicament, the phone alarm went off. The screen flashed a reminder note, “Call Stephen.”

It was almost the end of her two-hour forced break. She had a lot to get done before heading out, and calling her brother was the least of her priorities.

She checked the door and made sure the inside security latch was on. She looked around for any hidden cameras in the room but couldn’t find any. Swiftly, she moved her luggage bag to the bathroom, opened the locks and carefully put all her belongings on top of a towel laid on the floor. Once the bag was empty, she located the hidden zip under the bottom surface and removed the cloth covering. There was a black horizontal pouch sitting stealthily between the handles.  She removed the pouch and placed its contents on the powder table.  The contents included a miniature camera and a video recorder in the form of earrings, a small knife, a few extra memory cards, a voice recorder pen and a transmitter.

She checked the functioning of the camera and, once satisfied, put on the earrings. The left earring was the camera and the right was the video recorder. A simple tap toggled each device on and off. She put the voice recorder pen in her pocket, kept the rest of the items back in the pouch and repacked her bag.

It was Maxwell who had helped her with these sleek gadgets. She recalled her last meeting with Stephen and Maxwell and the unceremonious way they had parted. A few minutes after she left, she got a call from Stephen asking her to come back and see them. She remembered how at first she refused but capitulated after Stephen explained the reason. They decided to meet at the same coffee shop where they had convened before. Sanchez spotted them right at the entrance of the café. They took a seat away from the door, secluded from the usual hustle-bustle. Maxwell handed her a small bag, which could easily fit into her handbag. Before she could react, he opened the pouch and showed her the contents. In the beginning, Sanchez was puzzled, but when Maxwell explained the functions, she was impressed. Soon she parted, leaving behind their concerned faces, which made her a tad doubtful for the first time about her bearings and actions.

She looked at her watch and it was time for her to leave. She gave her earrings a final glance and, once convinced, stepped out of her room and locked the door firmly behind her.  Alex was waiting outside the cabin. While it was great to have him the first time, she detested the idea of him hanging around all the time.

“Now I have a fair idea about the ship. You can resume your other responsibilities.” Her tone was definitive.

Alex was puzzled, for he had strict orders from his seniors to escort the lady.

“Madam, this is my only work? Did I offend you by any chance?”

The fretfulness in his voice was evident. She knew there was no point talking to him and decided to take it up with Benedikt instead.

“Forget what I mentioned. Let us go.”

They started down the narrow passage towards the aft of the ship. Throughout the walk, her mind was racing, analysing every motive, every thought. She wanted to confront Benedikt but wasn’t sure if that was a good idea. Her mind was clouded with several questions, but sadly she had answers to none.

**Chapter 57**

***11:00 PM 21st April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

The day progressed without any other noticeable episode.  Her interaction with Benedikt was restricted to a few syllables, making her intuition that something was not right grow stronger. The only achievement of the day was getting Alex to stop guarding her.  Many a time she desired to confront Benedikt and enquire about his changed demeanour. But she couldn’t muster enough courage to approach him. It was an unknown hesitation, something she was unable to comprehend. And to her complete bewilderment, Benedikt was behaving as if nothing had happened. Besides limiting the conversation, he had returned to complete normalcy, hardly exhibiting any shade of discomfort.

Around 10:00 at night, Benedikt called it a day. Exhausted from the day’s commotion, she decided to retire.  She tossed and turned in bed but failed miserably to get any sleep. Frustrated, she decided to call Stephen in the hope of getting respite from her enduring conundrum.

Stephen picked up the phone on the first ring and blurted, “What took you so long?”

Sanchez’s agitation escalated further with her brother’s complaint. She was about to snap but decided otherwise, for fighting with her brother was the last thing on her mind. “I am surrounded by people all the time. I cannot call at my leisure.” Her sarcasm still spilled over despite her attempt to stay calm.

Stephen’s anxiety receded. They spoke for about five minutes. Sanchez promised to call him again the next day and concluded the conversation. Sleep was still defying her. She spent some time checking the videos and the photos she had captured during the day. The recordings and photos came out to her satisfaction. The initial lot was only trials and had no significance. With nothing else to do, she forced herself to sleep again. This time she tried distracting her mind by recalling the good old times. It seemed to work, and soon she dozed off.

**Chapter 58**

***7:30 AM 22nd April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

It was a new day and a new beginning. A sense of determination embraced her being. Sleeping on her anxiety was all that was needed to get her mojo back. It was an important day for both of them. While Benedikt had a meeting with the Russians, Sanchez was aiming to get the meeting secretly recorded. She had also decided to confront Benedikt about the previous day’s episode, but that she planned for later in the evening.

She was at her desk sharp at 7:30 AM, thirty minutes before her reporting time. Benedikt’s meeting was not scheduled until 10:00 AM. He too was in the office checking some final reports. He wasn’t expecting Sanchez so early but was pleased to see her and couldn’t conceal the smile on his face.

He was extremely secretive about the meeting. Sanchez had no access to the reports or the documents pertaining to the meeting. Her purpose in coming early was to find a way to be a part of it, and his presence in the office wasn’t helping her by any means. She was pulled out of her brooding by the intruding voice of Benedikt. “You are quite early. If you wish, you can come back in an hour.”

Sanchez wasn’t ready for that. With some struggle, she regained her composure and put forward a question, “Am I disturbing you?”

“Hell, no. You are welcome to stay.” He gave a sheepish smile.

Sanchez took advantage of his softened manner and continued, “Can I help you with anything?”

“Thanks. I am fine for now.” And he got busy looking through some papers.

Her attempt to get involved was politely put down by him. She was at her wits’ end when his sharp voice jolted her.

“Can you check the main conference room? Make sure everything is in order.”

“Sure.”

She beamed with renewed hope and headed straight to the conference room. The guards watching​ the area were puzzled to see her. She explained the reason for her visit and requested them to open the room. The guards scanned her, and once satisfied, they let her in.

The room was a spacious hall. There was a large rectangular table in the centre of the room, which could easily seat twenty people. She called upon the technical executive and got the AV and video checked. The chief steward was called next to check the food arrangements. After she was satisfied with the layout and amenities, she settled down to execute her plan.

In the far left corner, there was a side table, ​holding the AV equipment. She placed the pen, which was a stealth voice recorder, below the AV frame hinge, where it blended perfectly with the colour of the table. As she was checking the position of the pen, Benedikt’s voice startled her. He was standing right next to her. Her face turned pale as if she had been caught red-handed.

“What is going on?”

With every passing moment, Sanchez was becoming more fidgety, as if she had fallen into an abyss. Before she could collect her thoughts, he continued, “I have some work for you. Are you done checking things here?”

Those words fell on her ears like blessings. Though she was relieved​, she realised​ her flustered state might put her back in danger.

“Everything is fine. I have done a thorough check,” she blurted.

“Good. Let us head back to the office. I need some help.” He left the room promptly.

Sanchez was drenched in sweat. Fortunately, the conference room light was dimmed, which worked to her advantage. She headed to the restroom, washed her face and went straight to the office. As she was making her way back, only one concern crowded her mind, how she would get access to the room before the meeting. In the entire hullabaloo, she forgot to​ turn the recorder on.

**Chapter 59**

***8:30 AM 22nd April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

Benedikt was furious. He couldn’t come to terms with his incredulous gasp when Sanchez flashed the photograph. Sure, it was shocking enough to find out who her father was, but the fact that he had exposed his vulnerable side to her made him frantic. For him, it remained an unpardonable offence, and all his attempts to divert his mind from the unfortunate proceedings failed miserably. Though deep down he was aware he had recovered from the situation, he wasn’t naïve enough to ignore the perfect storm brewing inside Sanchez’s mind. And he knew sooner or later it would explode on him. To stop the situation from deteriorating further, he had tried making a few calls but got nothing concrete to help him frame his strategy towards tracing Sanchez’s father.

His meeting was in a few hours and he had many unfinished jobs to complete to make it a success. Knowing he couldn’t afford to muddle it, he forced his attention back to work. He was not too long into it when he saw Sanchez walking back into the office.  
He was a tad impatient, and to avoid exposing his nervousness, he said, “I need some help. Can you help me tally a few accounts for my meeting today?”

“Sure, no problem. I am good at accounting.” And she grabbed the file from his outstretched hands.

She couldn’t believe she had got her hands on the file she had been eyeing since morning. “Maybe he was inadvertently keeping it away from me,” she concluded as she settled at the desk to work on it.  
She opened the file and started flipping through it, and what she found left her agape. The information was irrefutable. She knew right at that moment that the file could be used against TransPacific in a court of law and could be an excellent source to bring the racket down. She had to get the file out of the room, but with Benedikt sitting next to her, it was almost impossible to execute any plan. She kept brooding for a while but failed to formulate any meaningful strategy. And when she was convinced nothing could be done, she pulled herself back from her spiraling thoughts to focus on her work.  
She had been at it for over twenty minutes when Benedikt decided to leave the room to attend a phone call. She was so engrossed in her work that she failed to notice him stepping out. It took her another ten minutes to wrap up the work, and when she finally took her attention away from the file, Benedikt was nowhere to be seen. For a moment she was dazed, but as the reality sank in, a frisson of hope swept her being.

She took a few deep breaths to calm herself. After she had her nerves under control, she gave a hurried glance around the room to make sure no one was watching her. Once convinced, she took the camera out of her purse. The camera was a piece of art and was small enough to hide in her fist. She hastily placed it inside the file, and with extreme caution started taking pictures of the pages.

After she was done taking the last snapshot, she tried rearranging the pages, and in her haste, the file slipped from her hand. She leaned forward to catch it midway, but to her horror, she lost her grip on the camera. Both the camera and the file came crashing to the wooden floor with a thumping sound.  There were scattered pages all around the room.

Hearing the noise, Benedikt stepped inside and saw papers all over the floor and Sanchez hastily trying to put them together.

Watching Benedikt enter the room, she froze and her mind went numb. All hell broke loose. She was flirting in the danger zone of getting caught in her misdeed. She recovered quickly from the situation, and before Benedikt could offer any help, she pushed the camera under a small heap of paper.  
Benedikt was still on the phone. He put the phone on hold and enquired, “Is everything ok?”

She tried hard to conceal her anxiety and blurted, “I accidentally dropped the file. I am sorry.”

“Let me help you.” He bent down to collect the dispersed papers.  
It was happening too fast, too soon. Benedikt was approaching the stack of papers where the camera was stashed. Not sure what to do next, she swept other scattered papers towards the stack.

Benedikt collected his share, put it on top of Sanchez’s pile, and before she could react, he balanced the phone between his ear and shoulder and lifted the entire stack to place it on the table. As luck would have it, the camera slid through the pile and fell to the floor. Benedikt saw the device flying out. He stretched his hand to grab it, and in that act the phone slipped from his shoulder and crashed on the floor.  
Benedikt’s entire attention was diverted to the phone. Taking advantage of the situation, like a hawk stooping towards its prey, she leaped towards the camera. She promptly picked it up and pushed it into her jeans pocket. Benedikt was too immersed in checking the phone. Once he found it working, he stepped out of the room to continue with his conversation. Sanchez had narrowly escaped a disastrous mishap. Perspiration covered her from head to toe, and she knew she wouldn’t have survived if Benedikt had got hold of the camera.

After she regained her senses, she arranged the file neatly on the table, rechecked her work, and put her final report on the table for review. Her only hope was that Benedikt would have forgotten about the strange-looking device, and there would be no questions raised about its existence in the future.

**Chapter 60**

***9:45 AM 22nd April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

The delegates’ arrival time was fast approaching. Benedikt went through the file one final time. Once satisfied with the workings, he said, “I have to go down to receive the visitors. Would you mind keeping the documents in the conference room?”

Since she left the conference room, her mind had been plotting innumerable strategies to find a way to be in the room again. Never once had she assumed it would come to her so easily. She looked away from him to conceal her excitement, grabbed the file and disappeared as fast as she humanly could.

The conference room was one deck up. Sanchez was walking with urgency. As she turned the corner, she saw the guards outside the room, standing in their usual vigilant posture. She pretended to ignore their presence and went past them to open the door.

“Sorry, miss, no one can go in.” The guard’s voice was firm as one of them raised his hand to block her from going any further.

“Benedikt wants the files on the table for the meeting. May I?” she asked with genuine urgency.

“You stay here. I will accompany her inside,” the guard declared and opened the door.

The guard stood at the entrance of the meeting room, keeping a close eye on her. She moved around, pretending to check the functioning of the gadgets. While she was next to the AV system, she swiftly reached for the recorder pen hidden behind the frame hinge and turned the switch on. Satisfied with her work, she thanked the guard and left the room. The recorder could run for a couple of hours. Her hope was to get enough evidence to bring TransPacific’s illegal trafficking to a final halt.

The visitors arrived in an entourage of stretch limousines. There were four chauffeur-driven executive cars with dark glasses, exuding an aura that meant serious business. A team of well-built escorts leaped out of the vehicles and surrounded the area, quickly forming a shielded lane.

The delegates, dressed in dark suits, came out of the cars and walked towards the gangway. Benedikt was standing at the entrance. He greeted each one of them personally and led them to the elevators, which would take them directly to the conference room.

The meeting lasted for an hour. While it was in progress, no one was allowed to enter the room, not even the chief steward, who was allowed to serve the guests only after the meeting concluded.

They left the ship at a quarter past one, with brisk steps and in complete discretion, as they had arrived. Soon after, Benedikt appeared in the office with a broad grin, suggesting that the meeting had gone according to plan.

“In one hour, we should go and check the stevedores. Also, if you could check the payment file once, that would be a big help. I need to clear their dues. We will be sailing soon, and I want nothing left for tomorrow.”

Sanchez’s mind was working hard to find a way to be in the conference room again. She made a faint attempt. “I am not able to locate my notebook. Have you seen it in the conference room? Maybe I left it there by accident.”

“I haven’t seen any. Why don’t you go and look for yourself?” His tone was overtly casual.

The trick worked. The guards were still there and gave Sanchez a confused look. She told them about her lost notebook. They were hardly bothered now that the delegates were gone, and opened the door without any hesitation. She leaped forward, grabbed the pen, switched the recorder off and left the meeting room promptly.

**Chapter 61**

***3:00 PM 22nd April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

The visit to the loading dock was uneventful. Everything was happening as per the schedule. For Sanchez, it was frustrating, to say the least. There was no sign of her father’s existence, and she had no concrete plan to initiate any enquiries with the people on the ship. With Benedikt’s suspicious behaviour, her inability to quiz the workers and no plan for going forward, she was like a boat without an anchor, drifting to oblivion.

It was around 6:00 PM when Benedikt announced it was time to head out for dinner. They had plenty of work to wrap up before the voyage could commence, and the decision was made to conclude the unfinished work after dinner. The chauffeur was waiting right outside the gangway. It was a short fifteen-minute drive. The restaurant was in an upscale neighborhood, crowded with Lamborghinis and Maseratis. The streets were cobbled, decorative 18th-century streetlights stood tall, and the atmosphere had a distinct aura of richness, well demonstrated by the affluent people who graced the streets.

The place was an authentic Lebanese restaurant with an ambiance from medieval times. It was designed like an old castle, with statues depicting sword fighting and jousting. Benedikt, like always, took control of the menu and ordered the drinks and the food. With great interest, he described the place and the delicacies it offered, but it all fell on deaf ears. Her mind was searching for the right moment or the right words to heave away her burden.

It didn’t take him long to realise she wasn’t with him. The reason for her indifferent posture was not unknown to him, and deep inside he was hoping that his presumptions were wrong. While he was tempted to inquire about her pensive look, he hesitated, as he wasn’t sure where it might lead.

Sanchez decided to break the silence. She didn’t mince her words as she asked, “Did you know my dad?”

Benedikt was taken off-guard. And after a long, uncomfortable pause, he responded, “No, why do you ask?”

“Your face turned pale when I showed you his picture. Listen, even if it is horrible, please tell me. You know I have been on this never-ending hunt for years now. Please help me.”  Her voice trailed with a clear shade of dejection.

For a moment, he was lost. He had no clue how to react. The last thing he wanted was to upset her. She was too important in his life, and it was hard for him to see her in such a miserable state.

“I never knew your father, but I have seen him once.” The words slipped out, and it was never in his plan to reveal any details.

He took a pause to see how Sanchez was taking it. She had anxiety in her eyes and restlessness in her appearance. He knew any more silence would drive her to insanity. Hurriedly, he continued, “It wasn’t in my control. He was caught in Belarus, and I happened to be there on a regular business trip. That’s all I know.”

Benedikt couldn’t face Sanchez, and in an attempt to avoid her constant gaze, he looked away. Sanchez was barely holding herself together.

“Is he alive?” Her voice trembled with fear.

“We are in an illicit trade, but we are not murderers. Not that I want to give you any false hope, but I firmly believe he is alive.” His voice reflected uncertainty.

Sanchez wasn’t convinced, but she knew her options were limited. She stopped quizzing Benedikt. She had lost her appetite. After a quiet dinner, they headed back to the ship.

Benedikt knew he had temporarily weathered the storm. What he mentioned was partly true, and he feared facing her on this topic again. He advised Sanchez to take some rest, but she refused. They worked until midnight and wrapped up the left-over work.

Sanchez had only one thing on her mind, to take charge of the situation. She had a rough sketch of a plan in her head and waited until the next morning to execute it.

**Chapter 62**

***4:00 AM 23rd April 2014, Tallinn, Estonia***

Sanchez couldn’t sleep well. She spent most of her time replaying the plan in her mind. The more she weighed the options, the more flaws she found, making her restless and exasperated. Perturbed, she somehow managed to find some sleep.

The ship’s loud whistle woke Sanchez from her brief repose. The vessel departed from Tallinn, making its onward day-long journey to Ventspils. Sanchez slipped out of bed, splashed some water on her face, dressed casually and hurried towards the loading station.

It was a quiet morning. The loading dock was missing the hullabaloo of the past few days. She approached the entrance with discreet steps.  Two mariners armed with sophisticated weapons guarded the place. They were curious to see a lady at such an ungodly hour, but they weren’t alarmed, for they had seen her frequenting the area with Benedikt before. “I am here to see the head stevedore on an important matter.” She was not sure he was there.

“Sure, he is at his desk,” came the reply, and she was let inside the cargo area.

She knew this was the easy part. The hard part was to convince Finch, the head stevedore, to speak.

Finch looked tired. The commotion from the last few days had worn him down. He was going through a file when a gentle tap on the door interrupted him.  He looked at her with a stern stare. He certainly recognised her but pretended otherwise, unsure of the purpose of her visit.

Sanchez started gently, “Would you have a few minutes for me?”

“What is it about?” His voice reflected clear hesitation.

Sanchez stepped inside his office, pulled her father’s picture from the file she was holding and asked, “Do you know this person?”

She looked straight at him and waited for his response. Finch briefly looked at the picture and immediately took his eyes off it.  “Please leave,” he said with a flat tone.

Sanchez wasn’t surprised. She was expecting a response along the same lines.

“I mean no harm, and whatever you say will be off the record. Please, any bit of information would be a big help.” She maintained her composure.

By now Finch had lost his patience. He was almost screaming. He threatened her with dire consequences if she refused to leave.

Sanchez had no option but to leave his office. There was a strong possibility of Finch complaining to Benedikt, but that was the least of her worries.

Though she had made no progress, one thing was certain. Finch knew her father. It was still dark, and except for the sailors, the entire ship was in deep slumber. Sanchez’s tiredness had surprisingly vanished. She turned her computer on and carefully downloaded all the images and videos from her gadgets. She was curious to learn what had transpired in the meeting room. She put on her earpiece and played the audio recording from the conference room. They were mostly discussing the loads, money and some future deals. She fast-forwarded the audio to hear anything that could lead her to her father. Except for being good evidence to trap the rogues, the audio offered nothing significant.

Frustrated, she decided to catch a few hours of sleep. She had to rest well to be ready for Latvia.

**Chapter 63**

***10:00 AM 23rd April 2014, Baltic Sea***

Benedikt’s aggravation was extreme. Ever since Sanchez had boarded the ship, it had been nothing but a series of mishaps. It was never part of his plan to stir her antsy emotions, and the more he tried mending the situation, the worse it became. He assumed staying close to Sanchez would help her know him better. His yearning for her was growing by the day, and his inability to approach her was breaking him apart. He was convinced that the only way he could restore her lost trust was by finding her father, but it wasn’t an easy task. A small inquiry could alarm the executives in TransPacific, which would do more harm than good. He had to execute his investigation with utmost secrecy, and he still hadn’t figured out his way forward. There was no one in his circle he could trust to further his probe, and all his options appeared weak and flawed.  And above all, the fear of Sanchez coming to know what he knew about her father was unsettling him.

As he dwelled on this predicament, the screaming siren broke his quiet brooding. He picked up his hard hat and rushed outside to find the reason behind the alarm. He saw a swarming crowd rushing aft. Upon enquiring, he learned one of the workers had been hurt. Once assured that there was no danger to the ship, he retraced his steps to his cabin.

There was a commotion near the ship’s stern. A doctor was attending to an injured worker who lay unconscious on a wooden shaft. From a distance, Sanchez could see the person was bleeding profusely. She was baffled to see the packed crowd suffocating the already distressed worker. She pushed people aside and maneuvered her way to the open shaft where the doctor was attending to him. Once she was close to the spot, she turned around and screamed at the top of her lungs, “Guys, you need to let the doctor do his job. Give him some space, please.”

That announcement worked, and people started dispersing. There were five of them left, the doctor, the injured worker, Finch, Sanchez, and the second officer. The worker appeared to be in bad shape. His left leg looked almost smashed. No one knew how it had happened, but it appeared one of the loads had accidentally hit him. The doctor ordered Finch to get him to the onboard dispensary. Finch sprang into action, and with the help of his fellowmen, carried the injured worker on a stretcher to the doctor’s office.

As they were walking, the second officer broke the silence, “How serious is it?”

“It looks bad. If we don’t get him to a hospital, he might lose his leg. Can we turn the ship back to the port? I can hold him for a couple of hours at the most,” the doctor stated with a hopeful plea.

The officer was puzzled. “I am not sure. This is the captain’s call.” And he scurried away to check with the captain.

Sanchez knew the answer wouldn’t be favorable. The dates were fixed, and there was an army of people lined up for the delivery of the goods. Any change in date would be a mammoth undertaking, which she was sure the captain wouldn’t do for a worker. Finch looked concerned, for his views were not different from hers.

The second officer came back in a few minutes, and sure enough, he had disappointing news. The ship couldn’t be turned around. It was evident that the chances of restoring his limb were next to none. Finch was frustrated, and so was the doctor.

Sanchez left the dispensary with a purpose and barged into Benedikt’s cabin without any warning. He was on the phone. He glanced at her, and from her look, he knew something was serious.

“You need to turn around the ship. Otherwise, the worker will die,” blurted Sanchez.

By now Benedikt was aware of the situation. “Some things are beyond my control. I am sorry for him, but I cannot turn the ship,” he said with a flat voice.

“Listen, if that worker dies, I will never forgive you.” Sanchez was hysterical, and she stormed out of the room without waiting for his response.

The heavy sedation had put the worker temporarily in a numb state. Though the flow of blood had receded, he was still in deep danger. “He has lost a lot of blood. I need a few volunteers to donate,” the doctor announced.

“Can he survive until evening?” Latvia was still ten hours away, and Finch looked worried.

“I doubt it. He needs urgent medical attention.”

A deep-throated voice broke into their conversation. It was Benedikt, who appeared from nowhere. “I have arranged for a medical helicopter. It should be here any moment. Take him to the top deck,” he announced.

Everyone in the room was pleasantly surprised. The doctor took the lead and thanked Benedikt for being considerate, followed by Finch, who looked genuinely moved by the unexpected gesture.

“Don’t thank me. Thank the lady. She made this happen.” He left the place as quietly as he had arrived.

Finch regretted the way he had behaved with her earlier. He wished he had been a little more considerate. He thanked her in a sheepish manner and turned to talk to the doctor to avoid any further conversation, mostly out of embarrassment.

The worker was flown out, and people went back to their work. Sanchez had a few unfinished jobs to conclude, and lost in her world, she sauntered towards Benedikt’s office.

**Chapter 64**

***10:00 AM 23rd April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia***

The fact that Sanchez was on her own, the danger that might be lurking around her every moment and the undeniable truth that Benedikt was with her all the time were making Maxwell extremely uncomfortable. It had been two days since she boarded the ship and she had called Stephen only once. Although she had confirmed her safety, Maxwell wasn’t convinced. Sanchez had put her trust in Benedikt, and he knew Benedikt wasn’t reliable.

He was going through the map, circling Gdynia repeatedly. “From here they should travel by road,” he said to himself. And after brooding for long, he was clear about what he wanted to do. Stephen was in the kitchen fixing lunch. He raised his voice so that Stephen could hear him. “We need to leave this place right now.”

Stephen came into the room wearing a confused look. “Where are we heading? Didn’t we decide to stay in Latvia until they arrive?”

“We need to be in Gdynia. That’s where the voyage ends. We don’t know where in Warsaw they will be. And the only way to find out is by following them. I want to be there before their arrival to plan everything. And chasing cars is not easy. Trust me on that.”

They packed their belongings in haste, left the half-cooked lunch and soon they were on their way to Gdynia.

**Chapter 65**

***11:30 AM 23rd April 2014, Baltic Sea***

The door to Benedikt’s suite was wide open. Sanchez appeared exhausted from the morning ordeal, and though she detested the idea of meeting him, she somehow dragged herself into his executive cabin. There was not a soul around, and the quietness was giving the surroundings an eerie stupor.

She was hesitant at first but soon decided to knock on the bedroom door. She tapped a few times, but there was no response. Disappointed, she came back to her desk and got busy going through some random files. She was lost in her thoughts when she heard the distinct guttural voice of Benedikt. He was standing right in front of her, looking at her with great interest.

“I truly appreciate what you did. Thank you.” She took a brief pause and continued, “And I am sorry for the way I behaved.” She took her eyes away from him, half embarrassed and half nervous.  And from the corner of her eye, she saw him smile, making her extremely uncomfortable. How she wished to escape from the place, but she couldn’t bolt away from her labyrinth of thoughts.

“You know, you are changing me as a person. I hardly remember when I did any good in the past. I don’t know if it is a change for the better or for the worse, but I want to thank you for discovering a new me.”

He knew Sanchez was not comfortable talking about him, and seeing her anxious, he changed the topic. “We have a few hours to Latvia. Why don’t you take some rest?”

Sanchez was looking for a way out of the conversation and was relieved to find her escape path. She was battling competing thoughts, posing questions she had no answers to, leaving her worn out and jaded. And with a lost soul and a confused mind, she walked back to her cabin.

**Chapter 66**

***8:00 PM 23rd April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia***

It was late in the evening when the captain announced it would take an hour for the ship to dock at Ventspils.  Sanchez got out of the armchair and pushed herself out of her futile brooding. Before heading out, she had one job to finish. She made sure the door latches were on and the path to the balcony was guarded. Once assured, she connected her satellite phone to the laptop and tried using it as an Internet gateway. After a few attempts, she succeeded. She uploaded all the videos and pictures from her camera to her private account. The speed was painfully slow, and it took her longer than she had expected. She checked the uploaded files, and once convinced, she deleted them permanently from her camera. She threw herself on the bed and a sense of quiescence reclaimed her.  For the first time in a while, she felt liberated.  A sharp knock on the door jolted her from her reverie. She hastily enquired, “Who is it?”

“This is Alex. Chief wants to see you.”

“Tell him I will be there in five minutes.”

Benedikt was busy going through some reports. He had a few questions about some documents, which she answered, and after that she had nothing else to do. While Benedikt was busy with his work and calls, Sanchez kept her mind occupied rehearsing the plan she wanted to execute in Latvia. Once done with his work, he glanced towards Sanchez and found her scribbling aimlessly with great focus. While he kept looking at her with interest, she hardly noticed him and remained lost in her rumination.

He reflected on the past few days’ proceedings and realised how unfair he had been all this while. He had promised to help her find her father, but to date, he hadn’t told her what he was going to do about it. Besides, he had confused her more with his erratic demeanour, particularly his unwarranted reaction on seeing her father’s picture. To add to her misery, he had made her work relentlessly, and never once had she complained.

He came out of his brooding and said in a soft tone, “Sanchez, go and take some rest. Let us resume tomorrow morning. Hopefully, Latvia can provide us with some leads to trace your father.”

She was still busy scribbling, paying no attention to his words. After a long, uncomfortable pause, he meekly continued, “I, unfortunately, cannot go around enquiring about your father. It can put everyone in danger.” More than him, it was his guilt talking.

Sanchez was still drawing some random pictures, and without taking her focus away from the notepad, she said, “By everyone you mean you.” She gave him a hard look. “Isn’t that correct?”

Benedikt’s ego was ruffled. His face turned pale. Though he somehow managed to control his anger, he couldn’t refrain from subtle sarcasm. “I hope you are intelligent enough to know that if by any stroke of luck your father is still alive, any snooping around can put his life in deep danger.”

He knew Sanchez must be wild by now, but he couldn’t stop himself from stating the facts. Sanchez chose to remain silent, and soon she excused herself and stormed out of the room.

As she walked away, tears started rolling down her cheeks. She hurried off, for she didn’t want to show her vulnerable side. The last thing she wanted was his sympathy.

Every single time Benedikt tried to correct things, he failed. His uncouth approach and intense anger were making Sanchez slip further away from him. He rushed out to the deck to find some relief. The gelid air was acting like a natural healer, and it instantly calmed his perturbations. He started enjoying the dark night, the cold air and the aloofness. Despite all the recent proceedings, a secret smile crossed his face. The clouded apprehensions faded away at that moment. He realised the barriers he had experienced all this while were nothing but an illusion created by his errant heart. He now had a purpose. For once he was certain and he knew what he had to do to mend things.

**Chapter 67**

***8:00 AM 24th April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia***

Sitting alone in her cabin, lost in her world, she made an earnest attempt to decipher the state of her mind. It was a strange emotion, a subdued pain that kept resurfacing without any visible explanation. She had never felt so vulnerable before. A lot had to be accomplished in Latvia, and she had neither the time nor any good reason to be miserable. She forced her mind to focus on the day’s proceedings, and as the possibilities crowded her thoughts, her despondent self slowly faded away. With the hope of a better tomorrow, she drifted off to sleep.

It was a loud cacophony, a blend of innumerable voices all trying hard to explain something with urgency. She tried to focus but failed miserably. Then she saw them appearing one by one, morphing their way into her cabin through the open door. Some were aghast, and the others were too eager to lay their hands on her. The voices were becoming unbearable. With all her might she tried to escape, but her body refused to follow her mind, and she helplessly watched those ghost-like creatures moving towards her with unprecedented angst.

One of them slowly morphed into a distinct figure, and out of the shallow darkness, she could see the hazy shadow of Benedikt. She craned her neck to get a good look but failed. Benedikt’s hand was slowly extending towards her. She gave one final push and managed to find her lost voice. She screamed and jumped out of her constrained state, perspiring profusely from head to toe.  Behind the heavy curtains the room was pitch dark and she couldn’t see any of those disturbing souls anymore.  The voices also had surprisingly receded.

It took her a few minutes to adjust to the darkness and to ascertain that she had had a bad dream. She turned the lights on and checked the time. It was 8:00 AM. She was relieved, and a wry smile crossed her lips.

“I have to stop overthinking,” she promised herself and put her mind to replaying the plan she had for the day.

Going to the warehouse with Benedikt was the first thing on her list. Her hope was to find someone who might have seen her father, someone who might have been his friend. She knew her plan was vague, but she couldn’t figure out any better alternative. Benedikt could have been a great help, but after the previous evening’s episode, she was averse to taking any help from him.

“Why does my heart break when he talks harshly? Why am I bothered when he misbehaves?” She tried reasoning but couldn’t arrive at any conclusive answer.

“Am I avoiding the truth? Am I in love?” Those competing thoughts rattled her, and she knew no matter how vehemently she refused to accept it, she was in love!

“What have I done?” she mumbled. She walked to the bathroom, splashed some cold water on her face, stared at the mirror and said to herself in a quiet but firm voice, “You are here for a mission. You don’t have time to fall in love. Find your dad and get the hell out of this place.”

With renewed energy and determination, she headed back to the office.

Benedikt was at his desk, busy going through some files. He was as charming as ever. The rugged look, the unkempt hair and the infectious smile made all her fortitude slip into oblivion. Before she could recover, he announced in a husky tone, “I am heading to the warehouse. Would you like to join me?”

She was spellbound. It appeared he had read her mind. All she could manage was to continue staring at him with awe.

“I know the warehouse is no fun, but here is what I can do to make it interesting. Right after our visit, we can check out the beautiful Market Square.”

Her mind was running in many directions, and she failed to offer a response for a while. A little embarrassed, she responded, “Sure. When do we leave?”

“Well, the unloading will take another few hours. Around 11:00?”

“Sure, that works.” She appeared much relieved, for she had found a way out of her mortified brooding.

“So, I will see you at 11:00 by the gangway.”

She reciprocated with a small smile and headed out to her cabin more confused than before.

**Chapter 68**

***10:30 AM 24th April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia***

The dark clouds looming over the rough sea made the port city look exquisite. A strong gust of wind was moving at a fast pace, appearing unruly, for it dared to take everything away with it. Sanchez was by the gangway well before time, and she hardly regretted her decision. Ventspils port was breathtakingly charming. Despite the large concrete structure that draped the port, it had a distinct aura of its own, and there was something in it that reminded her of Ayr.

“Madam, I need to have a word with you.” The disembodied voice almost made her jump. She turned around and found Finch standing by the edge of the hatch right where the gangway ended. He looked edgy, and when he got Sanchez’s attention, he turned and started walking towards the stern of the ship, far away from the crowd.

Sanchez was confused but decided to follow him anyway. She tried keeping some distance to ensure no one noticed them. After a few minutes’ walk, he slipped into a hidden corner by the end of the deck. Sanchez followed, and soon she was standing across from him.

In a hushed tone, Finch stated, “I know him. He used to work on this ship. One day he and a friend of mine, Yuri Avilov, moved to Vitebsk. They were good friends. I have not been in touch with Yuri for a long time. And he is the only person who can help you find Daniel.”

This was the first substantial lead she had got hold of in a long time. Before she could react, Finch continued in the same urgent tone, “Take this letter. Tell him it’s from me. He won’t refuse to help.”

Sanchez was all teary as she thanked him. He smiled and uttered the most genuine words he could muster, “You are a good person. Take care of yourself.” And he left.

It would be another five days before they arrived at Vitebsk and she knew these five days would be excruciatingly long.

**Chapter 69**

***11:00 AM 25th April 2014, Ventspils, Latvia***

Sanchez’s stay at Ventspils was laced with frustration. The anxiety of what the future would unfold kept her in an uneasy disposition all the time. She was engulfed in a frisson of fear, something she knew she could only put to rest after meeting Yuri.

While she was at the warehouse, she managed to get some valuable evidence, but nothing that could provide any substantial lead to find her father.

Benedikt tried hard to lift her spirits. They visited the beautiful Market Square, ate at an upscale restaurant, and he took every opportunity to humour her, but nothing worked.  The only time he found her engaged was at the Baltica Concert. However, it was short-lived. She returned to her despondent state the moment they stepped out of the hall.

He had so far failed to get any information about her father. The trusted sources he had called had no clue, and rightly so, for Daniel’s disappearance was an extremely hushed affair. He knew a call to the higher-ups would give him all the answers, but he was unable to find a good reason to justify the probing. He knew that once they doubted his intention he would be scrutinised, and that might expose Sanchez, a risk he was not ready to take at any cost. He could take the chance of calling his higher-ups only when he was convinced of her well-being, and her safety could only be assured once she left the ship. Convincing her to leave was the only resort he was left with. Though he knew it would be a tough conversation, he decided to confront her one last time.

It was the evening of their scheduled departure to Gdynia. They were sitting in the office taking a final look at the work when Benedikt announced a short break. Sanchez was about to leave the office when Benedikt asked her to accompany him for a stroll on the deck. With some hesitation, she agreed to come along.

It was a gorgeous evening. The gust of wind made the temperature drop, but not enough to make it unpleasant. They kept walking for a while without uttering a word. After a few minutes of uneasy silence, Benedikt said, “I have a request to make.”

“What is it about?”

Sanchez appeared lost, sensing something untoward.

“You need to first agree that you will give me a fair hearing and react rationally, not emotionally.”

Sanchez was anxious to know what Benedikt had to say. “Sure, I’m listening.”

“I have been contemplating for the past few days how to find your father. Yesterday, I spoke to some of my trusted contacts, but no one knows anything about him. After a lot of deliberation, I am left with only one option, calling the top executives.”

Sanchez was listening with great interest, and she waited for him to make his point.

“They are dangerous people. If I go and inquire about Daniel, they will come after me. They will scrutinise me. Eventually, they will come to know about you. And once they find out your real identity, you won’t be spared.”

She didn’t react and patiently waited for him to conclude.

Seeing her engrossed in the conversation, he continued, “I want you to leave the ship. I care a lot about you and I cannot knowingly put you in any danger. I promise I will do everything required to find your father. Trust me, I won’t fail you.”

“You are hiding something from me. Tell me the truth. What do you know about my father?” Sanchez struggled to calm her nerves, and it was evident in her fidgety manner.

“Trust me, I don’t know anything about him beyond what I have told you before.”  His words were laced with uncertainty, and he struggled to speak with conviction.

Sanchez couldn’t maintain her stoicism anymore and said in a raised voice, “How do you expect me to trust you? You have told me you don’t know my dad, so how do you know his name? Am I wrong in assuming we have a connection, a bond that is built on mutual trust and respect? My father means everything to me, and by hiding things you are breaking that special thing that exists between us.”

Benedikt wasn’t sure how to react. His voice was shaking as he continued, “You know you are special to me. In the last few days I have done things that I have never done in my entire lifetime. Trust me, I don’t know where your father is. I don’t want trouble for you. That is the only reason I am requesting you to leave.”

Sanchez was aware that he was hiding something about her father, but she also knew that he genuinely wanted to help her. She couldn’t leave the ship until she had met Yuri. She was in a quandary, for she didn’t want him to know what had transpired between her and Finch.

“If you truly respect me, then let me stay. I want to personally see every location. Let me stay until the end of this trip. If I still fail to find him, I will do as you say.” She demonstrated clear hesitation as she uttered those words.

After a long pause, Benedikt continued, “Alright, if this is what you want. I will not insist that you leave. But I will not go and actively look for your father until I am sure you are safe.”

Sanchez saw how dejected he looked. She struggled hard to comprehend why she became so perturbed whenever it concerned Benedikt, particularly when she was aware he was hiding something about her father.

How she wished she had a glimpse of what might be transpiring in his mind, and with great reluctance, she left for her cabin.

**Chapter 70**

***11:00 AM 26th April 2014, Gdynia, Poland***

The port at Gdynia was one busy place. Maxwell had an old contact who worked in the local port authority. With his help, he got the arrival information of Benedikt’s ship. With no concrete plan in hand, waiting for the ship was the only viable option left for them. They chose a small coffee joint right outside the harbour to kill time.

Their last conversation with Sanchez barely gave them any insight into her plans.  They had no idea how she was planning to get any information about her father from Benedikt. Maxwell was unable to comprehend Benedikt’s motive. He was unclear about what kind of business deal she could have with him. He was struggling to find any answer to pacify his intrusive mind. Every time he pondered, he was left with resentment and discomfort. He had a faint inkling that Benedikt might be in love with Sanchez, something he tried not to dwell on, but it kept resurfacing. The more he contemplated disposing of the idea, the more forcefully it revisited him.

The phone rang loudly, breaking into the contemplations he had been battling with for the past few hours. He looked at the phone and saw his friend’s name on the display. It was time for action. The ship was about to arrive.

**Chapter 71**

***3:00 PM 26th April 2014, Gdynia, Poland***

During her stay at Gdynia, Sanchez discovered a disturbing fact about the goods the ship was carrying. They were trafficking items of archeological importance. She learned about it by accident during one of the inspection tours she took along with Benedikt. The wrapper of one of the boxes was ripped, and gold-plated statues of some historical significance were dangling out. Using her stealth body camera, she took a good number of pictures. Later, when she checked them in private, she found that they resembled the remains of a second-century BC temple from Vietnam, something she had learned about during her college days in Ayr. It was a substantial finding, and she was certain that this, along with the other evidence she had gathered, could easily help to bring down TransPacific. She carefully stored the pictures in her online account and cleared every trace of their existence from the camera.

The unloading of the cargo was concluded late in the evening, and they were on schedule. The next morning, they were leaving for Warsaw. “Three more days before I arrive in Vitebsk,” she mumbled as she lay restless on the bed, staring at infinity. An unknown fear was looming over her mind. She tried hard to distract herself, but it kept revisiting her. She kept tossing and turning for hours before succeeding in getting some respite from her overthinking mind.

The shimmering morning hue found its way through the open edges of the window curtain and filled the room with its glare. She woke up a tad tired from her night-long brooding. The cold shower helped her to rejuvenate and brace herself for the day. The drive to Warsaw was smooth. The traffic was light, and they took less than the anticipated time to reach the hotel. Benedikt had a few meetings organised for the afternoon. On that pretext, Sanchez excused herself to spend some time visiting downtown.

The weather was beautiful, and the temperature was soothing, making her stroll enjoyable. She traversed the cobbled streets in no defined order, experiencing the architectural brilliance the city offered. From the sprawled gothic churches and the neoclassical palaces to the modern skyscrapers, the town reflected its turbulent history and its resurrection. The pewter evening sky was casting long shadows on the town’s façade. It was time to head back to the hotel. She realised she had not called Stephen for a while. A lot had happened since she had boarded the ship, and she had repeatedly missed calling him, sometimes by mistake, but mostly because of her emotionally worn-out state. Now that she was alone, she concluded it was the best time to call him.

Stephen, like always, sounded anxious. It was nearly a week since she had boarded that ship, and they were looking forward to meeting her in Warsaw. When Sanchez announced her presence in the old town, at first Stephen was thrilled, but then signs of concern dwarfed his excitement. He had been near the hotel all the while, with his eyes on the hotel entrance. He was unsure how Sanchez could have gone past their vigilant watch. He was baffled and concerned, and promised himself to be extra prudent from then on.

Sanchez agreed to meet them at a nearby coffee shop and raised her hand to stop an approaching taxi.

The wait was not long. The fact that his sister was walking without any visible limp made Stephen beam. While they wanted to spend the evening with her, Sanchez refused. She had been away for a while, and she didn’t want Benedikt to come looking for her. After the usual prattle and reassurances about her safety, she came straight to the point.

“I have enough evidence to pull the racket down. I have it all in my private online account. Before I hand it over to you, I need one assurance.” Sanchez looked squarely at Maxwell, waiting for his response.

“What is it?”

“You won’t do anything until I advise you to do so. I don’t want to jeopardise finding my father.”

“Have you found any leads?” Stephen asked.

“Nothing concrete, but I am on it.” Her unwillingness to answer the question was visible in her shaky voice.

“Are you hiding something from us?” This time it was Maxwell who put forward the question, looking edgy and uncomfortable.

“That isn’t the answer I am looking for. Do I have your word?” The irritation in her voice gave a clear sign of how much she detested the probing.

“You have my word. I will do nothing against your wishes. If you can share with me what is going on, I may offer some help,” Maxwell responded in a somewhat troubled tone.

“Here are the account details. Please don’t forget the agreement we have. I want to bring TransPacific down, but not at the cost of my father.” She kept the conversation short.

Maxwell looked uneasy, but he was constrained. They parted ways. He knew something was brewing in Sanchez’s mind, and this time he was determined to deal with the situation like a professional, ditching his emotions.

**Chapter 72**

***8:00 AM 29th April 2014, Warsaw, Poland***

It was pouring in Warsaw. The relentless rain and the gloomy weather were not helping Benedikt to drive away his restlessness. A sense of dejection engulfed him, and no matter how much he tried escaping, he failed. He could sense Sanchez drifting away from him but was unsure how to stop it. The stay at Warsaw had been busy.  He couldn’t find time to have any real conversation with her. Most of the time he was either in business meetings or on site visits. And in the little time he had, she always appeared aloof, lost in her own world, impeding any dialogue between them. After a lot of deliberation, he convinced his hesitant mind to confront her. This time he was determined to express his feelings. He wanted the yearnings, which had been subdued in the past for reasons beyond his control, to flow uninterrupted. The trip to Belarus was a short journey by plane, and he wanted to use the travel time to approach her. He had a plan, and with renewed hope he waited for the morning to arrive.

Benedikt was awake before the break of dawn.  A morning jog and a long shower worked towards calming his anxieties. Sanchez was waiting for him in the breakfast area. She couldn’t take her eyes off him. He was wearing faded denim trousers, an off-white shirt, and ultramarine slip-ons. His back-brushed long hair and the five o’clock shadow were driving her insane. It was a quiet breakfast, and soon they headed to the airport.

The plane took off without much ado. They were halfway through the journey when Benedikt mustered some courage and handed her a paper. Before Sanchez could react, he turned and moved to the back of the plane, leaving her all by herself.

Sanchez opened the folded paper and read the content with interest.

“You know it’s true that I never wanted to disappoint you, but despite my sincere efforts, I failed miserably.

“Imagine a day when you and I are together, and we enjoy the moment of our camaraderie. There will be no baggage, no commitment to honour and no strings that will hold us. It will be beautiful, two people enjoying each other’s company, intellect and the things that make the friendship real.

“I want to act on it today. For there is no tomorrow, and life is so unpredictable.  I want to take you to a faraway place where we can sit holding each other’s hands, treasuring the unspoken words.

“Everyone can find a reason or two to reject this viewpoint, or you may ask me why now, when it isn’t the right time. But I will have only one reason, enough for me to disregard all rationale, and the reason is – I love you. This one truth is strong enough to fight any amount of reasoning and questioning.

“I want to meet you now beyond the boundaries of right and wrong, at a place where there are no obligations, no constraints, and nothing to justify, where you can be you, speaking your mind freely without inhibition or any concern for repercussions.

“Believe in my words. Let us give each other our hands and let destiny lead us where it wants to take us. I assure you it will be the best thing you will ever experience. It will not be transient but for eternity, which we will keep in our own world away from anyone’s prejudice or judgment.”

As Sanchez read the message, her heart wrenched for him and her emotions overwhelmed her. A few deep breaths helped to calm her, and once she had her emotions under control, she approached the rear of the plane. Benedikt was sitting on a couch, randomly flipping through a magazine. As he sensed her presence, he cast a casual but attentive glance at her. There were anticipation, curiosity, and love in his blue eyes. He draped his surging heartbeats with a faint smile and waited for Sanchez to speak.

She knew it would be hard, but she was determined to speak the unwelcome words. And with that fortitude, she looked straight into his eyes and said in a soft, yet firm tone, “I cannot fall in love with a person who is responsible for my father’s disappearance.”

The words pierced Benedikt’s heart like a lightning bolt. He lost his voice for a while before he managed to say, “Do you really believe that I am responsible for your father’s disappearance?”

“You, your organisation, everyone is responsible,” she blurted and stormed away.

As she walked back, tears rolled down her cheeks. She knew she had torn him to pieces, but she refused to commit to anyone until she found out the real reason behind her father’s disappearance. Till then this was the only way to keep Benedikt at bay, or maybe this was her pretentious belief.

**Chapter 73**

***7:30 PM 29th April 2014, Vitebsk, Belarus***

Benedikt found Sanchez more engaged in Vitebsk. The gloominess that had stayed with her for the past few days had disappeared. Though she was far from being cheerful, it was enough for Benedikt to amicably coexist. She had requested him to take her to all the sites that he was planning to visit.

On the day of their arrival at Vitebsk, Benedikt was required to visit the warehouse. He insisted that Sanchez rest at the hotel but surrendered to her persistent demand.

They drove for an hour before turning onto a dirt road, which led them to the warehouse. By the time they arrived, the place was enclosed in darkness. Though summer was only a few weeks away, it was cold enough to send a shiver through her spine. The calmness in the surroundings gave no hint of the bustling environment inside the warehouse.

Benedikt got busy with the facilities head, and it gave Sanchez the perfect time to trace Yuri’s whereabouts. She spotted a person who was overseeing the unloading of cargo. She approached him with a broad smile and enquired, “Can you spare a minute for me?”

The person looked at her with a confused expression and moved away without giving any response. It took some time for Sanchez to realise that she had a language issue. In Belarus, they either spoke Belarusian or Russian, and she had no clue about either.

“Do you know Yuri? Yuri,” she said, stressing the name.  It made no difference. The man smiled and walked away without answering.

For the next couple of hours she tried hard to get people’s attention, but no one understood her words. Soon it was time to head back to the hotel. She left the warehouse, saddened and anxious.

**Chapter 74**

***11:00 PM 29th April 2014, Vitebsk, Belarus***

The Vitebsk stay was coming to a close, and Sanchez knew tomorrow might be her only chance to find Yuri. The night was rough. She spent hours on the Internet learning the basics of the Russian language, enough words to help her converse with the workers.

They left for the warehouse around mid-morning. Benedikt had many questions for her but couldn’t muster enough courage to speak his mind. During their entire commute, she was either staring aimlessly into space or occasionally going through the notebook she was carrying.

In the daylight, she could clearly see the warehouse from a distance. The lone building stood aloof in the middle of the vast open field. Soon they were back inside the warehouse. Nothing had changed since they had left. The place was still crowded with people. She waited for Benedikt to get busy, and once she was convinced, she went ahead with her plan. She looked around and saw an elderly man busy taking stock of some packages. She opened her notebook and, reading slowly in Russian from the pad, enquired whether he knew anything about Yuri. The reaction was no different from what she had experienced before. He ignored her. She tried putting the same question to a few other people but got no answers. She was not making any progress, and she decided to change her approach.
