She approached the old man again. This time she mentioned that she had an important message for Yuri, and it was from his old friend Finch, from the ship where he used to work before. He simply walked away, ignoring everything she said. She tried it with a few other people. She either got a cold stare or no response. She was unsure whether it was because of her inability to speak their language, the unfriendliness of the locals, or fear of some unknown force that was inhibiting them from speaking. Frustrated, she decided to exercise her last resort, Benedikt. She wanted to avoid taking his help for many reasons, the prime one being her inability to trust him. But her time and options were both running out, and he was the only person left who could help. She looked around and spotted him a few yards away, busy having an intense conversation with the locals. She waited for their discussion to end and took the time to reassess her options. Her emotions were betraying her, as she pondered how she was so close yet so far from Yuri. How she wished she did not have to approach Benedikt for help, and how she desired some miracle to happen in her favour.

She was brought out of her brooding by a gentle tap on her shoulder. She turned around and found a fragile-looking frame standing right in front of her. He handed her a piece of paper and went away. Sanchez hurriedly opened the folded chit and found something written in Russian. Though she couldn’t read it, she knew it was an address. She was thrilled. She had got hold of something concrete when she was least expecting it.

It was noon, and time to break for lunch. Sanchez had already scripted a plan to get out of the place. As she saw Benedikt approaching her, she leaned on the desk with her head hidden in her folded arms.

“Are you all right?” Benedikt enquired.

She raised her head with great difficulty. Her face was exuding pain, and she said in a faint voice, “I am not. Can I take the car back to the hotel?”

“What happened? Is everything ok with you? We will leave right away,” Benedikt blurted.

“Nothing serious. I am a little dizzy. You don’t have to come along. All I need is some rest.” She knew Benedikt would insist on coming, and she tried hard to stop him, but failed.

On their way back to the hotel, Benedikt insisted that she visit a doctor, but she refused and ended the discussion by agreeing to go if her condition deteriorated further. She rushed to her room on the pretext of taking some rest and called for room service.

Soon a young man appeared at her door. She called him inside, handed him a fifty-euro note and enquired about the address. The boy gave a wide grin and happily put the money in his pocket. He mentioned that the place was about thirty minutes away. She handed him some more money and instructed him to arrange a cab for her. She asked him to get the cab to the coffee shop next to the hotel in twenty minutes. He was a happy man. What he earned in a good week, he had earned in a day. He was more than willing to help and rushed to get the taxi arranged.

It was a long fifteen-minute wait before she stepped out of her room. She covered her face with a scarf and took the stairs. She waited by the stairway and peeked out to confirm no one she knew was around. Once convinced, she walked discreetly out of the hotel. Soon she was on her way to Yuri’s place.

**Chapter 75**

***2:00 PM 30th April 2014, Vitebsk, Belarus***

There were houses sparsely spread out in no defined order. All the homes were built in a similar pattern, but each one still stood out because of the liberal use of bright colours. Yuri’s dwelling was draped in turquoise blue. She kept staring at the house for a while before approaching it with wary steps. From a distance, the house appeared deserted with no sign of a soul. She dreaded the thought of not finding him, and with trembling hands she tapped on the wooden door. After a few knocks, a six-foot, frail-looking figure opened the door. She opened her notebook and read in Russian, enquiring if he was Yuri.

Though he looked sceptical, he reluctantly confirmed. Sanchez’s hope took a leap. She handed him the letter from Finch and patiently waited for his reaction. He read the letter and instantly made space to let her come inside. He locked the door firmly, and to her surprise, he spoke in fine English. “He surely had picked up his English from Dad,” she thought.

“Are you Sanchez? He used to talk about you all the time.” His old eyes had a spark which was hard to ignore.

“Do you know where he is?” There was anxiety, fear and a hint of hope in those words.

“How did you find me?”

Either he was avoiding the question, or he was sincerely curious.

“I will give you all the details, but first can you please tell me where my father is?”

He gave a wry smile and continued, “They took him away. And...” He hesitated.

“And what? Please tell me.”

“He is in Russia. I haven’t seen him for ages. They have confined him, and I believe the only reason he might be alive is because of the evidence he has against them.”

He took a pause and continued, “The evidence is his lifeline.”

“Why did you say might be?” The anxiety was evident in her shaky tone.

He looked into her eyes and stated, “Because it still baffles me why they would keep him alive. Besides, he was taken to a place where there are no rules.”

“Would you know where in Russia he is?”

“I have heard these goons have a settlement in Smolensk. I cannot confirm for certain, but Smolensk is your best bet. That place is well guarded, and the people are notorious. I wish I could have helped you, but unfortunately, I don’t know anyone there.”

She thanked him and wandered aimlessly back to her waiting taxi. The emotions were overwhelming her. She finally had some concrete evidence of her dad’s existence, something she had waited a long time for. She had no clue what to do next, but she was certain she would soon find a way to be in Smolensk.

**Chapter 76**

***3:30 PM 30th April 2014, Vitebsk, Belarus***

She got out of the cab a block away from the hotel, solely to avoid anyone sighting her.  It was a cold afternoon. The unruly wind was spiraling down with utmost ruthlessness, and a shiver was surging through her spine. The street was sparsely populated, and a dense layer of mist was forging a stark gloominess that was hard to subdue. After a few minutes of brutal walking, she entered the hotel lobby with cautious steps. She looked around and was relieved to find no sign of Benedikt or his accomplices. With supreme urgency, she hurried down the hallway towards the elevators. She was about to get into the elevator when Benedikt’s voice startled her. She turned around and found him standing right behind her. Her heart sank, and her feet went numb. For a moment, she felt as if she would crumble and fall. Despite the coldness, she sensed a trickle of sweat unceremoniously appearing on her temple. How she wished what she was witnessing was nothing but a bad dream.

She mustered some courage and raised her head to get a glimpse of him. His eyes were hollow, set firmly in his pale, gloomy face. She had seen him in different shades before, but not the one he wore today. He appeared like a dethroned king, lost and dismayed, aloof and confused. An aura of betrayal reflected from every shade of his dejected presence.

Before she could react in any meaningful way, his voice echoed, “It doesn’t appear you are sick anymore.” The shade of sarcasm was evident in that gruff tone.

She forced a smile and claimed, “I felt suffocated in the room, so I went for a walk.”

“Good, that was a long stroll to Noŭka.”

Sanchez was exposed. The entire charade she had put on so far folded like a deck of cards. She kept quiet, surrendering to the situation.

“We need to talk. Can you please follow me to my room?” His tone, though gentle, was exuding annoyance.

She trailed him into the elevator with no sign of resistance. They settled in the living room. The void of words and the unusual quietness made the place appear ghost-like.

Benedikt finally broke the silence and in an exhausted tone asked, “Why can’t you trust me?”

“I didn’t trust you? Despite all the odds, I believed in you. I came on this trip against my brother’s wishes. I respected you, I admired you, and all this while I listened to my heart, ignoring everything my mind ever told me otherwise.”

Sanchez was a shade below screaming. She didn’t know how, but she found the voice to defend herself, tired of pretending that everything was fine between them.

“The thread that brought us together was my dad. No matter how much I try, I can’t forget the reaction you had when you saw his picture. I know for certain that all this while you knew everything, but you pretended otherwise. Let me ask you – why should I trust you?”

From being meek not too long ago, she had transformed into a raging tigress. She was breathing heavily, and her cheeks were burning red with anger.

Benedikt looked squarely at her, aghast. He had never expected her to retaliate with such bluntness. “I admit I hid a few facts, but that was because I didn’t want to lose you. There is nothing that I know that can help trace your father.” His voice was timid and his demeanour edgy.

He hesitantly looked at Sanchez to observe her reaction and quickly turned away to escape her piercing gaze.

Unsure of what to do, he continued, “I was there on the day your father was captured, but trust me, I had nothing to do with his disappearance. That day I wasn’t even in charge of the operations. I was on a regular business trip.”

The intensity with which Sanchez was looking at him made him restless and flustered. He gulped some water to get some respite. This time she was determined to know the truth, and he knew it.

Benedikt continued, “That day, when you showed me your father’s picture, my guilt overwhelmed me.”

“What was your guilt?” Sanchez snapped.

“I... I told the Russians to get rid of him, but trust me, nothing untoward happened. They wanted to get hold of all the evidence your father had collected and so decided to keep him. I swear to God I know nothing beyond this. I have no idea where they have taken him or what they have done with him.”

He paused to watch her expression, exhausted from the monologue. She was unusually quiet. She kept staring at the vase on the table without blinking, still and unobtrusive. All Benedikt wanted was to conclude his part soon and bring an end to his prolonged misery.

“I didn’t hide facts to keep you away from your father. I didn’t want you to go away.”

She couldn’t take it anymore. She felt suffocated in his presence and decided to leave.

“I need some time alone.” And she disappeared, leaving Benedikt in a helpless, stifled state.

**Chapter 77**

***10:00 PM 30th April 2014, Vitebsk, Belarus***

The night was long and restless. While Benedikt’s hopes had crashed, Sanchez was in a continuous battle with her mind over his behaviour. She was hurt, but unable to find any reasons to support it. Her mind was clouded with agitation, frustration and unresolved queries. Part of her wanted to capitulate, but there was something in her that was looking for vengeance. The conundrum kept her awake the entire night, and before she realised it, it was morning. She decided to go for a run. “Maybe the morning freshness could help me get a clearer vision,” she concluded.

The morning coldness opened all her senses. There was a newfound zeal. She was like a free bird, and at that moment all her nightlong apprehensions vanished.   This time she had no competing thoughts, and no doubts holding her back. Everything appeared clear like the early morning brightness.

After a mile-long run, she looped back to the hotel and walked straight to his room. On the third knock he responded, and when he saw her through the peephole, he was startled. He opened the door in haste. The unkempt hair, the dishevelled look, and his confused expression made him appear dangerously attractive. After some struggle, she found her lost voice. “I hope I didn’t catch you at a bad time. Do you want me to come back?”

“No, you are fine. Have a seat. I will be with you in a minute.” And he vanished behind the closed door. He was back in a jiffy. He was wearing a crumpled shirt, and his hair was partly combed. Hastiness was written all over his effort. But despite all the madness, she could see the sparkle of eagerness in his wide blue eyes. She tried hard to take her eyes off his rugged looks but failed.

Frustrated, she detested her sordid pretence and blurted, “I am tired of pretending to be a different person with you. I want to trust you one last time.”

Benedikt was at a loss for words. He broke the awkward silence. “What you told me now means the world to me. You won’t be disappointed.” There was elation, an unprecedented joy in his tone.

Sanchez continued, “I have a fair idea where my father is and I want your help.”

“Where did you find him? Who told you? Is this real? Are you sure?” His barrage of questions seemed never-ending.

His inquisitiveness and childlike enthusiasm made Sanchez smile. And at that moment, she knew she hadn’t made a wrong decision.

“I will tell you everything, but I am famished. Can we order breakfast first?” she asked, melting away the discomfort they had between them for the past several days.

**Chapter 78**

***10:00 AM 15th May 2014, Smolensk, Russia***

The name Smolensk was not alien to Benedikt, for it had appeared a few times in his past business conversations. The setup at Smolensk was not under the control of TransPacific, and no occasion had presented itself in the past that could have taken him there. The place was the East European operations centre for their Russian counterpart and was beyond his control. It was strategically located near the border of Belarus, making it easy for the Russians to operate their dealings within Europe. It made perfect sense. Keeping Sanchez’s father in Russia would keep TransPacific out of any unwanted trouble.

His plan to visit Smolensk came as a surprise to his seniors. He presented a detailed plan for expanding business with the Russians, but despite a well-thought-out strategy, he got firm pushback.  In the past, he would have bulldozed his way in, but this time the situation required him to exhibit a different demeanour. He took care not to look too persuasive, for he never wanted anyone to cast any doubt on him. And because of his overcautious approach, it took him a good two weeks to convince his higher-ups.

The plan was all set. The Russians were briefed, and they were expecting him at their Smolensk location.  At first, Benedikt wanted to go alone, but Sanchez’s inexorable insistence dissolved all his reasoning.  He enjoined her not to display any emotions if she happened to see her father, to the point of annoyance. He instructed Sanchez to have Stephen wait at the border. It was a fallback plan. In case the Russians came after him, he could let Daniel and Sanchez escape with Stephen. The plan was crafted with the hope that he would succeed in helping Daniel escape.

It was a cold spring morning. The drive to Smolensk was a little over two hours. The place was located a few miles away from the highway in a deserted spot. The tall trees and the wilds in the surroundings concealed what looked like a long-standing bastion. They were stopped at the entrance and were let in after their car was thoroughly checked by an armed guard. Inside, the place was nothing charming. There was one large building, which looked like a monolithic dome. A person named Anatoly received them at the driveway. Anatoly was the manager of the Smolensk facility. He was a stout, well-built man and appeared to be in his late forties. The wide grin on his face looked permanent and his blue eyes had a subtle shadow of shrewdness.

They were taken to a large office and were joined by two more of his accomplices. All the discussions were in Russian and Sanchez could hardly contribute. Though she kept a pleasant appearance, there was a shadow of uncertainty in her eyes, which was hard to conceal. She knew they had arrived without any plan and soon had to figure one out, and unfortunately, time was not their best friend. The place looked uninhabited. Besides the guards, the valet, a few helpers, and the three officers, there was hardly anyone around.

A quick tap on her hand took her out of her brooding. Benedikt tilted his head so that he could be close to her ear and whispered, “They are going to show me the place. I want you to wait here.” He wore a phony smile in disguise.

With a reluctant heart, she forced a smile confirming her agreement, and he left. It was an intolerable wait. She did everything she could to divert her attention. She walked around, played with her phone, scribbled on paper, but nothing helped. After about thirty minutes, Benedikt returned, and it was time to leave.

Sanchez’s curiosity had gone from bad to worse. She tried pressing Benedikt in many ways, but each time he answered in monosyllables that he would tell her later. The valet brought the car to the driveway. They bid their host goodbye and soon they were out of the bastion on their way to Belarus.

Before she could open her barrage of questions, Benedikt said in a firm tone, “I saw your father and I have a plan for his escape.”

**Chapter 79**

***3:00 PM 15th May 2014, Vitebsk, Belarus***

Sanchez’s father was not in any confinement. During his conversation with Anatoly, Benedikt had mentioned how law enforcement in Europe was becoming increasingly draconian and how the forces were getting more united amongst the countries. He emphasised the likelihood of informers blending with their tribe. And on that pretext, he desired to use Smolensk as a makeshift hideout to interrogate some of the suspects. Anatoly was happy to accommodate his request. He mentioned a person they had got hold of a while back and still held in their internment.

Sanchez was overwhelmed. Her emotions had no boundaries. The fact that her father was alive dissolved the trepidations she had garnered over the years. How she wished she could get a glimpse of him, talk to him and tell him how much she missed him. She held on to her emotions with absolute resolve and succeeded in not breaking down.

Benedikt saw her struggling to hold on to her composure, and he swiftly changed the topic. “Do you want to know what my plan is?”

She was still looking away from him and managed only a nod, dreading she would choke if she chose to talk.

He explained the plan to her. Sanchez was unsure how it would turn out but remained inscrutable, for she had nothing better to offer. It was going to be a long night and she hoped for a better tomorrow.

**Chapter 80**

***10:00 AM 16th May 2014, Smolensk, Russia***

The next day, Benedikt arrived at Smolensk early in the morning.  He had tried every possible way to convince Sanchez not to come, but his efforts were brushed aside. Anatoly was waiting in the lobby to receive them. There was no change in his stance. He exuded the same warmth as he had displayed the day before. After exchanging pleasantries, Benedikt initiated the conversation. “We are excited about the possible partnership, and we are eager to take this forward. However, we need to resolve a trivial matter.”

Benedikt exercised extreme caution in choosing his words and took a brief pause to watch Anatoly’s reaction.

“What is your concern?” asked Anatoly.

“Nothing significant. It is about Daniel.” Benedikt paused, giving Anatoly ample time to comprehend what he was about to divulge.

“What about him?” Anatoly snapped. He didn’t seem pleased.  The forced smile disappeared for a split second, but soon the gleam was back on his countenance.

“While giving the report to my seniors, I mentioned Daniel. It seemed there was some miscommunication between us. According to Mr. Slater, Daniel should be long gone. They see him as a threat. We need to resolve this before we can go any further on our business expansion plans.”

This time his grin faded completely, and he assumed a more puzzled look. After a long pause, he responded, “He has been confined here for a few years now. He is not a danger to anyone. Why would your seniors be worried about him?”

Benedikt had never expected such strong support for Daniel. Though he was unsure about the outcome, he still went ahead with his plan. “I believe your words, but as you must appreciate, I have to answer to my seniors. Here is a suggestion I have. Why don’t I take him along, on the pretext of releasing him? My man will secretly follow him to see where he goes and what he does. If in the next month he doesn’t do anything alarming, the man is yours. You can keep him or let him go, your choice.”

Anatoly looked uncomfortable. After some brooding, he concluded, “I can’t do this without my seniors’ approval. I need to talk to them once.”

This was what Benedikt had wanted to avoid. If Anatoly’s executives chose to discuss this with Benedikt’s seniors, the charade he had put up so far would fall apart. Besides, he would need to do a lot of explaining, but he had no option but to agree.

“By all means.” He smiled to appear genuine.

Anatoly excused himself and went out to make a few calls. He returned after fifteen minutes, wearing his signature broad grin. “You are a good business partner and we have a promising future together. My seniors have agreed. You can come tomorrow morning and take him along.”

Benedikt couldn’t understand why he had been asked to come the next day. “I would prefer to take him today. I hope it is not an imperious request.” He forced a smile to hide his bafflement.

“I would have loved to, but Mr. Kozlov is not here until late in the evening. He wants to meet Daniel once before you take him away.” His smile appeared more like Russian Mores, and his jauntiness irritated Benedikt.

Benedikt concealed his frustration and, as in any other professional dealing, concluded, “Sure, I will see you tomorrow.”

There was something in Anatoly’s voice that appeared unconvincing. Perplexed, Benedikt drove the car, battling with his competing thoughts.

It took a shade short of screaming for Sanchez to pull him out of his deep thoughts. He looked at her, still trying to comprehend the reason behind her escalated tone.

“Will you tell me what transpired inside?” Hysteria consumed her being as she blurted the words.

He was unsure how to respond. He was certain the Russians were using a ploy.

“On the surface, it looks exciting. And I want to believe it is real. But it is not.” He was gazing at the lone road and said the words without blinking.

Sanchez couldn’t keep up with the erratic behaviour Benedikt was demonstrating. In a rage, she blurted, “For Christ’s sake, answer me. I can’t put up with your runic spell anymore.” Her hands covered her face and her breath was uneven.

Benedikt realised his rumination was putting her into a miserable state. He looked at her and continued, “I apologise. I was too busy deciphering Anatoly’s behaviour. As per the plan, I asked him to let your father come with us. I was not surprised when he said he wanted to check with his seniors. What confused me was the follow-up response.”

The conversation with Anatoly was in Russian, a language Sanchez had no command over. Her palpitations faded away. She appeared more rational as she wore an inquisitive look.

After a short pause, he continued, “He asked me to come tomorrow to get him. Why not today? Why is it important for Kozlov to meet him?”

“Are you saying they have agreed?” Sanchez asked.

“You do not understand. It can’t be that easy. Don’t you see the ruse?” The paranoia in his voice was hard to conceal.

“Maybe you are doubting unnecessarily. He may have meant what he said.” There was hope, a sense of belief in her voice.

For a moment, the glee on her face convinced him to go by her intuition, but soon he rejected the thought. He didn’t want to subscribe to a false design, and with some reluctance he continued, “I want to believe you, but I know them well. I am sure there is something behind this arrangement. Something he was hiding behind that grin.”

“So, what are you suggesting? Are you not going to come back tomorrow?” Her face was like a shroud.

Benedikt took his gaze away from the road and looked at her with intense eyes. “I will certainly come back. All I want is to be cautious. The last thing I want is to put a damper on your years of strenuous efforts.”

The anxiety receded from her face and she chose not to speak for the rest of the journey.

**Chapter 81**

***12:00 PM 16th May 2014, Liozna, Belarus***

Liozna was a small town near the border of Belarus and Russia. Stephen was waiting with anxious eyes to seize the moment. He wasn’t expecting Sanchez until half-past one, yet his perturbations were beyond limits.

“Stephen must be waiting for us. Do you want to call him? Maybe you can ask him to leave?” Benedikt tried striking up a conversation after a momentary lull.

Sanchez was lost in an unprecedented gloom. She didn’t respond but acted on the request. It didn’t take too long for Stephen to comprehend the situation and his face assumed shades of burned char.  The dwindling hope of meeting his father came crashing down. Maxwell was standing next to him. He sensed something wasn’t right.

“Is Sanchez fine?” Maxwell queried with a trembling voice.

With a grim expression, Stephen responded, “They couldn’t get Dad out. We have to leave this place.”

Maxwell had got himself invited in an unceremonious way. Ever since Sanchez had decided to board the ship, he had kept Stephen’s company.  When he learned about Benedikt’s plan to free Daniel from the clutches of the Russian mafia, he was startled. Though he was sceptical of the proceedings, he refrained from expressing his views. He wanted to be at Liozna to witness it first-hand. Stephen was too naive to understand his intention and gave in to his persuasive demand. Maxwell had all the evidence against Benedikt, and to ensure he got him this time, he planned his arrest.  With utmost discretion, he deployed his team, all hiding in the tall bushes by the side of the road. But his ulterior plan was foiled by the call from Sanchez.

“We should still wait for her.” Maxwell didn’t sound convincing as he hastily put his thought forward.

Stephen gave him a confused look. “But Sanchez asked us to leave. It is not happening today.”

“I can’t trust Benedikt. What if she is in danger? What if she needs help but cannot speak out?” Maxwell tried to put some reasoning behind his frail words.

Stephen didn’t put up any further resistance and passively submitted to his demand. Both waited, counting every passing minute. Time had halted for them.

**Chapter 82**

***10:15 AM 16th May 2014, Smolensk, Russia***

Something didn’t appear right. Though Benedikt’s countenance reflected calmness, Anatoly knew it was a disguise.  He made sure not to display even a trickle of doubt on his visage and excused himself from the room. He headed straight to Kozlov’s office. Kozlov was his boss and head of the Smolensk operations.

Kozlov was on a call when Anatoly entered. He ignored his presence and continued with his call.  The room’s surreal setup appeared intimidating, but it didn’t bother Anatoly. He had been in this room many times before and was used to the decor and the protocol. When he was finally done, Kozlov gazed towards Anatoly. It was an indication to start the conversation. Kozlov’s tapered green eyes became narrower as Anatoly provided a detailed narration about his conversation with Benedikt.

“I sense some foul play. He is up to something. I need to call Evan,” Kozlov declared, and without much ado he went ahead to make the call.

He called a few times but there was no response.

“Ask him to come tomorrow. Come up with some excuse. Make sure you appear genuine. I need to talk to TransPacific on this. Daniel is a closed chapter and I have no intention to revisit his case.” Kozlov had a distinct way of communicating. When he spoke, others held back their opinions.

Anatoly left the room. He had clear direction from his senior, and that was what mattered.

**Chapter 83**

***12:30 PM 16th May 2014, Liozna, Belarus***

The road was mostly deserted except for the occasional passing vehicle. This time Maxwell was determined to arrest Benedikt. He had made all the arrangements to capture him provided he showed up.  It was unlike Benedikt to act in such an imprudent manner. The Benedikt he knew was fierce, vicious and ruthless. He doubted he would come, but he wanted to believe otherwise.

While he was busy debating with his competing thoughts, a blurry image of a car appeared not too far away from where he was hiding. It was a beamer, the type of car Stephen had mentioned Benedikt would be driving. All his senses came to life as he observed the car with vigilant eyes. The noon sun formed a mirage on the long road, and the car appeared as if it was floating on a body of water. Maxwell stayed behind the bushes on the side of the pavement, observant and ready to surge.

Stephen, unaware of his companion’s ulterior motives, waved at the car, directing it to stop. The car came to a halt and a puzzled-looking Sanchez rolled down the window.

“Didn’t I ask you to leave?” Her voice reflected more surprise than frustration.

“We want to ensure you are safe,” Stephen blabbered without much conviction.

“Who else is with you?” This time it was Benedikt. Stephen’s uneasiness made him curious. Besides, he had said “we” instead of “I.” Benedikt’s eyes were searching the surroundings as he waited for Stephen to respond.

Before Stephen could speak, the sound of loading guns broke the tranquility of the deserted setting. Six armed men approached them, three from each side of the car and Maxwell from the front. Maxwell wore a cunning smirk as if he had won the battle.

Sanchez was trapped in a quagmire, unsure how to react. On one side, she had her brother, whom she trusted with her life, betraying her belief, and on the other, she had Benedikt, who despite all odds had gone out of his way to help her. All she could comprehend at that moment was Benedikt being in danger. Everything was happening too fast. She gave Stephen a hard stare. Her eyes reflected signs of agony and frustration. Time was running out and she had to do something quickly if she was to save Benedikt. And then a thought crossed her mind. She turned towards Benedikt. But before she could say a word, his pensive look froze her. A faint smile graced his pale lips and a sense of betrayal loomed over his visage. He said softly, “I will always love you, no matter what happens.”

She took her pistol out of her bag, the one Benedikt had given her before leaving for Smolensk, and whispered with urgency, “Make me your hostage.”

Before he could react, Sanchez said again, this time with agitation, “You don’t have time. Can you hurry up?”

A confused Benedikt swung his arms around her neck. Draped in some stupor, he pointed the gun at her temple and screamed, “Put your guns down, or else I will shoot her.”

Maxwell was flabbergasted. He couldn’t believe the tables had turned against him. Sanchez was too precious for him to do anything stupid. He screamed, “You are making a mistake. Put your gun down, and I promise I will request the authorities to show leniency.”

“You don’t get it, do you? Another step and I will shoot her.” Benedikt’s tone was dead and firm.

Maxwell knew he meant business. As he was contemplating, the hysterical screaming of Sanchez shook his being. “Do as he says. His men had shot me once and I have no doubt he will shoot me again.”

“Alright. I will do as you say.” An aghast Maxwell instructed his men to step back.

“Drop your guns, turn back and walk a hundred steps away,” Benedikt demanded.

Maxwell ordered his men to follow the instructions.

“Stephen, get all the guns and put them in the car trunk,” commanded Benedikt. A startled and ashamed Stephen followed the orders.

Benedikt reversed the car with urgency. The tyres made a screeching sound as he drove with full force in the direction he had come from. He knew once he was in Russia, Maxwell could do no harm.

Maxwell looked at the vanishing car with horror, but there was nothing he could do to change things. He was worried, perspiring and perplexed. All he had was a broken heart, smeared thoughts, and a hope that she would be safe.

**Chapter 84**

***1:00 PM 16th May 2014, Smolensk, Russia***

Benedikt’s car was away from the clutches of Maxwell, far into the Russian side of the border. Though the immediate perilous situation was behind him, his plan was in complete shambles. But despite all the hullabaloo he went through, Benedikt appeared ecstatic. The ruse Sanchez had played on Maxwell was glued to his vision and he still couldn’t get enough of it. He wanted to say so many things, but words defied him.

“Thank you for your help.” In the end, those were the only words he could manage.

“Why do you love me so much? All I have given you is pain and trouble.” She gently squeezed his hand and continued, “You risked your life, your job, just for me. And all I have offered you is despair.” Her voice was deep and had a distinct shade of empathy.

Benedikt was shocked. He had never witnessed Sanchez opening up before.  His mind was battling hard to find the right syllables when his phone created a sudden dissonance. He glanced at the phone with disdain, but the name flashing on the screen needed his attention. He asked Sanchez to be quiet and answered the phone with utmost disinterest.

“You are creating more problems than answers. Why did you volunteer to take Daniel out of Smolensk? Worse, you took my name? Can you explain what is going on?”  The voice on the other end reflected annoyance.

At other times Benedikt would have fought back with equal vigour. But today was different. He parked the car on the side of the road and in a composed tone responded, “It was impromptu. I was there when Daniel was captured, and I know how much of a risk he is to our business.”

A harsh tone interrupted him, “It happened a long time ago. Since we trusted our partners to take care of the matter, it makes no sense to intervene again. You have an important assignment to finish and you should focus on that instead of nosing around in trivial matters.”

This time the voice was further elevated, but that didn’t deter Benedikt from putting forward his point, “If we had killed him at that time, we wouldn’t be having this discussion now. We don’t know what secrets he knows, and what evidence he may have hidden somewhere. We are sitting on a ticking time bomb. One day it will explode and then it will be too late.”

The person on the other side of the phone tried to intervene, but this time Benedikt didn’t let him speak. “I have a plan. On the pretext of letting him go, I will follow him. If he turns rogue I will get him killed. If not, I will do as you suggest. Why is it so difficult for you to understand that the exposure we have with Daniel cannot be taken lightly?”

It made sense to the person on the other side of the phone. In a sceptical tone he continued, “You have created a lot of unrest. Make sure you handle this one with perfection.” The line went silent.

Sanchez heard the entire conversation. Before Benedikt could put forward any rational argument, she said, “You don’t have to explain. I know why you said what you said.”  She wore a pensive smile and, with a dejected look, took her gaze away from him.

**Chapter 85**

***8:00 AM 17th May 2014, Smolensk, Russia***

It was a bright morning. The breeze was gentle, and the mercury was at its best. This time Benedikt decided to go alone. The way things were turning, he was unsure what to expect. Unlike last time, Sanchez was reasonable and understood the rationale behind it. She was waiting for him a few miles away from the bastion.

Anatoly was at his deceitful best. He met Benedikt with the same verve as the day before. Since Benedikt’s departure a lot had happened. Evan had returned Kozlov’s call. He was appalled to learn about Benedikt’s demand. Kozlov expressed his discomfort and said he had no intention of letting Daniel go.

After a few hours, Evan called him back and tried convincing him to let Benedikt take Daniel with him. He explained the reasoning behind their demand and assured him that Benedikt would be closely monitored and, if found guilty, would not be spared. Kozlov, though not convinced, still agreed, for he couldn’t have said no to TransPacific. After the conversation, he called Anatoly and asked his men to discreetly follow Benedikt. He wanted a second pair of eyes from his clan to ensure there was nothing dubious planned.

Daniel was brought to the room.  He looked frail like before and wore a confused look. And to Benedikt’s disbelief, he left the place with Daniel unscathed.  He had his share of doubts, for it went too smoothly to appear real. He was watchful, and for now all that mattered was Daniel’s safety.

Sanchez’s annoyance with her brother had not receded. She couldn’t believe Stephen would hide such a thing from her. She was so furious she refused to call Stephen to plan for Daniel’s safe passage until Benedikt insisted. He knew that if he escaped from the Russians, he would be followed, and the only way he could ensure Sanchez’s safety was with Maxwell’s help. The plan was the same as before, but with one change. Sanchez would walk across the border along with Daniel. Once he had secured their safety, he would think about his escape plan.

Sanchez spotted the car from a distance. She was numb, as if her feet were frozen to the ground, and despite the strong urge she failed to run towards the car to check her father’s presence.

Benedikt’s car came to a complete halt, and Sanchez saw her father behind the car doors. He looked wearily thin. The clothes he was wearing were larger than his frame. His face was covered with a white beard and his hair appeared dishevelled. His complexion was a shade below pale. One thing that didn’t fail was his smile, which had been glued to her memories all these years since he disappeared. This time she leaped forward with all her might and hugged him tightly. Tears were flowing in abundance for all the right reasons. There were no words. All that was visible were teary eyes and smiling faces.

“Dad, you are free. We will all live together like we used to.” The words found their way out as she spoke in a trembling voice.

Her dad could manage only a few words through his choking voice, “Thank you. I never thought I would ever see you again.”

She got in the car and they started their journey towards Liozna. Benedikt was extremely vigilant.  His prudent eyes spared none. And without any untoward incident, they reached their destination.

“You should go now.” Benedikt was curt and showed no sign of parting remorse as he spoke for the first time since Sanchez got in the car.

She turned a deaf ear to his instruction and instead said to her father, “Dad, Stephen is waiting for you. Maxwell has arranged all the necessary protection. Soon TransPacific will be exposed and we will all be together.”

She took a deep breath and continued, “You need to walk across the border alone. I can’t come now as I need to take care of a few things.”

His countenance displayed a worried look, and he said, “I am sure you have a reason. Don’t make me wait for long this time.”

Time halted and they hugged for a long while. They parted with reluctance and slowly Daniel walked away, disappearing along the road as it bent away from her vision. She leaped forward and from a distance saw the faint image of Stephen. Once convinced of her father’s safety, she dashed back to the car and took the seat next to Benedikt.

Benedikt was frozen as if lightning had struck him. He kept staring at her with astonishment.

She had a wry smile as she said, “After what you did to the Russians, they won’t spare you. I am not worried about TransPacific. Within the next 24 hours it will be busted, but the Russians will be on the loose.”

Benedikt had a puzzled look as Sanchez continued, “I have collected enough evidence against them, and I am sure my father has more. Maxwell will have it all. Let us get out of this place before the Russians come looking for us.”

“Us?” Benedikt was still in a shocked state.

“Yes, us. Provided you are willing to tolerate this abstruse person.” She had that mischievous smile on her face, which he knew well.

Benedikt’s joy had no boundaries. “Yes, and for a lifetime.”

“When you were not around, illusions of you comforted me, and now that you are here, my endearment for you is beyond any comprehension,” Benedikt said without any inhibitions. He was hopeful the tides would turn. The scattered clouds slowly engulfed the sun, forming floating shadows over the vast valley of wilderness. They had a few hurdles to cross, some barriers to dodge and many places to visit before they could repose.
